## 1. Statement, scope, and accepted inputs

The regional assertion proved in this draft is:

For every four-step word h=(u,w,z,v) with nonnegative entries,

    u+w+z+v >= 168/25,       w <= 4/3,                 (1)

there is no original primitive certificate with all twenty nonnegative
finite multipliers, exact flow, and a matrix positive semidefinite on
the full five-gradient space. The first, third, and last steps have no
additional prescribed intervals. No ordering, reversal, or coordinate
permutation is used.

If the four old cut conditions and the old terminal-triangle condition
hold, the proof supplies one explicit static function depending on h,
of dimension at most three, whose primitive energy is

    V_h < -961/4375.                                  (2)

This removes the previous restriction 6w+7v<=18 from the round39 draft.
It covers its actual complement within w<=4/3, and does not merely fix
the first step or perturb the former physical box.

Only accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, and accepted1252b8823bb5a290
are inputs. The first supplies the exact original-array identity; the
second supplies the interpolation orientation. All functions, every
physical update, all directed rows, and all parameter comparisons
needed below are proved jointly here. Neither unverified8d4369993526363e
nor any earlier unverified triangle assertion is a correctness premise.

Audit8d78e0975df8400a and guidance1501c682ec2aff49 have been read. The
original scalar draft43a616da7da451c7 remains defective as printed; its
original file and hash are preserved. No scalar first-increment bound,
global domain reduction, Bernstein table, broad row-redundancy claim,
or whole-box assertion from that package is used here. This document
does not purport to repair or accept that separate scalar package.

## 2. The full original certificate and a realization device

Put s=u+w+z+v and C=1+s. Original finite indices are0,1,2,3,4.
Let Lambda_ij>=0 for all twenty distinct finite pairs and Lambda_ii=0.
Let e_all=(1,1,1,1,1)^T and define

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e_all)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2u,-2w,-2z,-2v,2s)^T,
    Dprim=diag(u(2-u),w(2-w),z(2-z),v(2-v),-s(s+2)),
    M=Dprim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.

The proposed certificate requires L_flow^T e_all=omega and M PSD on
all of R^5. Original gradients at zero steps are not identified or
deleted. For actual positions X_i, gradients G_i, and values F_i set

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(F_i-F_4)
         +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2-sC||G_4||^2.

The accepted exact identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(G_0,...,G_4)>.(3)

The inner product of matrices is trace of the transpose times the
second matrix. Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0.
Thus a negative actual V_h contradicts the entire original certificate.

All witnesses here use saturated values

    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                       (4)

Their residuals and primitive energy simplify exactly to

    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>,
    V_h=||X_0||^2-||X_4+sG_4||^2.                  (5)

For the second identity, 2sum h_i<X_i,G_i>-sum h_i^2||G_i||^2
telescopes to ||X_0||^2-||X_4||^2 under the original updates; the
remaining terms are -2s<X_4,G_4>-s^2||G_4||^2.

Here is the static realization used below, including the optimum.
Suppose K is the convex hull of the displayed gradients, contains0,
and every source vertex satisfies <G-G_j,X_j-G_j><=0. Define

    F_K(X)=max_(G in K)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).             (6)

Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer as the Euclidean
projection P_K(X). The normal inequalities imply P_K(X_j)=G_j, so
the function has precisely(4) at each original query. Two projection
inequalities, added, give

    <X-X',P_K(X)-P_K(X')> >= ||P_K(X)-P_K(X')||^2.

Hence P_K is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing inequalities give

    0<=F_K(X')-F_K(X)-<P_K(X),X'-X>
       <=<P_K(X')-P_K(X),X'-X><=||X'-X||^2.

This proves differentiability and gradient P_K. The function is convex
as a maximum of affine functions, and finite because K is compact.
Since0 is in K, F_K>=0=F_K(0). In particular it has a true minimizer.
No uniqueness or normalized objective payoff is asserted. For(4), all
Q_(i,star) vanish; Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,X_i-G_i> will also be checked.

## 3. The older cuts and the terminal triangle, freshly derived

For j in{0,1,2,3}, let Pj=sum_(i<j)h_i and Rj=sum_(i>j)h_i.
Every full certificate obeys

    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+Pj)(1+Rj).                     (7)

When h_j>1, put A=1+Pj,B=1+Rj,r=(h_j-1)/B. The function(6) with
K=[-r,1], started at X_0=A, has gradient1 throughout the prefix up
to j. The selected update moves X_j=1 to -Br. The remaining gradients
are all -r, and X_4=G_4=-r. Every original zero step is included.
Equation(5) gives V_h=A^2-C^2r^2, proving(7). When h_j<=1 the cut
is automatic.

Write

    m=1+w, N=u+m, D=1+v, c=z-1, d=v-1.

Then C=N+D+c. In our threshold region, the endpoint cuts give

    u,v <= 2C/(C+1) < 2.                            (8)

If an endpoint is at most1 its bound is automatic; otherwise(8) is
the rearranged corresponding cut. Consequently

    z>C0-19/3=104/75>1,   C0:=193/25,               (9)

using w<=4/3. Thus c>0 is derived, not imposed as a reduction.

When v>1, the terminal adjacent pair also requires cd<1. If cd>=1,
use the one-dimensional function with derivative min(x,1), namely
F(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1 and F(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1. Its initial point N
gives the exact queries

    X=(N,m,1,-c,cd),       G=(1,1,1,-c,1).

All values are(4), the function has minimum0, and(5) gives

    V_h=N^2-(N+zv)^2<0.                             (10)

Now assume v>1 and cd<1. Define

    E=Dd=v^2-1,
    Ktri=D+c(1-E)=c^2 d+(c+D)(1-cd)>0,
    Rtri=c^2 d/Ktri,   t=c/D,
    e=(1,0), r=(Rtri,sqrt(Rtri(1-Rtri))),
    p=-(c e+r)/v,   n=-c e-p,
    Btri=(cd-Rtri)/v.                               (11)

Here0<Rtri<1 and Btri>0: Ktri-c=D(1-cd)>0 implies cd>Rtri.
Direct inner products give

    <e,r>=||r||^2=Rtri,  <e,p>=-(c+Rtri)/v<0,
    <e,n>=-Btri,   <p,n>=<r,n>=t Btri.               (12)

For the last identity, expand p and n and use Ktri Rtri=c^2 d;
equivalently <p,n>=Rtri(1-cd)/v and
Rtri(1-cd)/(cd-Rtri)=c/D=t.

Take the original base trajectory

    Xbase=(N e,m e,e,-c e,r),
    Gbase=(e,e,e,p,r).                               (13)

All physical updates hold because -c e-vp=r. Its nonzero target
normals are (N-1)e at0, w e at1, and n at3. At0 and1 the e-component
of each other vertex is at most1. At3, the comparisons from e and r
are respectively -(1+t)Btri and0 by(12). The other target normals are
zero. This checks all ordered finite rows. Also
c e+v p+r=0, with positive coefficients, so0 belongs to conv{e,p,r}.
The optimum products are N-1,w,0,t Btri,0, all nonnegative.
Thus(6) is one legal static function realizing(13), and(5) yields

    V_h=N^2-C^2 Rtri.

The older terminal-triangle necessity, on its proved domain, is

    cd<1,       C^2 Rtri<=N^2       when v>1.         (14)

Nothing from an unexamined global domain reduction was used.

## 4. The enlarged nested family and every original row

This section is valid for all u>1,w>=0,z>1,v>1 with cd<1, without
assuming a threshold. Put

    a=u-1, q=a/m>0, t=c/D>0,

and use all triangle quantities from(11). Given rho>=0, form vectors
in the direct sum of two Euclidean planes:

    X_0=(N e,sqrt(rho)e),       G_0=(e,sqrt(rho)e),
    X_1=(m e,-sqrt(rho)a e),    G_1=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),
    X_2=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),      G_2=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),
    X_3=(-c e,sqrt(rho)cq e),   G_3=(p,-sqrt(rho)q p),
    X_4=(r,-sqrt(rho)q r),      G_4=(r,-sqrt(rho)q r). (15)

All vectors lie in the sum of the two-dimensional subspace
{(y,-sqrt(rho)q y): y in R^2} and the line generated by(e,sqrt(rho)e).
Thus the function and trajectory can be placed in dimension at most3.
The use of direct-sum coordinates is just explicit notation, not a
restriction on the full original certificate.

The first auxiliary update is1-u=-a, the second is-a+wq=-q because
a=mq, the third is-q+zq=cq, and the fourth iscq e+vq p=-q r.
The base updates were checked in(13). Every step retains its original
length. Assign the values(4).

The complete target normals are

    Y_0=((N-1)e,0), Y_1=(w e,-sqrt(rho)wq e),
    Y_2=0, Y_3=(n,-sqrt(rho)q n), Y_4=0.

Here is the exhaustive finite-row list, grouped by target:

* Target0: sources1,2 give0; source3 gives
  (N-1)(<e,p>-1)<0; source4 gives(N-1)(Rtri-1)<0.
* Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q)<=0; source2 gives0;
  sources3,4 respectively give
  w*(1+rho*q^2)(<e,p>-1)<=0 and
  w*(1+rho*q^2)(Rtri-1)<=0.
* Targets2 and4: every source gives0.
* Target3: sources1,2 give-(1+rho*q^2)(1+t)Btri<0;
  source4 gives0; source0 gives precisely

      Q_03=Btri[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-qt)].                (16)

Thus all twenty original finite rows hold if and only if

    0<=rho<=(1+t)/(q(1-qt))       when qt<1,
    arbitrary rho>=0             when qt>=1.         (17)

This includes qt=1 and rho=0. Necessity is the single row(16), since
Btri>0. No other pair is deleted. The optimum products <G_i,Y_i> are

    N-1, w(1+rho*q^2), 0, t Btri(1+rho*q^2), 0.

They are nonnegative; hence every optimum row holds too. Moreover
cG_1+vG_3+G_4=0, so the static function(6) for

    K_nested=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3,G_4}                  (18)

has minimum0 and realizes every sample. It is one fixed function for
this word and this amplitude, not a different function at each query.
It is not asserted to be a common function independent of the word.

Its exact energy for every allowed amplitude is

    V_h(rho)=N^2-C^2 Rtri+rho*(1-C^2 q^2 Rtri).     (19)

This is the complete amplitude test for this explicit family on the
stated domain. At qt<1 it suffices and is necessary to test both
endpoints of(17), since(19) is affine. At qt>=1 it is equivalent to
N^2-C^2 Rtri>=0 and1-C^2 q^2 Rtri>=0. These equivalences concern
this family alone, not all legal functions or all certificates.

For qt<1 choose the upper endpoint in(17), and define

    k=(qN^2-1/q)/(1+q)
      =u*(N-m/(u-1))=u^2+m*u-m-m/(u-1).

Expanding(19) after multiplication by1-qt gives

    V_h=(1+q)/(1-qt) * Gtri,
    Gtri=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2 Rtri.                      (20)

Indeed the coefficient of C^2 Rtri becomes-(1+q), and the remaining
numerator is (1+q)N^2-(1+t)(qN^2-1/q).

## 5. The planar member needed below v=sqrt(2)

For u>1,w>=0,z>1,v>=0, put q=(u-1)/m,t=c/D and use scalar base data

    Xbase=(N,m,1,-c,-t), Gbase=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    Xaux=(1,-a,-q,cq,tq), Gaux=(1,-q,-q,tq,tq),
    X_i=(Xbase_i,sqrt(rho)Xaux_i),
    G_i=(Gbase_i,sqrt(rho)Gaux_i).

The four updates are N-u=m,m-w=1,1-z=-c,-c+vt=-t, and
1-u=-a,-a+wq=-q,-q+zq=cq,cq-vtq=tq. Use(4).
The base normals are(N-1,w,0,-vt,0), and the auxiliary normals are
(0,-wq,0,vtq,0). The complete finite rows are:

* Target0: sources1,2 give0 and sources3,4 give-(1+t)(N-1).
* Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q); source2 gives0;
  sources3,4 give-w*(1+t)(1+rho*q^2).
* Targets2,4: all sources give0.
* Target3: sources1,2 give-vt*(1+t)(1+rho*q^2); source4 gives0;
  source0 gives vt[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-tq)].

All optimum products are N-1,w(1+rho*q^2),0,
vt^2(1+rho*q^2),0. Also G_4=-tG_1, so0 is in the hull of
G_0,G_1,G_4. The function(6) realizes these data.

The full allowed amplitude is(17) when v>0 and tq<1, and arbitrary
rho>=0 when v=0 or tq>=1. In particular, whenever0<tq<1 the capped
rho=(1+t)/(q(1-tq)) is legal even at v=0. At that amplitude(5) gives

    V_h=(1+q)/(1-tq) * Gcut,
    Gcut=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2 t^2.                       (21)

This is rederived here; no fixed trajectory is extrapolated across
an unchecked phase.

## 6. Two elementary tail formulas

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), so lambda(lambda-2)=1, and let L=C-N denote
the sum c+D in this section only. At fixed D and c=L-D, write

    R(L,D)=c^2(D-2)/[D+c(1-D(D-2))].                (22)

For D>=lambda and cd<1 its denominator is positive. With
E=D(D-2)>=1 and chi=1-(E-1)t in(0,1], equation(22) reads

    R=t^2 E/chi,
    R_c=E*t*(1+chi)/(D*chi^2) >= 2t/D >0.           (23)

For the inequality, (1+chi)/chi^2>=2 follows from
1+chi-2chi^2=(1-chi)(1+2chi)>=0. In the planar case Rcut=t^2,
the corresponding derivative is exactly2t/D.

For any fixed0<rbar<1, the inequality R(L,D)>rbar is equivalent,
on a positive denominator, to

    F_rbar(D):=(D-2)(L-D)(L-(1-rbar)D)-rbar*L >0.   (24)

If L>=18/5 and lambda<=D<=14/5, its second derivative satisfies

    F_rbar''=6(1-rbar)D-2L(2-rbar)-4(1-rbar)
        <=[64(1-rbar)-36(2-rbar)]/5
         =-(8+28rbar)/5<0.                         (25)

Therefore positive endpoint values imply positivity throughout the
closed interval, by concavity. This avoids assuming R is monotone
in D; it need not be. At D=lambda, its exact value is

    R(L,lambda)=(L-lambda)^2/lambda^2.               (26)

## 7. Every possible old survivor in(1) enters the proved phase

Assume all cuts(7) and the terminal condition(14) hold. First v<9/5.
Indeed if v>=9/5, the last cut implies C>=v/(2-v)>=9. By(8),
N<13/3 and D<3, so c=C-N-D>5/3 and d>=4/5. Thus cd>4/3,
contradicting(14). This used no capacity upper bound from elsewhere.

Next N>4, so u=N-m>5/3. If v<=sqrt(2), the third cut gives

    N>=C(C-D)/(C+D).

For C>=C0,D<=lambda<17/7, this is greater than
C0(C0-17/7)/(C0+17/7)=89359/22200>4. The monotonicities follow from
partial_C[C(C-D)/(C+D)]=1-2D^2/(C+D)^2>0 for C>D, and
partial_D=-2C^2/(C+D)^2<0.

For sqrt(2)<=v<9/5, suppose instead N<=4. Then L=C-N>=Lstar:=93/25.
Use(22) at the virtual Lstar. Its denominator stays positive because

    (Lstar-D)(D-2) <= (Lstar-2)^2/4
                  =1849/2500<1.

The monotonicity R_c>0 in(23), with the actual cd<1, gives
R(L,D)>=R(Lstar,D). Take rstar=16/C0^2, which lies in(0,1/3).
At D=lambda, using lambda<17/7,

    C0*(Lstar/lambda-1)
       >C0*(Lstar/(17/7)-1)=43618/10625>4.

Thus R(Lstar,lambda)>rstar. At D=14/5, direct substitution gives

    R(Lstar,14/5)=2116/5185>1/3>rstar.

Equations(24),(25) with L=Lstar show R(Lstar,D)>rstar for the whole
interval. Consequently C^2 R(L,D)>16>=N^2, contradicting(14).
This proves N>4 also on the higher-terminal branch.

We have therefore derived, for every old survivor in(1),

    5/3<u<2, 1<=m<=7/3, c>0,
    q=(u-1)/m in(0,1), 0<t<=N/C<1.                (27)

The last inequality is the third cut. Thus qt<1. All zero and small
step cases were handled before choosing a family; none was discarded.
For v<=sqrt(2) use the planar function in Section5. For v>=sqrt(2)
use the nested triangle in Section4, whose cd<1 was proved above.

## 8. Reduce the whole region to fixed first step and prefix mass

On the bounding rectangle5/3<u<2,1<=m<=7/3, the coefficient k obeys

    k_m=u-1-1/(u-1)<0,
    k_u=2u+m+m/(u-1)^2>0,
    k>=k(5/3,7/3)=5/6>0.                           (28)

Both energies have the form

    G=N^2-k*(C-N)/D-C^2 R,

where R=t^2 in the planar case and R is(22) in the triangle case.
Where c>0 and the triangle denominator, if present, is positive,

    G_C=-k/D-2C R-C^2 R_c<0,
    G_m=2N-k_m*(1+t)+k/D+C^2 R_c>0.                (29)

For the planar case additionally

    G_D=(k+2C^2 t)*(C-N)/D^2>0.                    (30)

In the planar branch increase D to lambda using(30). In either branch
increase m to7/3 and decrease C to

    Cmin(u)=max(C0,u/(2-u)),

which is permitted by the original first cut. These comparisons only
increase G. They need not preserve every other old predicate, since
they are comparisons of the explicit expressions, not new candidate
certificates. Their necessary sign conditions do hold: c stays positive,
since C0-2-7/3-14/5=44/75>0; k stays positive by(28). In the triangle
case m increasing and C decreasing only decrease c and hence preserve
cd<1 and positivity of the denominator.

We are left with m=7/3 and lambda<=D<=14/5; the planar case has just
D=lambda. For each such D the maximum over u is at

    U0=2C0/(C0+1)=193/109.                          (31)

To prove this, first take5/3<u<=U0. Here Cmin=C0, N>4, U0<9/5, and

    k_u<18/5+7/3+21/4=671/60<45/4,
    7/25<t<3/5,
    C0^2>59.

For the lower t bound, c>59/75 gives t>59/210>7/25. For the upper
bound use N>4 and lambda>12/5, giving t<(93/25)/(12/5)-1=11/20.
By(23) or its planar equality,

    G_u=2N-k_u*(1+t)+k/D+C0^2 R_c
        >8-18+59/5=9/5>0.                          (32)

Next take U0<=u<2 with Cmin=C_u=u/(2-u). Since U0>7/4,
C_u'=2/(2-u)^2>32. Let L0=C0-U0-7/3. It exceeds18/5. Along this
branch c=C_u-u-7/3-D increases with u. Therefore

    t>2/7, R_c>=2t/D>10/49, C_u^2 R_c>10.

The total derivative is

    2N-k_u*(1+t)-k*(C_u'-1)/D
        -2C_u C_u'R-C_u^2 R_c*(C_u'-1)
      <26/3-310<0.                                 (33)

This proves(31). All comparison paths in u toward U0 decrease c:
below U0, increase u at fixed C0; above U0, decrease u with C=C_u.
Thus a triangle denominator that was positive remains positive. No
unproved phase or physical reversal is used in the reduction.

## 9. Uniform terminal comparison at the remaining corner

At u=U0,m=7/3,C=C0 the exact constants are

    N0=1342/327<411/100,
    L0=C0-N0=29561/8175,
    723/200<L0<11/3, in particular L0>18/5,
    k0=815039/427716>19/10.                          (34)

For example N0-m/(U0-1)=1342/327-109/36=4223/3924,
which gives the displayed k0 after multiplication by193/109.

We claim, uniformly for lambda<=D<=14/5, that

    C0^2 R(L0,D) > (383/100)^2.                     (35)

Its denominator is positive throughout, since
(L0-D)(D-2)<(11/3-2)^2/4=25/36<1. Put
rbar=(383/772)^2; then0<rbar<1/4 and C0=772/100.
At D=lambda, use sqrt(2)<99/70, hence lambda<169/70, and(34):

    C0*(L0/lambda-1)
      >(193/25)*[(723/200)/(169/70)-1]
       =324433/84500 > 383/100.

At D=14/5, c=L0-D>4/5 and

    Ktri=14/5-(31/25)c<226/125,
    c^2 d>64/125,
    R(L0,14/5)>32/113>1/4>rbar.

Thus(24),(25) prove(35) over the entire interval. The planar energy
at D=lambda has exactly the same R by(26). Combining(29)--(35)
now gives for either branch

    G < (411/100)^2
         -(19/10)*(18/5)/(14/5)
         -(383/100)^2
      = 2779/1250-171/70
      = -961/4375.                                  (36)

Every quantity bounded below in the subtracted products is positive.
The multiplier in(20) or(21) is(1+q)/(1-qt)>1 at the original word.
Hence its actual static witness satisfies(2).

## 10. Conclusion, exact comparison scope, and remaining task

If any old cut fails, Section3 gives an actual scalar V_h<0. If the
terminal condition fails at v>1, its scalar or planar function in
Section3 gives V_h<0. Otherwise Sections7--9 apply the explicitly
legal nested family and give the strict uniform margin(2). In every
case(3) excludes all original twenty-sign, exact-flow, full-R5 arrays.
This proves the regional assertion(1) as a joint unverified draft.

The explicit old inventory N_pub, including every N1--N8 predicate on
its conditional domain, is retained. This exclusion needs only its
four cuts and terminal adjacent triangle; no omitted old predicate is
asserted redundant. S_scalar remains the entire specified proportional-
gradient polynomial geometry with all legal values, not all nonlinear
one-dimensional trajectories. No smaller list is substituted for it.

More precisely, the conjunction of the full old inventory, S_scalar,
and the full planar-plus-terminal-triangle nested families just derived
is empty in(1). This is a correctly scoped enlargement of the nested
family. It is not a claim that the previously published planar test
alone already excludes every new point here. No independent-test
separation from the complete previous conjunction is asserted: that
would require an actual survivor of that previous conjunction. Nor is
one inferred from a virtual parameter used in the comparisons.

This complete regional exclusion is the output. The only surviving
threshold possibility left by it has second physical step w>4/3, still
subject to every old predicate and original-array requirement. There
is no justification for swapping w and z or reversing the word.

No exact h,Lambda,M seed, global four-step impossibility, negative
objective error, or original asymptotic profile theorem has been
established. The sufficient lift04219554e41d9750 remains conditional.
The original Goal remains OPEN. Formal verification is not authorized
in this allocation; all new assertions here remain UNVERIFIED.

Resource use: zero numerical evaluations, solver calls, machine-algebra

# Bidirectional endpoint certificate for every whole-root-second pair

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Project gd-exact-profile-20260926,
worker max4, invocation78, 2026-10-02. No new mathematical statement in
this report has been submitted to, or accepted by, the verifier.

This is one authorized analytical invocation, with two mechanisms:
(1) an explicitly paid backward cycle repairs the adjacent leaf/root
interface; (2) a chronological dyadic classification extends the same
certificate to every first checkpoint before the whole root. There is
no mathematical program, numerical/arithmetic replay, solver, recurrence
execution, formal call, fact_submit, consultation, installation, or
subagent. The original unrestricted minimax target remains OPEN.

## 1. Statement, complete quantifiers, and accepted inputs

Fix an arbitrary integer m>=1536 and use the SAME enclosing parameters
at every child:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_h=1-1/(4m),
    c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a=beta_h/c,
    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l), 0<=l<m,
    S_l=sum H_l, X_l=1+S_l, h=H_m, n=2^m-1.

Define actual zero-based physical indices and masses

    k=2^(m-1)-1, j_c=2^(m-2)-1,
    U=X_(m-1), e=a*c^(m-1), d0=1+a,
    Z=1+2S_m=4U+2e-1.

Here k is the whole-word root, j_c the root of its LEFT child, and d0
the ACTUAL final leaf. The report concerns EVERY first checkpoint

                         0<=j<k.                         (1.1)

For arbitrary finite real Euclidean dimension, consider every actual
GD transcript of ONE globally convex differentiable function with
1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, with queried gradients

    g_i=p  for 0<=i<=j,
    g_i=q  for j+1<=i<=k,
    g_i=r  for k+1<=i<n.                                (1.2)

The vectors p,q,r, their lengths and mutual angles, all sampled values,
heights, affine offsets, the initial orientation, and the dimension
are unrestricted beyond legality and (1.2). No orthogonality, common
direction, fixed rank, zero height, or zero interpolation remainder
is assumed. Every original physical step is retained.

Family E_j additionally has actual terminal gradient g_n=r. Family Q_j
allows EVERY legal terminal gradient and EVERY static smooth-convex
completion of the entire prescribed queried prefix. For each such
function and any of its own minimizers x_star with x_0!=x_star, define

               V=2(f(x_n)-min f)/||x_0-x_star||^2.

The candidate conclusion, proved jointly below, is

    sup_(E_j) V = 1/Z = K_n(h),
    1/Z <= sup_(Q_j) V <= 1/(Z-d0^2),                    (1.3)
    B_n sup_(E_j) V < 47/50,
    B_n sup_(Q_j) V < 940/999 < 19/20.                  (1.4)

Here K_n is the COMPLETE accepted Huber/single-cut maximum, including
every original cut, and B_n is the ORIGINAL benchmark denominator
with every integer split. The bounds apply about every actual
minimizer of each completed function; in particular they use each
function's OWN closest initial distance if that is the chosen radius.

For advertised L>0, replace f by (f-min f)/L and translate the chosen
actual minimizer to0. Its gradient is 1-Lipschitz, the physical word
is unchanged, and V is exactly 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2).
No factor-2 normalization is changed.

The accepted correctness premises are only:

* 1252b8823bb5a290: necessity/sufficiency of all ordered smooth-convex
  interpolation inequalities and its explicit conjugate interpolant.
* b98bb807d514e80d: the definition and lower-witness scope of K_n, and
  the exact original benchmark coordinates/recursions.
* 931b9653ce0f7201: at precisely this same-m tau=1/4 hierarchy and every
  m>=1536, the UPPER comparison B_n/Z<47/50.

These actual facts were read. No obstruction for the spiral families
in 5fd396eba75651e7 is transferred to the present family; its older
benchmark comparison is unnecessary. The earlier endpoint report
6b8cd30a96a9165b is UNVERIFIED awareness, not a premise. All endpoint,
cycle, hierarchy, contact, and PSD calculations needed here are
rederived jointly below. Latest steering: 885472ffb7c11cba; own plan:
402b82b97ff12a3f.

This is a theorem candidate only for (1.2). Pairs not involving the
whole root, nonconstant gradients within a sector, and unrestricted
W_n(H_m) remain outside its scope. In particular it does NOT prove
the full-W hypothesis 17/(16Z) in accepted931b9653ce0f7201 and does
not resolve the original minimax problem.

## 2. Complete endpoint system and original-query lift

Normalize the chosen optimum to (x_star,g_star,f_star)=(0,0,0).
For any original pair write the full chronological word as

    h=(block_0,b,block_1,c_step,block_2),
    P=sum block_0, M=sum block_1, T=sum block_2,
    u=P+1, v=M+1, d=b-1, e=c_step-1,
    A=u+d=P+b, B=v+e=M+c_step, U_loc=u+v+d.

The notation U_loc is general in this section; at a root-second
pair it equals U from Section1. At such a pair e is also exactly
the root excess from Section1 and T=U-1.

Let F_p,F_q,F_r denote the values at the first query of each sector,
and set

    Z_p=x_0, Z_q=x_0-Ap, Z_r=x_0-Ap-Bq.

For every ACTUAL cumulative step mass s in its sector, the jets are

    (Z_p-sp, p, F_p-s||p||^2),  0<=s<=P,
    (Z_q-sq, q, F_q-s||q||^2),  0<=s<=M,
    (Z_r-sr, r, F_r-s||r||^2).                         (2.1)

For E the final range is 0<=s<=T. For Q its queried part is
0<=s<=T-d0 and the separate actual terminal position is
x_n=Z_r-Tr. Equal gradients force the displayed values: the two
convexity inequalities between any two such samples force equality
with their common affine support. Each original step inside a sector
therefore pays its actual h_i||g_i||^2 decrease.

For ordered samples define the signed residual, in the orientation
of accepted1252b8823bb5a290,

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.     (2.2)

Keep both actual endpoints of every queried sector, identifying
coincident index labels, and the optimum jet. For E keep the terminal
as the last r endpoint. For Q additionally keep the separate terminal
jet (x_n,g_n,f_n). Require ALL ordered inequalities among these jets,
including all remote, optimum, and terminal rows.

This is a complete endpoint representation, not a relaxation. Indeed,
for a source sector with data (Z_a,g_a,F_a), source mass s, and target
sector (Z_b,g_b,F_b), target mass t, substitution gives

    Q_(a(s),b(t))
     =F_a-F_b+<g_a,Z_b-Z_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2
                        +t(||g_b||^2-<g_a,g_b>).       (2.3)

The source mass cancels and the target dependence is affine. Hence
every original interior ordered row is a convex combination of
endpoint rows. Same-sector rows are zero. Rows involving the optimum
or separate terminal are affine in the varying sector mass as well.
Endpoint inequalities imply ALL original query, terminal, and optimum
rows; the converse follows because the endpoints are actual samples.
This includes singleton first or middle sectors, notably j=0 and j=k-1.

For a full constructive lift, for each retained endpoint/terminal/
optimum jet i put

    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    H(vect)=||vect||^2/2+max_i{<y_i,vect>-delta_i},
    F(x)=sup_vect{<x,vect>-H(vect)}.                    (2.4)

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 makes F finite, convex, differentiable and
globally 1-smooth, interpolating every supplied jet. For an interior
sector sample its affine anchor is affine in s, since

    y(s)=Z_a-(s+1)g_a,
    delta(s)=F_a-(s+1/2)||g_a||^2.

Its anchor is thus a convex combination of endpoint anchors. Adding
EVERY original interior anchor changes neither H nor F. The given
values and gradients remain intact, and induction through the actual
GD updates reaches every original sample and the actual terminal.

For completeness, Q_(star,i)<=0 gives delta_i>=0. The optimum anchor
is0, so H(0)=0 and H(vect)>=||vect||^2/2. Consequently min F=0 and

              argmin F=conv{y_i:delta_i=0}.             (2.5)

To justify the last formula, grad F(x)=0 exactly when x belongs to
the subdifferential of H at0. The quadratic has derivative0 there;
the subdifferential of the finite affine maximum is the convex hull
of the active slopes, precisely the displayed set. Inclusion of
that hull follows from the supporting inequalities; separation of
a point outside the hull gives the reverse inclusion.

Thus (2.5) is the full minimum set of the constructed completion,
not a selected subset. A different legal completion can have a
different minimum set. The certificate below starts from any actual
minimizer of THAT function, so it does not inherit a radius from
another completion. Each nonempty closed convex minimum set in finite
dimension has a closest point to x_0.

## 3. Exact backward cycle and all nonzero original multipliers

Let L_p=j be the last p query, L_q=k the last q query, and R_0=k+1
the first r query. Their actual jets are

    L_p: (x_0-Pp,p,F_p-P||p||^2),
    L_q: (x_0-Ap-Mq,q,F_q-M||q||^2),
    R_0: (x_0-Ap-Bq,r,F_r).

Direct substitution into (2.2) yields

    Q_(L_p,star)=F_p-<p,x_0>+||p||^2/2,
    Q_(L_q,star)=F_q-<q,x_0>+A<p,q>+||q||^2/2,
    Q_(R_0,star)=F_r-<r,x_0>+A<p,r>+B<q,r>+||r||^2/2,

    Q_(L_q,L_p)=F_q-F_p+(P+1/2)||p||^2
                                  +||q||^2/2+d<p,q>,
    Q_(R_0,L_q)=F_r-F_q+(M+1/2)||q||^2
                                  +||r||^2/2+e<q,r>,
    Q_(L_p,L_q)=F_p-F_q+(1/2-A)||p||^2
                                  +(M+1/2)||q||^2-v<p,q>.

In particular the exact two-row cycle is

    C_pq:=Q_(L_p,L_q)+Q_(L_q,L_p)
       =-d||p||^2+(d-v)<p,q>+v||q||^2.                (3.1)

No value or initial-point term remains. This cycle is NOT itself PSD;
its negative p-square and mixed term must also be paid.

Choose 0<=alpha<=theta<=1, beta=theta-alpha, gamma=1-theta, eta>=0.
The core multiplier table is

| Original ordered row | Multiplier mu |
|---|---:|
| Q_(L_p,star) | alpha |
| Q_(L_q,star) | beta |
| Q_(R_0,star) | gamma |
| Q_(L_q,L_p) | alpha+eta |
| Q_(L_p,L_q) | eta |
| Q_(R_0,L_q) | theta |

All other core multipliers are ZERO; their primal rows are still
required by Section2. At L_p, outgoing alpha+eta equals incoming
alpha+eta. At L_q, outgoing beta+alpha+eta equals incoming theta+eta.
At R_0 the outgoing sum is gamma+theta=1. The optimum has incoming1.
The cycle therefore changes no value flow. The weighted value part
is exactly F_r.

Set gbar=alpha*p+beta*q+gamma*r. If t_pay is a terminal charge and
w=t_pay+1/2, the sum of the core rows, plus t_pay||r||^2, equals

                 F_r-<gbar,x_0>+Q(p,q,r),

where the COMPLETE gradient quadratic is

    Q=(alpha*u-eta*d)||p||^2+(theta+eta)*v||q||^2
          +w||r||^2
       +[alpha*d+beta*A+eta*(d-v)]<p,q>
       +gamma*A<p,r>+[theta*e+gamma*B]<q,r>.           (3.2)

Let U_loc=u+v+d and D=U_loc+e+w. On the common-gradient direction
p=q=r, Q=D||r||^2. The p row sum of (3.2) is

                   [alpha*u+A-eta*(d+v)]/2.

The cumulative p,q row sum is [(1+theta)U_loc+e]/2: set p=q in
(3.2), whose first-group square coefficient is theta*U_loc and
whose cross coefficient with r is (1-theta)U_loc+e.
Therefore both contact equations, including the cycle cost, are

    theta=(U_loc+e)/(2D-U_loc),
    alpha=[A-eta*(d+v)]/(2D-u).                       (3.3)

These are rederived contact equations. The old forward weights have
not been held fixed while adding a cycle.

Put s=p-q and z=q-r, so gbar=alpha*s+theta*z+r. After (3.3), the
common-gradient square and all its cross terms vanish from
Q-D||gbar||^2. The remainder on ALL real increment vectors is

    J11||s||^2+2J12<s,z>+J22||z||^2,
    J11=alpha*u-eta*d-D*alpha^2,
    J22=theta*(U_loc-D*theta),
    J12=(1-theta)*(2D*alpha-A)/2.                     (3.4)

For an explicit mixed-coefficient check before imposing the second
contact equation, the s,z coefficient in Q is
alpha*u+theta*A-eta*(d+v). Subtracting 2D*alpha*theta and using
eta*(d+v)=A-alpha*(2D-u) gives exactly 2J12 in (3.4).
The s-square comes directly from the p-square in (3.2); the z-square
comes from p=q, where the cycle is identically zero. Thus (3.4)
accounts for the entire reduced space, not just its diagonal.

Two explicit choices of the multipliers will suffice:

DIAGONAL CYCLE choice, valid when u*v>d*U_loc:

    alpha=A/(2D), eta=alpha*u/(d+v), beta=theta-alpha,
    J12=0,
    J11=alpha*(u*v-d*U_loc)/[2(d+v)].                  (3.5)

The second identity in (3.3) follows since
eta*(d+v)=alpha*u and A=2D*alpha. For the J11 identity,

    J11=alpha*[u*v/(d+v)-A/2],
    2u*v-A*(d+v)=u*v-d*(u+v+d).

FORWARD CHAIN choice, reserved below for the actual left-child root:

    eta=0, alpha=A/(2D-u), beta=theta-alpha,
    J11=alpha*(u-D*alpha),
    J12=alpha*u*(1-theta)/2.                          (3.6)

Both choices use the same original row table, and both retain (3.4)'s
J22. Positivity of all multipliers and the entire residual will be
proved for the stated physical regions, not assumed.

## 4. Initial point, terminal step, and full quadratic identity

For E take t_pay=T and add Q_(n,R_0) with multiplier1. Since g_n=r,
this row is f_n-F_r+T||r||^2. The total weighted sum is

                    f_n-<gbar,x_0>+Q.                 (4.1)

For Q let N_-=n-1 be the ACTUAL final queried index, and s_n=g_n.
Add Q_(N_-,R_0) and Q_(n,N_-) with multipliers1. Their sum is

    f_n-F_r+(T-d0^2/2)||r||^2
                          +||s_n+(d0-1)r||^2/2.      (4.2)

Indeed x_n=x_(N_-)-d0*r, and

    Q_(n,N_-)=f_n-f_(N_-)+d0<s_n,r>+||s_n-r||^2/2,
    Q_(N_-,R_0)=f_(N_-)-F_r+(T-d0)||r||^2.

Completing the square gives (4.2). Set t_pay=T-d0^2/2 in Q.
Thus for Q the total row sum is

        f_n-<gbar,x_0>+Q+||s_n+(d0-1)r||^2/2.          (4.3)

For the root-second family the entire right child is the last block,
so T=U-1, N_->R_0, and all these indices are genuine distinct samples.
The first terminal row simply carries value flow through the final
constant-gradient sector; no suffix is removed. The final square
holds for every real s_n, and imposes no terminal-gradient restriction.

Now set lambda_ij=2mu_ij, with mu from Section3 and the respective
terminal path. Every original finite node has flow0 except n, whose
outgoing-minus-incoming flow is2. The optimum absorbs2. In particular
all unlisted original interior nodes have zero flow. All sampled
values cancel, including all paid heights. The full identity is

    ||x_0||^2/(2D)-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij
       =2D||gbar-x_0/(2D)||^2
          +2[J11||s||^2+2J12<s,z>+J22||z||^2]
          +terminal_square,                           (4.4)

where terminal_square=0 for E and
terminal_square=||s_n+(d0-1)r||^2 for Q.

This is an identity on the ENTIRE formal space (x_0,p,q,r) for E,
and (x_0,p,q,r,s_n) for Q, with arbitrary real vector entries. Its
lift to the original queries is obtained by (1.2) and the exact
original positions; unused interior row multipliers are0. This is
full PSD on the equal-sector subspace, not a claim of PSD with all
original query gradients independent. The initial orientation and
common-gradient direction are accounted for by the first square.

If D>0 and J is PSD, all multipliers nonnegative and Q_ij<=0 yield

                   2f_n<=||x_0||^2/(2D).              (4.5)

The sign follows from (4.4): its nonnegative right side is at most
||x_0||^2/(2D)-2f_n because the weighted residuals are nonpositive.

## 5. Hierarchy bounds, uniform in the enclosing integer

All elementary hierarchy estimates are derived here. Literal
concatenation gives

    X_(l+1)=2X_l+a*c^l, X_0=1,
    X_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a/(c-2).             (5.1)

The latter formula has the correct initial value and satisfies the
recurrence by v_h(c-2)=a. No recurrence is executed.

From 7/5<sqrt(2)<99/70, verified by squaring positive rationals,
lambda>12/5 and lambda<169/70. Since 1/(4m)<=1/6144,

    12/5<c<(169/70)(6145/6144)<29/12.

The last comparison is equivalent to 169*12<2*6144. Also
beta_h>=6143/6144>99/100, so

    a>(99/100)(12/29)>2/5.

The identity lambda(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda imply
c(c-2)>1>beta_h. Therefore

    2/5<a<c-2<5/12, 0<v_h<1, X_l>=2^l.              (5.2)

Using (5.1),

    a*c^l/X_l=a/[v_h+(1-v_h)(2/c)^l].

Its denominator lies in (v_h,1] for all finite l>=0. Hence

    2/5<a<=a*c^l/X_l<c-2<5/12,
    7/5<d0=1+a<17/12<3/2,
    U>=4096, 2/5<e/U<5/12.                           (5.3)

The weak middle inequality is equality only at l=0. U>=4096 follows
from U>=2^(m-1) and m-1>=12.

For the two terminal regimes put

    epsilon_E=1/2, epsilon_Q=(1+d0^2)/2,
    epsilon=epsilon_E or epsilon_Q,
    delta=epsilon/U, t_root=e/U.

Because d0<3/2, 0<epsilon<13/8 and delta<1/1000. For root-second
pairs the mass identities are

    U_loc=u+v+d=U, T=U-1,
    w=U-epsilon, D=2U+e-epsilon,
    2D=Z                    for E,
    2D=Z-d0^2               for Q.                    (5.4)

In particular, with D_bar=D/U,

    2399/1000 < D_bar < 29/12,
    theta=(1+t_root)/(3+2t_root-2delta),
    9/25<theta<3/8,
    J22=theta*(U-D*theta)>(3/80)U.                   (5.5)

For the first bound use t_root>2/5 and delta<1/1000. The lower
theta bound follows from theta>(7/5)/(23/6)=42/115>9/25. The upper
one follows from theta<(17/12)/(19/5-1/500)<3/8.
For its diagonal residual,

    1-D_bar*theta
      =[1-t_root-t_root^2-delta*(1-t_root)]
                          /(3+2t_root-2delta)
      >(59/144-1/1000)/(23/6)>5/48.

The last comparison uses 59/144-115/288=1/96>1/1000. Multiplying
by theta>9/25 gives J22/U>3/80. These uniform bounds apply to BOTH
terminal regimes; no approximation in m or omission of a leaf occurs.

## 6. Mechanism1: the actual adjacent leaf-before-root pair

Take exactly the assigned discriminator

    j=k-1=2^(m-1)-2, k=2^(m-1)-1.

The first selected step is the last leaf of the left child, so its
excess is d=a, there is no intervening ordinary block, and

    u=U-1-a, v=1, d=a, A=U-1, T=U-1.                (6.1)

The parameters e,d0,epsilon,D remain those of Section5. Use

    theta=(U+e)/(2D-U),
    alpha=(U-1)/(2D), beta=theta-alpha, gamma=1-theta,
    eta=alpha*(U-1-a)/(1+a).                         (6.2)

They obey both contact equations. Every multiplier is nonnegative:
u>0, d+v=1+a>0, alpha>0, eta>0; also

    alpha<U/(2D)<4/19<9/25<theta<3/8<1.

Here D/U>2399/1000>19/8. Thus beta>0 and gamma>0. The residual is
diagonal, with J22>(3/80)U and

    J11=alpha*[(1-a)U-1-a]/[2(1+a)]>0.              (6.3)

For the last sign, a<5/12 and U>=4096 give
(1-a)U-1-a>(7U-17)/12>0. Equations (3.5), (4.4), and (6.3) provide
ALL the nonnegative multipliers and the complete sum of squares.

The cycle mass is allowed to be large. Its negative p-square is paid
in (6.3), and its mixed effect is removed by the reselected weights,
not ignored. This repairs the precise physical pair that defeated the
old forward-only triangular support. It does not rely on a formal
negative direction being a feasible primal transcript, and does not
reverse the chronology of GD.

## 7. Mechanism2: every first checkpoint, with a physical split

Set X=X_(m-2) and d_c=a*c^(m-2). The actual left child is
(H_(m-2),1+d_c,H_(m-2)), so

                       U=2X+d_c.                      (7.1)

For any first selected index j<k let ell=nu_2(j+1), the largest
nonnegative integer with 2^ell dividing j+1. The first step excess
is d=a*c^ell. Its actual left and right child words are both H_ell,
with mass X_ell-1. Both lie inside the complete left child of the
whole root. Hence the original before/after masses satisfy

    u>=X_ell, v>=X_ell,
    d<(5/12)min(u,v), u+v+d=U.                       (7.2)

The asserted child words and index formula follow by induction on
literal concatenation: the middle position of H_(l+1) has index
2^l-1 and step1+a*c^l; inside either child the same rule holds.
In the right child, adding2^l to j+1 does not change its 2-adic
valuation when it is less than l. Thus every nonroot selected node
belongs to a complete pair of H_ell children inside that left child.

There are exactly three chronological regions:

* If j<j_c, the selected step is strictly before the left-child root.
  Its preceding mass obeys u<X, while its following mass includes
  the whole step1+d_c and the entire right H_(m-2), so
  v>=X+d_c+1. In particular u/v<5/7, using d_c/X>2/5.
* If j=j_c, precisely u=v=X and d=d_c.
* If j>j_c, the preceding mass includes the entire left H_(m-2)
  and step1+d_c, so u>=X+d_c+1. The following part is a strict
  suffix of the right H_(m-2), so v<X. Thus v/u<5/7.

The inequalities include the neighboring leaves, the first leaf j=0,
and the last leaf j=k-1. More explicitly, before the root the selected
step still belongs to its first child of total sum X-1, so
P<=X-1-h_j and u<=X-h_j<X. The reflected argument gives v<X
after the root. All steps are positive, so the separation cannot
be lost at any intervening index. This is a physical dyadic
classification; it is not a numerical test over checkpoint pairs.

### 7.1 Every noncentral checkpoint: diagonal cycle

For j!=j_c set l_small=min(u,v), l_large=max(u,v),
r_mass=l_small/l_large and t_small=d/l_small. Then (7.2) and the
classification give

    0<r_mass<5/7, 0<t_small<5/12,
    d*U/(u*v)=t_small*[1+(1+t_small)*r_mass]
      <(5/12)*[1+(17/12)*(5/7)]=845/1008<1.           (7.3)

Choose the diagonal cycle (3.5). As A=u+d=U-v<U,

    0<alpha=A/(2D)<4/19<theta<3/8,
    beta=theta-alpha>0, gamma=1-theta>0, eta>0.

Every original multiplier is nonnegative. Equation (7.3) makes

    J11=alpha*(u*v-d*U)/[2(d+v)]>0,
    J12=0, J22>(3/80)U>0.

Consequently (4.4) is a complete sum of nonnegative squares for
every noncentral first checkpoint and every real vector geometry.
This includes Section6's discriminator without any limiting argument.

### 7.2 The actual central checkpoint: forward chain

For j=j_c use eta=0 and alpha=A/(2D-u). This central case is treated
directly, without assuming that the diagonal-cycle condition holds.
Write x_mass=X/U. From (7.1) and 2/5<d_c/X<5/12,

    12/29<x_mass<5/12, u/U=x_mass,
    A/U=1-x_mass, 7/12<A/U<17/29.

The denominator of alpha in units of U satisfies

    2D_bar-x_mass < 29/6-12/29=769/174 <40/9,
    2D_bar-x_mass > 2*(2399/1000)-5/12 >35/8.

Therefore

    alpha>(7/12)/(40/9)=21/160>13/100,
    alpha<(17/29)/(35/8)=136/1015<27/200.             (7.4)

The last rational comparison is 27200<27405. Thus
0<alpha<theta<1 by (5.5), and beta,gamma and all row multipliers
are nonnegative. The contact equations are (3.3) with eta=0.

The two diagonal entries and mixed entry have the following explicit
uniform bounds:

    J11/U=alpha*(x_mass-D_bar*alpha)
      >(13/100)*[12/29-(29/12)*(27/200)]
      >(13/100)*(7/80)=91/8000>1/90,
    J22/U>3/80,
    0<J12/U=alpha*x_mass*(1-theta)/2
      <(27/200)*(5/12)*(16/25)/2=9/500.              (7.5)

For the strict inner comparison, 12/29-261/800=2031/23200,
whereas 7/80=2030/23200. These estimates imply

    J11*J22-J12^2
      >U^2*(1/2400-81/250000)>0,                     (7.6)

because 250000>81*2400=194400. This proves positivity on the WHOLE
two-increment space by the explicit decomposition

    J11||s+(J12/J11)z||^2
                    +(J22-J12^2/J11)||z||^2.         (7.7)

Both coefficients are positive. This is a uniform analytic proof of
full PSD, including arbitrary mixed directions, not a determinant
scan or merely a repaired diagonal.

The three physical regions exhaust EVERY j in (1.1). The same
terminal charge alternatives were used throughout. No time-reversal
principle is invoked, and no root-first result is imported as a premise.

## 8. Sharp fixed-terminal member, full K, and the benchmark

Equations (4.5) and (5.4) prove the respective upper bounds in (1.3).
To attain the fixed-terminal bound, put kappa=1/Z and use the ONE
static scalar Huber function

    f_H(x)=x^2/2                         for |x|<=kappa,
    f_H(x)=kappa*|x|-kappa^2/2            for |x|>=kappa,
    x_0=1.

Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz, and
its unique minimizer is0. For every actual cumulative word mass
0<=s<=S_m,

    x=1-kappa*s>kappa, grad f_H(x)=kappa,
    f_H(x)=kappa*(1-kappa*s)-kappa^2/2.

Indeed the smallest x is (1+S_m)/Z>1/Z since S_m>0. Induction over
every original step gives exactly these iterates, with no replay or
skipped leaf. Thus p=q=r=g_n=kappa, all sampled heights are paid,
the true closest radius is1, and

                  2f_H(x_n)=2/Z-Z/Z^2=1/Z.

This is in E_j and Q_j for every j, proving the asserted lower bounds
and the exact fixed-terminal optimum.

To compare with the COMPLETE K_n of acceptedb98bb807d514e80d, take
any original index i, let u_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t, T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t,
and d_i=h_i-1. All d_i are positive here, and the full definition is

    K_n(h)=max{1/Z, max_(0<=i<n) d_i^2/[u_i^2*(1+2T_i)]}.

Put ell_i=nu_2(i+1). Its two actual children have
mass X_(ell_i)-1, so

    u_i>=X_(ell_i), T_i>=X_(ell_i)-1,
    0<d_i<(5/12)X_(ell_i).

Writing X=X_(ell_i)>=1,

    (u_i-d_i)(1+2T_i)
       >(7/12)X(2X-1)>2*(25/144)X^2>2d_i^2.         (8.1)

The middle inequality follows from 2X-1>=X and 7/12>25/72.
Since S_m=u_i+d_i+T_i, factoring gives

    u_i^2(1+2T_i)-d_i^2(1+2S_m)
       =(u_i+d_i)*[(u_i-d_i)(1+2T_i)-2d_i^2]>0.

Every single-cut term is therefore strictly below1/Z. Along with
the global Huber term, this proves K_n(h)=1/Z for ALL original cuts.

For precision, the benchmark retained throughout is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(integer 0<=i<n) Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
    B_n=max_(integer 0<=i<n) G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/Z<47/50 at the actual horizon
n=2^m-1. Also Z=4U+2e-1>4U-1>=16383, d0^2<9/4, whence
d0^2/Z<1/1000. Consequently

    B_n sup_(E_j) V <47/50,
    B_n sup_(Q_j) V <=B_n/(Z-d0^2)
       <(47/50)*(1000/999)=940/999<19/20.

The last comparison is 18800<18981. This uses the accepted benchmark
ratio only in its valid UPPER direction, applied to a separately
derived family upper bound. It does not certify the strength of a
positive lower witness by reversing that ratio.

## 9. Scope, evidence status, and handoff

All new mathematics above is one JOINT UNVERIFIED proof attempt.
No intermediate calculation has been promoted into the fact graph,
and none is imported as an accepted building block elsewhere here.
The complete draft rederives its new endpoint lift and multiplier
algebra jointly from the cited accepted interpolation premise.

The precise progress is a candidate resolution of the missing
leaf-immediately-before-root pair and then EVERY whole-root-second
pair in the arbitrary-real-vector three-sector family. It explicitly
keeps full endpoints, all original queries, the actual final step,
every sampled height, the actual terminal jet, and each completion's
own minimum set/radius. The backward cycle does not add value flow;
it changes both contact and the full residual. The central checkpoint
has a separate complete PSD proof.

This report does not assert that the earlier root-first draft is
verified, combine two drafts into a fact, or give an all-pair result
when neither selected step is the whole root. More fundamentally,
even an all-pair three-sector bound would leave unrestricted varying
within-sector gradients and the full W_n outside its scope. The
original all-horizon/all-word minimax target remains OPEN.

Skills applied: query-memory and direct-proving. The required Matlas
query returned two statements about constant-step gradient-descent
summability and projected-gradient estimates; neither was applicable
or used. Web retrieval located Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,
"Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance
of First-order Methods", arXiv:1502.05666, as background. The existing
own downloaded text at
downloads/multistage_envelope_completion_20261002/1502.05666v6.txt
was read around its explicit conjugate-interpolant construction.
No external paper result is imported: all interpolation correctness
used here comes from accepted1252b8823bb5a290.

Initial broad recall combined oversized outputs and was truncated.
All accepted inputs and the relevant endpoint report sections were

# A critical directed cycle: exact central reversal and the remaining suffix estimate

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT, max4 round53, 2026-10-01.
No fact_submit was authorized or made. No numerical evaluations, trajectory
replays, symbolic programs, solvers, benchmark DP, installations, or subagents
were used. All new formulas below are proved together as draft evidence, not
adopted from earlier unverified findings.

This is ONE graph, weight, and initial-state law. It passes the requested early
screens: a vanishing exact radial energy gap, a genuine sector reversal on the
original word, newly activated overlapping edges, and noncommuting Hessians.
The full terminal payoff is NOT bounded at benchmark scale here. Section 9
states the exact remaining scalar energy estimate and its exact original-B
comparison. Section 10 explains why two natural proof attempts do not give it.
In particular, the large midpoint payoff is not a terminal lower bound, and
this document does not rule the family out.

## 1. Fixed law, normalization, and accepted interfaces

Fix an integer m>=8. Write

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda*(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    t_j=1+a*c^j, 0<=j<m,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,t_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n=2^m-1, d=t_(m-1)=1+beta*c^(m-2),
    N=ceil(pi*sqrt(d/6)), theta=pi/(2N),
    mu=sin(theta)^2, z=cos(theta),
    v_i=cos((2i+1)*theta), 0<=i<N.                         (1)

All children use this SAME enclosing m. The function, vertex count, and start
are fixed before the first query. The graph has exactly N directed edges
i->i+1 modulo N, each of weight one. For x in R^N define

    (D x)_i=x_i-x_(i+1 mod N),
    F(x)=(1/8)*sum_i ((D x)_i)_+^2,
    x_0=v, x_(k+1)=x_k-h_k*grad F(x_k), h=H_m.           (2)

Here s_+=max(s,0), and vector positive parts are componentwise. The declared
smoothness constant is L=1 throughout (including the payoff). Section 2 proves
this bound and also identifies the least constant, which is 1-mu. We do NOT
recalibrate the word by that least constant. There is no vanishing global
scalar multiplying F: its coefficient is the fixed number 1/8 for all m.

Let 1 be the all-ones vector, e_i the i-th coordinate unit vector,
P=I-11^T/N, and R=dist(v,argmin F). The payoff is

    V_m=2*F(x_n)/R^2.                                   (3)

Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original W_n normalization and all-directed
residual Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.
Every such inequality is derived directly here, including both terminal
directions and all optimum rows. No terminal-reduction shortcut is used.

For the original benchmark, use accepted b98bb807d514e80d and
04219554e41d9750. In full, C_0=B_0=1 and

    Omega(s,t)=(s+t+sqrt((s+t)^2+4st))/2,
    G(s,t)=2s+t/2+sqrt(t^2+8st)/2,
    C_q=max_(0<=k<q) Omega(C_k,C_(q-k-1)),
    B_q=max_(0<=k<q) G(C_k,B_(q-k-1)), U_q^ZJ=1/B_q.     (4)

Every original split remains. The ray part of accepted04219554e41d9750 says

    lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1) -> Z_1, 0<Z_1<infinity.       (5)

Only this unconditional ray assertion is used; none of that fact's conditional
seed hypotheses is asserted to hold. Its statement and the proof's benchmark,
ray enclosure, and convergence sections were read. The prior triangle draft
89154918c70588a0 is motivation only, not a mathematical premise.

## 2. Static function, true radius, exact gap, and smoothness

Put A=D/2. The scalar function s_+^2/2 is convex and continuously
differentiable with derivative s_+. Thus

    F(x)=||(A x)_+||^2/2,
    grad F(x)=A^T(Ax)_+=D^T(Dx)_+/4.                    (6)

The elementary bound sum_i(x_i-x_(i+1))^2<=4 sum_i x_i^2 implies ||D||<=2;
the positive-part map is 1-Lipschitz. Formula (6) therefore proves that F is
convex, everywhere finite, continuously differentiable, and 1-smooth.

Its minimum is zero and its entire minimum set is span{1}. Indeed F(x)=0
means x_i<=x_(i+1) around the entire directed cycle, forcing equality everywhere.
Also 1^T grad F(x)=0, so the mean is preserved at every step, including a zero
step. Pairing i with N-1-i shows sum_i v_i=0. The identity cos^2 u=(1+cos 2u)/2
and the telescoping formula
2*sin(pi/N)*cos((2i+1)*pi/N)=sin((2i+2)*pi/N)-sin(2i*pi/N) give

    R^2=||v||^2=N/2.                                    (7)

There is no hidden choice of a more distant minimizer in this denominator.

For later use, the unweighted combinatorial Laplacian of a path on r vertices, divided by4,
has eigenvectors cos(k*pi*(i+1/2)/r), 0<=k<r, and eigenvalues
sin^2(k*pi/(2r)). This follows by substituting the cosine addition formula at
each interior vertex and the one-neighbor formula at each endpoint. Their
pairwise orthogonality follows by expressing products of cosines as sums and
using the finite geometric series; hence these r vectors form a basis.

The strict active edges of F at any x form a disjoint union of paths, since
all edges of a directed cycle cannot have strictly positive differences. If
H_x=(1/4)*sum_(i:(Dx)_i>0) (e_i-e_(i+1))(e_i-e_(i+1))^T, then

    grad F(x)=H_x x, 2F(x)=x^T H_x x.                    (8)

Every path component has at most N vertices. Its largest eigenvalue is at
most cos^2(pi/(2N))=1-mu. Along a segment, (6) is continuous and affine on
finitely many subsegments; on each its derivative is such a path Hessian.
Integrating along the segment proves that grad F is (1-mu)-Lipschitz.
At the strictly decreasing point v all path edges0,...,N-2 are active and the
closing edge is inactive. Perturbing v a sufficiently small amount in the top
path eigenvector keeps this strict sector. The derivative attains norm1-mu
there. Consequently the LEAST smoothness constant is exactly1-mu.

The exact radial energy gap on the mean-zero space is

    min_(x !=0, sum x_i=0) 2F(x)/||x||^2 = mu.           (9)

Here is a direct proof that does not confuse the gap with strong convexity.
Minimize 2F on the compact mean-zero unit sphere. Its minimum rho is strictly
positive because the only zeroes of F are constants. The multiplier equation
and sum grad F=0 give grad F(x)=rho*x at a minimizer: the multiplier on 1 is
zero, and taking the inner product with x uses <grad F(x),x>=2F(x).
By (8), rho is a positive eigenvalue of a path-component Hessian. The path
spectrum above puts every such positive eigenvalue at least mu. Conversely,
v is strictly decreasing and is the lowest positive cosine eigenvector of
the N-vertex path, so grad F(v)=mu*v and 2F(v)=mu*||v||^2.

Thus (9) is exact, but some sector Hessians have large kernels. In particular
no claim of globally mu-strong convexity is made.

A useful elementary upper value bound is

    sum_i ((Dx)_i)_+^2 <=2||Px||^2, hence 2F(x)<=||Px||^2/2. (10)

To prove it, replace x by Px and partition strict active edges into maximal
decreasing runs. On a run with endpoint values b>=e, the sum of the squared
nonnegative drops is at most (b-e)^2<=2(b^2+e^2). Different runs have disjoint
vertex sets, since at least one inactive edge separates them. Summing yields
(10). Isolated vertices add only nonnegative norm to the right side.

The smoothness constant stays near one while the relevant radial gap mu tends
to zero. Already T_t(v)=(1-t*mu)*v for 0<=t<1/mu shows that no strict norm
contraction independent of N can be transported from the triangle family.

## 3. All original directed rows with the non-isometric incidence

For ANY two points x_i,x_j define y_i=D x_i, r_i=(y_i)_+, and Delta r=r_i-r_j.
Their actual value and gradient residual is exactly

    Q_ij=-(1/4)*sum_e r_(i,e)*(-y_(j,e))_+
         -(1/32)*sum_e (Delta r_e+Delta r_(e+1))^2 <=0,  (11)

with indices modulo N. No condition on chronological proximity or sector is
present, so this includes nonlocal, optimum, and terminal rows.

Proof: for phi(s)=s_+^2/2 a direct check of the four sign combinations gives

    phi(u)-phi(w)+u_+*(w-u)+(u_+-w_+)^2/2=-u_+*(-w)_+.

Sum this at u=(Ax_i)_e,w=(Ax_j)_e and then correct the gradient norm from
||Delta p||^2 to ||A^T Delta p||^2, where p_i=(Ax_i)_+=r_i/2. This yields

    Q_ij=-<p_i,(-Ax_j)_+>
          -(1/2)<Delta p,(I-AA^T)Delta p>.

Here AA^T is the CYCLE Laplacian divided by4, not a projection. For every w,

    <w,(I-AA^T)w>=(1/4)*sum_e(w_e+w_(e+1))^2.

Substitute Delta p=Delta r/2 to obtain (11). This also proves the required
positive semidefiniteness without using an isometric incidence identity.
Ties give zero positive parts and cause no exception.

## 4. The rounding law and the exact first child

The following elementary estimates hold for every m>=8:

    d>144, mu<1/96, 5/4<mu*d<3/2,
    mu*t_j<2/3 for every j<=m-2.                         (12)

First lambda>12/5, beta>=7/8, and (12/5)^3=1728/125>13 give
d>1+(7/8)*13^2>144. Put b=pi*sqrt(d/6). Since sqrt(6)<pi and sqrt(d)>12,
1/b<1/12 and b<=N<b+1<(13/12)b. Thus
theta<=sqrt(6)/(2sqrt(d))<1/8 and theta^2<1/96.
Integration of cos u>=1-u^2/2 gives sin(theta)/theta>=1-theta^2/6>99/100.
It follows that

    mu*d < pi^2*d/(4N^2) <=3/2,
    mu*d >(3/2)*(12/13)^2*(99/100)^2 >5/4.

For the last strict comparison, the numerator216*9801=2117016 exceeds
(5/4)*1690000=2112500. Finally

    t_(m-2)/d =1/c+(1-1/c)/d <5/12+1/144=61/144,
    mu*t_(m-2)<61/96<2/3.

On the positive ray of v, grad F(qv)=q*mu*v for q>=0. By (12), each factor
1-mu*t_j in the first child is positive. Induction over the ACTUAL physical
steps, not a rearranged nonlinear trajectory, gives

    x_(2^(m-1)-1)=A_m*v,
    A_m=product_(j=0)^(m-2)(1-mu*t_j)^(2^(m-2-j))>0.    (13)

The multiplicity comes from the original recursive concatenation. Regrouping
these scalar factors is justified only because the entire first child has
just been proved to stay in this one sector.

For completeness A_m has a uniform positive lower bound. Let
S=sum H_(m-1). Summing the physical multiplicities gives

    S=2^(m-1)-1+[a/(c-2)]*(c^(m-1)-2^(m-1)).            (14)

We have 0<a/(c-2)<1 because c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta.
Also d>2^(m-1): the lower bound (7/8)*(12/5)^(m-2) exceeds2^(m-1) at m=8
(using13^2), and its ratio to2^(m-1) increases by6/5 with each increment of m.
Consequently

    S/d <1/(c-2)+1 <lambda+1 <7/2,
    mu*S<21/4.

Since -log(1-u)=integral_0^u(1-s)^(-1) ds<=3u for0<=u<=2/3,

    exp(-63/4)<A_m<=1.                                  (15)

This bound is only for the first child. It is not a suffix or terminal bound.

## 5. First nonlocal cycle and the central sign reversal

The first three queried points of the ORIGINAL run are alpha_i*v with

    alpha_0=1, alpha_1=1-mu*t_0,
    alpha_2=(1-mu*t_1)*(1-mu*t_0).

All three alpha_i are positive and distinct. Their original residuals are

    Q_ij=-(mu*(1-mu)/2)*(N/2)*(alpha_i-alpha_j)^2.        (16)

This follows by substituting F(alpha v)=mu*alpha^2*N/4 and grad F(alpha v)=
mu*alpha*v into the definition of Q. In particular the first three-query
nonlocal cycle has

    Q_01+Q_12+Q_20
      =-mu*(1-mu)*N/4*[(alpha_0-alpha_1)^2
                         +(alpha_1-alpha_2)^2+(alpha_2-alpha_0)^2]<0.

All other directed cycles are already covered by (11).

At the unique largest step, the still-positive point in (13) is updated by
the path Hessian. Equations (12)--(13) give the EXACT post-central point

    x_(2^(m-1))=-b_m*v,
    b_m=A_m*(mu*d-1), A_m/4<b_m<A_m/2.                  (17)

All former path differences are now negative. Only the closing edge is
positive, with value2*b_m*z. Thus

    2F(x_(2^(m-1)))/R^2=2*b_m^2*z^2/N.                 (18)

This is the actual MIDPOINT payoff, not the terminal one. In particular the
quadratic eigenmode is no longer invariant: grad F(-v) has support only at
vertices0,N-1, whereas -mu*v has nonzero interior coordinates for N>3.

## 6. First two changing-sector suffix steps

Positive homogeneity allows the common amplitude b_m to be factored out;
this does NOT reset a state. Define X_0=-v and let X_k follow the entire
physical suffix H_(m-1) using the SAME enclosing m. Then the actual original
state at the corresponding suffix index is b_m*X_k.

Set u=t_0/4, w=t_1/4. We have1/4<u<3/8 and15/32<=w<1/2. At X_0 only
edge N-1 is positive, of value2z. At X_1 the exact positive differences are

    r_(1,N-1)=q=2z*(1-2u)>0,
    r_(1,0)=r_(1,N-2)=p=z*(2u-4mu)>0.                   (19)

All other differences remain their strictly negative values from -v. For
example, the newly activated edge0 has difference
cos(3theta)-(1-2u)*cos(theta)=z*(2u-4mu), by the triple-angle identity.
The inequality mu<1/96 gives p/z>11/24.

At X_2 the three previously active edge values are

    r_(2,N-1)=(1-2w)*q+2w*p>0,
    r_(2,0)=r_(2,N-2)=(1-2w)*p+w*q>0.                  (20)

The next two edges become active with equal value

    r_(2,1)=r_(2,N-3)=w*p-8mu*z*(1-2mu)>0.             (21)

Indeed cos(3theta)-cos(5theta)=8mu*z*(1-2mu), while
w*p/z>(15/32)*(11/24)=165/768>1/12>8mu*(1-2mu).
Every other interior difference is unchanged and negative. N>12 here, so
the named edges and the unaffected interior are distinct.

Let L_i=(e_i-e_(i+1))(e_i-e_(i+1))^T with cyclic indexing. The first two
suffix Hessians are

    H^(0)=L_(N-1)/4,
    H^(1)=(L_(N-1)+L_0+L_(N-2))/4.                    (22)

They do not commute. Applying their commutator to e_1 gives

    (H^(0)H^(1)-H^(1)H^(0))*e_1=(e_(N-1)-e_0)/16 !=0.

This is an actual sequence of strict sectors, not an assumption about which
Hessians might occur. The second suffix step enlarges the active path again.

For a nonlocal cycle crossing these first suffix transitions, (11) gives a
strict Q_(X1,X0): the positive edge0 at X_1 has value p, while its value at
X_0 is -4mu*z, so its first summand is -p*mu*z<0. Since all other directed
residuals are nonpositive, Q_(X1,X0)+Q_(X0,X2)+Q_(X2,X1)<0.

## 7. Exact chronology for all depths, including ties and zero steps

For an arbitrary nonnegative step t let

    T_t(x)=x-(t/4)*D^T(Dx)_+,
    E_t(y)=y-(t/4)*D D^T y_+.

Thus in edge coordinates the exact recursion is

    y'_(i)=y_i-(t/4)*(2(y_i)_+-(y_(i-1))_+-(y_(i+1))_+). (23)

It preserves sum y_i=0. The formula uses positive parts at each ACTUAL state;
no simultaneous diagonalization or time-homogeneous linear semigroup is
asserted. At a tie the positive part is zero, and at t=0 the map is the identity;
the query and all interpolation rows remain present.

For fixed m define the maps for whole child words by

    Phi_0=identity, Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j o T_(t_j) o Phi_j,
    Psi_0=identity, Psi_(j+1)=Psi_j o E_(t_j) o Psi_j.    (24)

Composition is read from right to left. These expressions apply the first
child, then its central step, then the second child to the OUTPUT of the first
two pieces. They neither change m nor reset x or y at a boundary.

Consequently the original terminal state and its exact payoff are

    x_n=b_m*Phi_(m-1)(-v),
    Y_m=Psi_(m-1)(-Dv)=D Phi_(m-1)(-v),
    E_m=|| (Y_m)_+ ||^2,
    V_m=b_m^2*E_m/(2N).                                (25)

Here E_m denotes a scalar terminal edge energy, not the one-step map E_t.
Equation (25) is an exact all-depth formula for the actual trajectory. Merely
writing it down is not the missing terminal estimate.

One actual but nondecisive upper bound follows directly from (23). Put
M(y)=sum_i(y_i)_+ and

    P_m=product_(j=2)^(m-2) (t_j/2)^(2^(m-2-j)).

For0<=t<=2, (23) is a sum of nonnegative multiples of neighboring positive
parts minus y_-, and M(y')<=M(y). For t>2, discard the negative central
coefficient1-t/2 to get M(y')<=(t/2)M(y). Here t_0<3/2, t_1<2, and
t_j>=t_2=1+lambda*(1-1/m^2)>3 for j>=2 and m>=8. Since M(-Dv)=2z,

    0<=E_m<=M(Y_m)^2<=4z^2*P_m^2,
    0<=V_m<=2*b_m^2*z^2*P_m^2/N.                        (26)

This upper bound allows enormous growth. It proves neither a useful family
obstruction nor a benchmark-scale positive lower bound. It is included to
distinguish a proved actual terminal bound from the unproved desired estimate.

## 8. Exact asymptotic scale of the retained incoming amplitude

The fixed dimension law and c=lambda(1+1/m) give

    d/lambda^m -> e/lambda^2,
    N^2/lambda^m -> pi^2*e/(6lambda^2),
    mu*d ->3/2.                                        (27)

Here e=exp(1). For the first limit, (1-1/m)(1+1/m)^(m-2)->e;
this follows by bounding log(1+s) between s-s^2/2 and s for small positive s.
The additive1 in d and the ceiling error in N vanish relative to d and sqrt d.
The limit sin(theta)/theta->1 proves the third limit.

Reindex r=m-1-j in (13):

    A_m=product_(r=1)^(m-1)
          [1-mu-(mu*d-mu)*c^(-r)]^(2^(r-1)).

Then

    A_m -> A_infty
      =product_(r=1)^infinity [1-(3/2)*lambda^(-r)]^(2^(r-1))>0,
    b_m -> b_infty=A_infty/2>0.                         (28)

To justify convergence of the product, its logarithmic summands are bounded
in absolute value by3*2^(r-1)*[mu+(3/2)*lambda^(-r)], using (12). For an
integer cutoff r_cut and r>r_cut, the sum of the second term tends to zero
uniformly as r_cut->infinity because
2/lambda<1. The first term over ALL r is at most3*mu*2^(m-1), which tends
to zero by (27). Each finite set of summands converges by (27). The limiting
series is absolutely convergent, and each factor is strictly positive since
(3/2)/lambda<1. This proves (28), including positivity, without computation.

Equations (18), (27), and (28) show that the midpoint payoff has exact order
1/N. Combined with (5), its ratio to the ORIGINAL terminal benchmark diverges
like a positive constant times N. That observation identifies energy available
at the reversal. It gives no right to transport it through the suffix.

## 9. The precise remaining nonlocal estimate and original-B comparison

Define the fully specified nonnegative number

    J_m=N*|| [Psi_(m-1)(-Dv)]_+ ||^2 = N*E_m.           (29)

There are no free parameters, omitted rows, adjustable amplitudes, or altered
children in (29). The exact terminal comparison is

    V_m/U_(2^m-1)^ZJ = b_m^2*B_(2^m-1)*J_m/(2N^2).    (30)

The coefficient in front of J_m has the positive finite limit

    K_cycle=3lambda^2*b_infty^2*Z_1/(pi^2*e)
           =3lambda^2*A_infty^2*Z_1/(4pi^2*e).          (31)

Hence the following are precise conditional bridges, not proved conclusions
about J_m:

* If liminf J_m>0, this one law gives a fixed positive multiple of the original
  benchmark on H_m.
* It gives the asymptotic lower bound (1-o(1))/B_(2^m-1) exactly when
  liminf J_m>=1/K_cycle.
* Its original-B ratio tends to zero exactly when J_m tends to zero.
* A claimed limiting constant requires an actual limit for J_m; none is proved.

Equivalence in the second bullet means the usual eventual asymptotic lower
inequality, or equivalently liminf of the payoff ratio at least1. All these
statements follow from (30) and convergence of its positive coefficient; no
asymptotic balanced approximation to B has replaced the all-split recurrence.

The exact open analytical task is to estimate (29) for the chronological
positive-part recurrence (23) with the word (24). Even a proof of
liminf J_m>0 is missing. The radial gap, the first-child product, legality,
and the positive midpoint amplitude do not imply it. Success for (29) would
still concern H_m only, not the original theorem over every nonnegative word.

## 10. Two direct attempts and concrete shortcut failures

Attempt A: extend the scalar low-eigenvalue product through the right child.
This fails at its first gradient query. Formula (17) changes the active path
to the single closing edge; grad F(-v) is supported at two endpoints and is
not -mu*v. Equations (19)--(22) further show actual overlap and noncommutation.
Thus the continued polynomial product is not the selected function's GD.

Even replacing the product by a sum-of-steps diffusion law loses actual order.
Starting at X=-v, either first step t_0 or t_1 yields exactly the same strict
three-edge sector in (22). Both lie below2 and exceed8mu. Writing s=t_0,t=t_1,
the two two-step compositions have difference

    T_t T_s X - T_s T_t X=(t-s)*(H^(0)-H^(1))*X !=0.     (32)

Indeed expand (I-tH^(1))(I-sH^(0))X and the reversed expression; the st term
cancels. The two omitted edge differences of X both equal -4mu*z, so
(H^(0)-H^(1))X is nonzero; t_1>t_0. This comparison refutes an order-free
shortcut on the same static function. It is not a second proposed run or a
change to the specified word.

Attempt B: use the exact radial gap and small-step dissipation to retain the
post-central energy. Equation (23) has nonnegative positive-part coefficients
only when t<=2. The actual level2 step is t_2=1+lambda*(1-1/m^2)>3, and later
levels are larger. The positive-mass argument yields only the expanding
factor t/2 in (26); it cannot give the needed lower bound on J_m.

The absence of a lower norm bound from the small radial gap is concrete.
On this SAME directed cycle consider the mean-zero vector w=e_(N-1)-e_0.
Only its closing edge is positive, so grad F(w)=w/2. The actual level1 step
t_1=2-1/m therefore gives

    T_(t_1)(w)=w/(2m), F(T_(t_1)w)=F(w)/(4m^2).         (33)

Meanwhile the graph's exact gap mu tends to zero exponentially. Thus gap
information alone cannot prevent strong damping of a possible edge-localized
component. This test is a counterexample to the generic proposed inference,
not an assertion that the specified suffix equals w.

The telescoping identity also keeps a signed overstep contribution:

    ||X_(k+1)||^2-||X_k||^2
      =-4h_k F(X_k)+h_k^2||grad F(X_k)||^2.             (34)

For steps above2 the smoothness bound does not turn (34) into a usable
lower bound on terminal energy. Continuous-time diffusion results or a
stable explicit discretization cannot be substituted for these large steps.

No counterexample to the actual statement liminf J_m>0 has been obtained.
Attempts A and B fail as proof routes; the selected family remains open.

## 11. Literature depth and scope

Matlas was queried for convex directed squared-positive-part graph energies
and their dynamics. Its returned theorems concerned different consensus or
optimization systems and supplied no applicable suffix theorem. Web search
located the exact primary paper:

Rasoul Shafipour, Ali Khodabakhsh, Gonzalo Mateos, Evdokia Nikolova,
*A Directed Graph Fourier Transform with Spread Frequency Components*,
arXiv:1804.03000v2 (2018), https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.03000v2 .

The PDF was downloaded to downloads/1804.03000v2.pdf and read through the web
tool's PDF text extraction. Section II-A equation(2) defines directed variation
as sum_ij A_ij*(x_i-x_j)_+^2 with nonnegative weights; its directed edge
convention agrees with D above. Proposition1's directed-path and directed-cycle
maximum-variation claims and their Appendix B--C proofs were read, as was the
underlying-undirected-graph comparison in Proposition2 and its proof.
This supplied context for the energy and the decreasing-run argument.
The paper studies variation and basis construction, not the chronological
large-step suffix (23). No external theorem is imported as a premise: (2),
(6), (9)--(11), and all dynamics are rederived in this joint draft.

## 12. Handoff

The required all-depth benchmark-scale terminal estimate is the unmet part of
this assignment. The exact selected law has been tested beyond generic setup:
its full first half, first nonlocal cycle, central sign reversal, first two
active-set transitions, true radius, non-isometric legality, amplitude limits,
and exact original-B interface are given above. A finite actual terminal upper
bound is proved but is nondecisive. No paid benchmark-scale terminal lower law,
family impossibility, full target theorem, or new verified fact is claimed.

This ends the one allocated analytical round. A later continuation would need
a new TASK and resource authorization, and should target (29), not repeat the
triangle family or assume an invariant quadratic mode after (17).
