# Three-step capacity with arbitrary signed terminal credit

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No SDP/PEP or
fact_submit call. This closes the proposed three-step discovery domain if
the candidate survives verification. It does not settle the general signed
cone question or the original all-horizon normalized objective target.

## 1. Statement and definitions

Let h=(u,v,w) be a nonnegative three-step schedule, let T=1+u+v+w, and put
t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, T_0=lambda^2=3+2t. Along gradient descent on a convex
differentiable function f with 1-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer, write
x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), and

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)+sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                             -(T-1)T||g_3||^2,
 J_h=sum_(i<3)h_i Q_(i,3), E_theta=V_h-theta J_h.

Claim. If T>=T_0 and h differs from (t,2,t), there is a fixed, finite-dimensional
convex differentiable 1-smooth function, with a minimizer, whose full h-run has

              V_h<0 and Q_(i,3)=0 for every i<3.       (1)

Consequently, for EVERY real theta, universal validity of E_theta>=0 with
T>=T_0 forces h=(t,2,t). The same necessary conclusion holds for any intrinsic
certificate V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij whose values cancel, whose remainder is PSD
on the complete independent formal gradient space, and whose multipliers are
nonnegative except for arbitrary signs on incoming-terminal finite pairs.
No validity-versus-certifiability equivalence is assumed.

At h=(t,2,t), an explicit ordinary primitive certificate is given in Section 7.
Therefore, for each theta>=0, the exact maximum three-step capacity in the
E_theta-valid class and in its nonnegative-certificate class is T_0, uniquely
attained by Silver. The arbitrary-signed-certificate class has that same
maximum. In particular the conclusion applies throughout 0<=theta<1.

The proof includes the required domain reductions directly. C32 and earlier
max4 findings motivated them, but are not used as unverified premises.

## 2. Legal projection witnesses and the first four constraints

Here is a self-contained way to turn each finite array below into a fixed
legal function. Suppose its GD equations hold, its terminal point satisfies
x_3=g_3, and its assigned values are

 f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

Set y_i=x_i-g_i. Expansion gives

 Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, Q_(i,3)=0,
 V_h=||x_0||^2-T^2||g_3||^2.                         (2)

If every finite Q_ij<=0 and <g_i,y_i>>=0, take K=conv{0,g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3}.
Those inequalities say precisely <y_j,z-g_j><=0 for every vertex z of K and
hence for every z in K. Thus g_j=P_K(x_j). The function

 F_K(x)=max_(z in K)[<x,z>-||z||^2/2]

interpolates all assigned values and gradients. The projection normal
inequalities imply <P_K(x)-P_K(x'),x-x'>>=||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2, so P_K is
1-Lipschitz. The maximizing formula gives
0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),d><=<P_K(x+d)-P_K(x),d><=||d||^2,
which proves differentiability with gradient P_K. Thus F_K is globally convex
and 1-smooth; it has minimum F_K(0)=0. No unverified interpolation theorem is
needed for these arrays.

At any selected step b>1, with preceding and following sums P,Q, use derivative
clip(x,-(b-1)/(Q+1),1) and start at P+1. The preceding gradients are 1 and the
following gradients are -(b-1)/(Q+1), ending at that negative threshold.
The function obtained by integration has all the properties in (2). Its
energy yields the necessary cut inequality

              T(b-1)_+ <= (P+1)(Q+1).               (3)

For b<=1 this inequality is automatic. This includes schedules with zero
steps. The first and last cuts give

              u,w <= M(T):=2T/(T+1)<2.              (4)

For u,v,w>1 write a=u-1, A=u+1, r=v-1, D=w+1, eta=r/D.
The following additional inequalities are necessary if no witness (1) exists:

 F_Q=1-T^2 a^2 r^2(w-1)^2 >=0,                     (5)
 F_B=A^2-T^2 eta^2 >=0,                            (6)
 F_C=A^2-T^2 r^2(w-1)^2 >=0,                       (7)
 F_N=a u F_B+(1+eta)u^2(2-u^2) >=0.                (8)

For (5), use f(x)=x^2/2 and x_0=1. For (6), use the middle cut in (3),
whose x=(A,1,-r,-eta), g=(1,1,-eta,-eta). For (7), provided r(w-1)<=1,
use derivative min(x,1), start at A, and obtain
x=(A,1,-r,r(w-1)), g=(1,1,-r,r(w-1)). It is a fixed convex 1-smooth
function with minimum at0, and its terminal point is inside its quadratic
part. Each of these functions satisfies (2).

For (8), provided eta a<1, combine the middle-cut base from (6) with the
auxiliary array

 x_aux=(1,-a,ra,eta a), g_aux=(1,-a,eta a,eta a),
 f_aux,i=x_aux,i*g_aux,i-g_aux,i^2/2.

The auxiliary normals are (0,0,w eta a,0). Its only positive finite residual
is Q_aux,(0,2)=(1-eta a)w eta a. Every outgoing optimum inequality holds,
since each g_aux,i*y_aux,i is nonnegative. In the base array,
Q_base,(0,2)=-(1+eta)w eta, and all finite residuals are nonpositive.
Take their orthogonal sum with auxiliary scale sqrt(epsilon), where

                   epsilon=(1+eta)/[a(1-eta a)].

Residuals add, the sole positive auxiliary edge is canceled exactly, every
other finite edge is nonpositive, and every optimum condition holds.
The projection construction above realizes one fixed legal function. Its
energy is F_B+epsilon(1-T^2 eta^2 a^2)=F_N/[a(1-eta a)]; the denominator
is positive. The equality follows by 1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2).
The terminal point stays core, so every incoming-terminal residual is zero.

## 3. Complete reduction to the asymmetric region

Assume T>=T_0 and assume, for contradiction, that every applicable witness
above has nonnegative energy. First v>1: otherwise T<=2+2M(T) gives
T^2-5T-2<=0, whereas at T_0 this polynomial equals 2t>0 and it increases
thereafter. Thus r>0 and the middle cut applies, initially with A=u+1,D=w+1.
Since T=A+D+r, it gives

 T(T-A-D)<=AD<=(A+D)^2/4,
 T<=lambda(A+D)/2<=lambda(M(T)+1).

Hence T^2+(1-3lambda)T-lambda<=0. The polynomial increases for T>=T_0,
and its value at7 is 56-22lambda>0 because lambda<5/2. Thus T<7, and
u,w<M(7)=7/4. Both endpoints exceed1: if u<=1, then A<=2,D<11/4 and
r>T-19/4, so Tr>T(T-19/4)>=T_0(T_0-19/4)=11/4+5t/2>11/2>AD,
a contradiction. The proof for w is identical.

The extra hypotheses needed in Section 2 now hold everywhere in this box:

 r(w-1)<=A(w^2-1)/T<363/(64T_0)<1,
 eta a<=Aa/T=(u^2-1)/T<33/(16T_0)<1.                (9)

For the first numerical inequality, 363<64T_0 follows from
171<128sqrt(2), whose square is 29241<32768. Also

                   u+w>=2T/lambda-2>=2t.            (10)

If u<=t, then w>=t. The left-minus-right difference in the necessary
inequality Tr(w-1)<=A from (7) is at least
T_0(T_0-t-w-2)(w-1)-(t+1)=T_0(w-t)(2-w)>=0.
It is positive except when u=w=t,T=T_0; equality then gives v=2.
Thus every nonexceptional candidate has u>t.

If w>=t as well, consider q_T(u,w)=T(u-1)(w-1)(T-u-w-2) on
[t,M(T)]^2. It is strictly concave in each endpoint separately. Its minimum
is at a corner; strict concavity excludes equality at an interior endpoint
when corner minima have already been identified. At T_0, since
M(T_0)=1+t/2, the four corner values are 1,lambda/2,lambda/2,lambda/2,
with the value1 only at (t,t). All four corner values strictly increase with
T>=T_0: their factors are positive and increasing, including
T-t-M(T)-2 and T-2M(T)-2, whose derivatives are respectively
1-2/(T+1)^2 and 1-4/(T+1)^2. Thus (5) excludes this case except Silver.
Every other candidate has u>t>w.

For such endpoints, F_N strictly decreases as r>0 increases, with u,w fixed:
the negative squared term in F_B decreases and u^2(2-u^2)<0. At r=1,

 D^2 F_N/u=u^2 D^2+(4-2u^2-u^3)D-(u-1)(u+2)^2=:P_u(D).

This quadratic has one positive and one negative root. At D=lambda,
P_u(lambda)=-(lambda+1)u^3-2u^2+4(lambda+1)<0 for u>t: it vanishes
at t and has negative derivative in u>0. Its positive root is therefore
beyond lambda. Since D<lambda, F_N<0 when r=1, and then also for r>=1.
Necessarily r<1. We have proved, with every witness hypothesis checked,

       t<u<7/4, 1<v<2, 1<w<t, T_0<=T<7,
                       u+w>=2T/lambda-2.             (11)

## 4. The additional two-cut witness forces u<8/5

Here the different letters R=u-1, k=v-1, C=w+1, B=v+w+1=C+k+1 are used
to avoid confusing the first and second bridge. Thus T=B+R+1.
Set eta_1=R/B, zeta=k/C, p=-1/R. Consider the two scalar arrays

 x_base=(1,-R,-C eta_1,-eta_1),
 g_base=(1,-eta_1,-eta_1,-eta_1),
 x_aux=(p,1,-k,-zeta), g_aux=(p,1,-zeta,-zeta).

Both obey the same three GD steps; assign values by f=g*x-g^2/2.
Their normal arrays are respectively
(0,-(B-1)eta_1,-w eta_1,0) and (0,0,-w zeta,0).
The base is an actual clipped-derivative trajectory. Within (11),
R zeta<3/8<1. The auxiliary array's only positive residual is
Q_aux,(0,2)=w zeta(1/R-zeta); all its other finite residuals are zero
except Q_aux,(1,2)<0. Every optimum inequality holds in both arrays.
The base residual Q_base,(0,2)=-(1+eta_1)w eta_1 cancels that positive
edge when the orthogonal auxiliary scale is sqrt(epsilon_1), where

             epsilon_1=eta_1(1+eta_1)/[zeta(1/R-zeta)].

All other finite inequalities remain nonpositive. The projection realization
in Section 2 supplies a fixed globally legal function; its terminal point is
core and every incoming-terminal Q is zero. Its energy is

 V_mix=1-T^2 eta_1^2+epsilon_1(R^(-2)-T^2 zeta^2)
      =(C+kB)(B+R)/[B^2 k(C-Rk)]
                     *{C-Rk[1+R(T+1)]}.             (12)

The prefactor is positive. For an algebra audit, substitute
epsilon_1=R^2 C^2(B+R)/[B^2 k(C-Rk)], put the two energy terms over
that denominator, and use B=C+k+1,T=B+R+1. Thus nonnegative energy forces

                    R k[1+R(T+1)]<=C.              (13)

In (11), w<t and T>=T_0 imply
k=T-2-u-w>1+t-u=t-R, C<lambda. Therefore (13) implies

            R(t-R)[1+(T_0+1)R]<lambda.              (14)

The function H(R)=R(t-R)(1+(T_0+1)R) strictly increases on
t-1<R<3/4. Indeed its derivative is
t-2R+(T_0+1)R(2t-3R); here t-2R>-1/10, T_0+1>6,
R>2/5 and 2t-3R>1/2, so H'(R)>11/10>0. These bounds follow from
7/5<t<3/2. At R=3/5,

           H(3/5)=(201t+27)/125>1+t,

because the difference is (76t-98)/125>0. Thus (14) forces R<3/5,
or u<8/5. This is the extra information absent from the four-test screen.

## 5. The quadratic witness forces a small imbalance

Fix the u,w now under consideration and reduce the formal capacity to T_0,
that is, put v_0=T_0-1-u-w. Write

 q=t-1, delta=u-t, z=t-w, d=delta-z=u+w-2t.

By (10), (11), and Section 4,

       0<delta<8/5-t<1/5, 0<z<=delta,
                   0<=d<delta, v_0=2-d>1.           (15)

For fixed u,w, the product T(u-1)(w-1)(T-u-w-2) increases with T when
T-u-w-2>0. Thus (5) at the actual capacity implies its version at T_0.
Since T_0 q^2=1, that version is

             (q+delta)(q-delta+d)(1-d)<=q^2.

Expanding and rearranging gives

 d[(q+delta)(1-d)-q^2+delta^2]<=delta^2.

Because d<=delta, the bracket is at least (1-q)(q+delta)>0. Hence

 d/delta <= delta/[(1-q)(q+delta)] < 4/7 < 3/5.     (16)

For the strict bound, the fraction increases with delta, so delta<1/5
reduces it to 1/[(1-q)(5q+1)]. Since 2/5<q<1/2,
(1-q)(5q+1)>=7/4: subtracting 7/4 gives
(1/2-q)(5q-3/2)>=0. This proves (16) by rational bounds alone.

## 6. The mixed witness contradicts that small imbalance

For fixed u,w and u>t, write eta=(T-u-w-2)/(w+1). An exact expression is

 F_N/u = u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-(u-1)T^2 eta^2.       (17)

It strictly decreases with T in the relevant range, because eta increases,
u(2-u^2)<0, u-1>0, and eta>0. Thus F_N>=0 at the actual T would imply
F_N>=0 at T_0. At T_0, hold u=t+delta fixed and vary d; then
w=t-delta+d and eta=(1-d)/(lambda-delta+d). This eta strictly decreases
with d, while the right side of (17) strictly decreases with eta. Thus
F_N/u at T_0 strictly increases with d.

By (16), it suffices to show negativity at the LARGER comparison value
d=3delta/5, that is, w=t-2delta/5 and v_0=2-3delta/5.
Let D=w+1,A=u+1,r=v_0-1 at this comparison point. The numerator

 N(delta)=D^2 F_N/u
         =(u-1)(A^2 D^2-T_0^2 r^2)
                              +(T_0-A)D u(2-u^2)

expands exactly to

 N(delta)=delta[ -4-18t/5
          +(376+294t)delta/25
          -(128+70t)delta^2/25
          +(29-3t)delta^3/25
          -6delta^4/25 ].                           (18)

For 0<delta<1/5, discard the two negative higher-order terms and use
delta<=1/5, delta^3<=1/125, and 29-3t<29. The bracket is at most

 -4-18t/5+(376+294t)/125+29/3125
                   =-(3071+3900t)/3125<0.           (19)

Thus N(delta)<0, contradicting the required nonnegative F_N at T_0.
This eliminates every candidate in (11). Consequently the original assumption
that all the listed legal witnesses have nonnegative energy is impossible
for T>=T_0 outside Silver. A negative witness from this finite explicit list
therefore supplies (1), with its whole trajectory and all terminal slacks.

## 7. Attainment and precise consequences

At h=(t,2,t), use the following nonnegative finite-pair multipliers:

 lambda_01=2, lambda_10=2lambda,
 lambda_21=2t lambda, lambda_31=lambda_13=2lambda,
 lambda_23=2lambda^2+2t lambda, lambda_32=2lambda^3,

with every unlisted entry zero. Expansion of the four value coefficients
and ten independent gradient monomials, using t^2=2, gives

                       V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0.

Hence V_h>=0 on every legal trajectory. For theta>=0, adding theta h_i
to every incoming-terminal multiplier gives a nonnegative certificate for
E_theta with the same zero remainder. This proves all the attainment claims.

If a putative arbitrary-signed incoming-terminal certificate exists for a
non-Silver h with T>=T_0, evaluate it on (1): the signed terms vanish, all
other multiplier terms are nonpositive, and the PSD remainder would be
at most V_h<0. This is a contradiction on the full formal matrix.

The result is an exact three-step capacity statement, not a claim that
E_theta-validity always forces ordinary primitivity below this capacity or
for longer schedules. It supplies no new upper schedule and no asymptotic
comparison with U_n^ZJ. All mathematics in this note remains unverified.

# Actual finite Gibbs functions: three analytical screens

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max, round51,
under TASK.md and master_guidance a935cdbe3d2539bd. This is the ONE
allocated round after completed50/attempted51, with completed ceiling51
and attempted ceiling52. No fact_submit or formal verification is
authorized or performed. There is no self-renewal.

All new mathematics below is a joint candidate argument, not a fact.
No unverified earlier finding is a mathematical premise. No host
mathematics, numerical evaluation, solver, DP, machine algebra, trajectory
replay, consult, installation, or native subagent was used.

## Verdict and scope

Three explicit finite feature geometries were screened.

1. A centered three-feature simplex gives an actual nonquadratic Gibbs
   function, a finite unique minimizer, sharp smoothness, a paid initial
   radius, and actual noncollinear gradients. Its selected concentrated
   family is strictly below the SAME-word Huber reference. Its original
   H_m benchmark ratio tends to Z_1/(2 exp(1))<1. This regime is closed.
2. A coupled log-cosh chain gives another actual finite Gibbs function,
   an attained minimum, exact radius, and exact finite propagation under
   arbitrary physical steps. Its untouched-coordinate lower certificate
   is at most O(n^-2). Only that certificate route is too weak. An upper
   bound on the ACTUAL terminal error of the chain is NOT proved.
3. A growing tree simplex gives a legal static function on every original
   H_m, with sharp global smoothness, unique minimum, exact initial radius,
   every physical query, and a nonlinear coupled probability recurrence.
   Its target payoff is BLOCKED at an explicitly priced terminal entropy
   inequality. It is not a prescribed-label transcript or a feature-fit
   oracle. Neither successful concentration nor the desired payoff is
   asserted. Two proposed simplifications of its dynamics fail exactly.

The original every-n/every-h exact-profile target remains OPEN. Even a
successful H_m entropy bound would be component progress, not the required
uniform bridge. The new tree does not repair the invalid transcript in
88f3db7b0a2bb506 by assigning new values to its old vectors: here values,
gradients and queries all come from a specified finite static function.

## 1. Original word, normalization, and accepted reference scopes

Fix m>=8 and put

 lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
 N=2^m, n=N-1, h=H_m, S=sum_i h_i, M=1+S.

Every child uses these SAME enclosing-m parameters. The first physical
step satisfies 1<h_0=1+a<3/2. Counting occurrences of each level gives

 M=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)
   =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta*c^m,
 zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in (0,1).                         (1)

In particular M<c^m and M/c^m tends to1, since zeta tends to1 and
(2/c)^m tends to0. Also c^m/lambda^m tends to exp(1).

For any base function Phi with a proved positive gradient Lipschitz
constant Lbase and minimum Phi_star, use the actual project function

 f=(L/Lbase)(Phi-Phi_star),
 x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Lbase) grad Phi(x_i),
 V=2(Phi(x_n)-Phi_star)/(Lbase*R_init^2),              (2)

where R_init is the distance to the WHOLE minimizer set. Thus f has
L-Lipschitz gradient and uses exactly the original steps h_i/L. The
quantity V is a single actual-instance score in the W=2R convention.

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d supplies the complete functional

 K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),
   max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/
       [(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2(1+2sum_(i>j)h_i)]},

and the original ALL-split recurrence C_0=B_0=1,

 C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
 B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
 Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
 G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
 U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3).                 (3)

Accepted 1fca3e5dd555cb4a defines F_ray_n as the maximum over ALL feasible
chronological alternating-ray checkpoint choices, with its original
retained offsets, areas and terminal optimization. In particular

 Huber(h):=1/(2M-1) <= K_n(h) <= F_ray_n(h).          (4)

Accepted dfde2fa326c908bf defines Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t*z),
xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z*Poly_i(z), with a zero optimum sample. Its FULL
domain D_h imposes

 (Re(z)-|z|^2)*Energy_C >= 2*|Im(z)*Area_C|

on EVERY directed simple cycle C of {star,0,...,n}, where
Energy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2 and
Area_C=sum_(j->i in C)Im(conj(xi_j)*xi_i). Define the comparison

 Cfull(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z),                   (5)

where Gap_h is the maximal legal terminal score for those specified
points and gradients in the accepted fact. This is the full unaugmented
channel, not its universal lens and not a short-cycle screen.

One lower bound on this COMPLETE channel will suffice for several
comparisons. Set theta=1/(2M). It is real in (0,1), hence in D_h. The
actual scalar quadratic theta*x^2/2 gives

 Gap_h(theta)>=theta*Poly_n(theta)^2>1/(8M).         (6)

Indeed each theta*h_i is in [0,1/2), and multiplying the inequalities
(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v gives Poly_n(theta)>=1-theta*S>1/2.
This uses the maximality of Gap_h, not a false identification of Gap_h
with a quadratic value.

Accepted 04219554e41d9750 supplies the ORIGINAL ray limit and enclosure

 B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1,
 0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=41209/10000,             (7)

where B_1=4 follows directly from (3). The proof's Section7 and the
full stated enclosure were read. No seed existence or seed consequence
from that fact is used. No new external theorem is imported.

The original problem asks whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where
W_n^*=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h). Its affirmative lower bound
requires one N_epsilon working for EVERY word at EVERY n>=N_epsilon.
Neither the diagnostic family (1) nor any result here provides that.

## 2. Direct finite-Gibbs calculations and all original rows

For an explicitly specified finite list of vectors v_s, positive weights
b_s, and temperature tau>0, put

 Phi_tau(x)=tau*log sum_s b_s*exp(<v_s,x>/tau),
 p_s(x)=b_s*exp(<v_s,x>/tau)/sum_t b_t*exp(<v_t,x>/tau),
 mu(x)=sum_s p_s(x)*v_s.

Differentiation of a finite sum gives, on the whole Euclidean space,

 grad Phi_tau=mu,
 Hess Phi_tau=(1/tau)[sum_s p_s v_s v_s^T-mu mu^T].  (8)

The quadratic form is a variance, so Phi_tau is convex. If the feature
diameter is Dfeat, its variance in any unit direction is at most
Dfeat^2/4. To prove this, for a random scalar T in [b,d], the nonnegative
quantity (T-b)(d-T) gives
Var(T)<=(d-E T)(E T-b)<=(d-b)^2/4.
Consequently Lbase=Dfeat^2/(4tau) is valid globally. One may also use
an explicitly proved sharper covariance bound in a particular geometry.

The EXACT probability update at the physical step is

 p_(i+1),s = p_i,s * exp[-h_i <v_s,mu_i>/(Lbase*tau)]
             / sum_t p_i,t*exp[-h_i <v_t,mu_i>/(Lbase*tau)]. (9)

This equation follows from the actual vector update; no label is chosen
after a step. Every weight remains strictly positive at every finite
query. All functions and recurrences in this manuscript are finite even
when their feature counts are large; none is evaluated by a host job.

Attainment must be checked separately. In the centered uniform examples
below, sum_s v_s=0 and the features span their stated ambient space.
Jensen's inequality gives Phi_1(x)>=0=Phi_1(0). Equality requires all
<v_s,x> equal; their mean is zero, so they all vanish and span implies
x=0. This proves the actual unique minimum, not an infimum at infinity.
The chain's attained minimum is computed explicitly in Section4.

Temperature is not a free normalization improvement. Choose x_star to
be a closest attained minimizer to the chosen initial point, and let
pi_s=p_s(x_star), z=(x-x_star)/tau and
Lbase=Ccov/tau, for a fixed proved covariance bound Ccov. Then

 Phi_tau(x)-Phi_tau(x_star)=tau*log sum_s pi_s exp(<v_s,z>),
 z_(i+1)=z_i-(h_i/Ccov)sum_s p_i,s v_s,
 V=2 log(sum_s pi_s exp(<v_s,z_n>))/(Ccov*||z_0||^2). (10)

Here sum_s pi_s v_s=0. The temperature cancels simultaneously from the
physical recurrence and the paid score at a fixed scaled initial state.
Changing that state, the features, weights, or dimension can still matter;
(10) does not exclude those choices or holding x_0 fixed while changing tau.

All original directed and optimum interpolation inequalities follow
from the actual function and its stated smoothness, as follows. For any
convex differentiable Lbase-smooth Phi and any x, define
k(u)=Phi(u)-<grad Phi(x),u>. It is minimized at x. Integrating its
Lipschitz gradient gives
k(y-grad k(y)/Lbase)<=k(y)-||grad k(y)||^2/(2Lbase).
Comparison with k(x) proves

 Phi(y)>=Phi(x)+<grad Phi(x),y-x>
                  +||grad Phi(y)-grad Phi(x)||^2/(2Lbase). (11)

With F_i=(Phi(x_i)-Phi_star)/Lbase and g_i=grad Phi(x_i)/Lbase,
(11) is exactly

 Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0

for EVERY ordered pair of distinct physical queries, including BOTH
terminal orientations and EVERY optimum pair. This agrees with accepted
1252b8823bb5a290. No local-to-global inference about prescribed jets is
being used. Zero steps simply repeat an actual query.

## 3. Candidate one: a genuine simplex Gibbs plateau, but below Huber

Use the two-dimensional Euclidean subspace E_A={x in R^3:sum_j x_j=0}.
Let u=(1,1,1)/3, v_j=e_j-u, and

 Phi_A(x)=log[(exp(x_1)+exp(x_2)+exp(x_3))/3].

These are three explicit uniform affine features on E_A. The minimum is
uniquely zero at0, and grad Phi_A=p-u. The feature diameter is sqrt(2),
so L_A=1/2 is valid. It is sharp: logits can make p tend to(1/2,1/2,0),
and the covariance in direction (1,-1,0)/sqrt(2) tends to1/2.

For ANY nonnegative word of total S>=4, specify BEFORE iteration

 x_0=4S*v_1+(0,1,-1),
 R_A^2=||x_0||^2=32S^2/3+2,
 x_(i+1)=x_i-2h_i*(p_i-u).                          (12)

The transverse vector is orthogonal to v_1, so the displayed radius is
the exact closest-minimizer distance. Initially x_1-x_2=4S-1 and
x_1-x_3=4S+1. At a step either difference decreases by at most 2h_i.
Hence at EVERY physical query, including the terminal,

 x_i,1-x_i,j>=2S-1 for j=2,3,
 1-p_i,1 <= eps_A:=2 exp(1-2S).                     (13)

The exact first-coordinate recurrence gives

 x_n,1=4S/3+2sum_i h_i(1-p_i,1).

Writing Phi_A(x_n) as its largest logit plus a remainder, (13) yields

 4S/3-log3 <= Phi_A(x_n)
 <=4S/3-log3+(2S+1)eps_A.                           (14)

For S>=4, (2S+1)eps_A is decreasing in S and is at most18 exp(-7).
Since exp(1)>2, this is less than9/64<1/6. Since log3>1, (14) gives

 0<Phi_A(x_n)<4S/3-5/6,
 V_A=4Phi_A(x_n)/(32S^2/3+2)<1/(2S+1).             (15)

For the final strict comparison, multiply the upper bound's numerator
16S/3-10/3 by 2S+1. The result is
32S^2/3-4S/3-10/3, strictly below32S^2/3+2.
The elementary inequality exp(1)<3, used for log3>1, follows by bounding
the exponential series tail: k!>=2^(k-1) for k>=2, strictly for k>=3.
The lower bound in (14), with log3<2, proves positivity for S>=4.

This actual trajectory is not an exactly constant-gradient ray. On the
original H_m, put t=2h_0 in(2,3), or use the weaker t<4. Initially
p_1>p_2>p_3, eps_A<1/64, and every later p_1>1-eps_A. Under (9), p_1
strictly decreases at this first step because exp(-t p_1) is strictly
smaller than the other two update factors. Meanwhile p_2-p_3 strictly
increases. To check the latter, the derivative of q(z)=z exp(-t z) on
[p_3,p_2] is at least exp(-t eps_A)(1-t eps_A)>7/8, using
exp(-v)>=1-v. The probability normalizer is at most
exp(-2(1-eps_A))+eps_A<1/4+1/64<7/8;
exp(3/2)>4 follows from its first four series terms. The mean value
theorem now proves (p'_2-p'_3)>(p_2-p_3). Thus the positive first
coordinate of p-u decreases while its positive transverse difference
increases. The first two gradients cannot be collinear.

Equations (14) also give 2S*V_A ->1 as S tends to infinity, uniformly
over the chronological word at a fixed total S. The family is a real
Gibbs realization of Huber-order performance, but (15) is a decisive
early screen. Do not polish it into a claimed improvement.

The COMPLETE same-word comparisons are

 0<V_A<Huber(h)<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h),
 V_A/Cfull(h)<8M/(2M-1)<5  for S>=4.                (16)

The second inequality compares with the full domain (5), using (6).
It is only a factor bound; it asserts NEITHER domination by nor strict
improvement over the full complex channel. No equality K=Huber or
F_ray=Huber from an unverified historical draft is imported.

For the original H_m, (1), (7), and (14) prove

 B_n V_A -> Z_1/(2 exp(1))
 <=41209/[20000 exp(1)]<41209/50000<1.              (17)

Here exp(1)>5/2 follows from its first four series terms. This is a
constant-gap failure of THIS explicit family on the diagnostic, not
an upper bound on W_n(H_m) or on other simplex initial conditions.

## 4. Candidate two: coupled finite sign features and a weak support bound

Let a_chain>0 and let D=2(n+1). There are D-1 variable coordinates.
Fix the boundary values x_0=a_chain and x_D=0 only in this section, and
define on the variables x_1,...,x_(D-1)

 Phi_B(x)=sum_(j=0)^(D-1) log cosh(x_j-x_(j+1)).     (18)

The boundary indices are spatial, not time indices. This is EXACTLY a
finite Gibbs function, not a sum of quadratic surrogates:

 Phi_B(x)=log sum_(sigma in {-1,1}^D) 2^(-D)
       exp[a_chain*sigma_0
               +sum_(j=1)^(D-1)(sigma_j-sigma_(j-1))*x_j]. (19)

Equation (19) explicitly specifies every feature and positive weight.
No enumeration is needed to define this finite sum. Its Hessian is
the path difference matrix transposed, times a diagonal matrix of
sech^2 edge differences, times the difference matrix. For any variation
z with z_0=z_D=0,

 sum_(j=0)^(D-1)(z_j-z_(j+1))^2<=4sum_(j=1)^(D-1)z_j^2.

Thus the actual function is globally convex and L_B=4 is a valid
smoothness constant. We do not claim this bound is sharp. Since the
edge differences sum to a_chain, strict convexity of log cosh and
Jensen give the UNIQUE attained minimum

 x_star,j=a_chain*(1-j/D),
 Phi_B,star=D log cosh(a_chain/D).                   (20)

Start the variable vector at zero. Its true squared distance is

 R_B^2=a_chain^2*(D-1)(2D-1)/(6D).                  (21)

The finite sum of squares in (21) follows from
sum_(j=1)^(D-1)(D-j)^2=(D-1)D(2D-1)/6; the last identity is proved
by induction using the difference of the two cubic expressions.

At each physical time i the update is exactly

 x_(i+1),j=x_i,j+(h_i/4)
     [tanh(x_i,j-1-x_i,j)-tanh(x_i,j-x_i,j+1)].      (22)

The fixed boundary values are retained at every time. Induction on i
shows x_i,j=0 for all j>i: if j>i+1 both adjacent differences in (22)
are zero, including the right boundary. This holds for all finite
nonnegative steps, however large, and keeps all queries.

Put r_hor=n+1. At the terminal, x_n,j=0 for j>=r_hor. The first
r_hor edge differences sum to a_chain; all subsequent differences
vanish. Jensen gives the actual LOWER bound

 V_B >= Aprop := [r_hor log cosh(a_chain/r_hor)
                   -D log cosh(a_chain/D)]/(2 R_B^2)>0.   (23)

The numerator is positive because k log cosh(a_chain/k) is strictly
decreasing in positive k: with u=a_chain/k, its derivative is
log cosh(u)-u tanh(u)<0, as the latter expression has derivative
-u sech^2(u)<0 and is zero at u=0.

But the CERTIFICATE Aprop itself is too small. The inequality
log cosh(u)<=u^2/2 follows by integrating tanh(u)<=u for u>=0 and
using evenness. Dropping the positive subtracted minimum in (23),

 0<Aprop<=3D/[2r_hor(D-1)(2D-1)]<=3/(2r_hor^2).    (24)

The final bound uses D-1>=r_hor and D/(2D-1)<=1. This is an upper
bound on a proved lower guarantee; it is NOT an upper bound on V_B.
No assertion that (23) is attained by the physical GD trajectory is made.

This certificate failure is not repaired by making the chain's valid
smoothness constant sharp. When D>=3, at the zero variable vector an
internal edge has zero difference and Hessian contribution
(e_1-e_2)(e_1-e_2)^T. All other edge contributions are positive
semidefinite, so every valid global smoothness constant is at least2.
Using ANY such constant instead of4 preserves finite propagation and
the same constrained value in (23), and its normalized support guarantee
is at most2*Aprop. Thus all the vanishing comparisons below persist,
with at most a factor2. The original H_m domain has D>=3.

On original H_m, r_hor=N, so the COMPLETE references obey

 Aprop/Huber <=3(c/4)^m,
 Aprop/K_n <=3(c/4)^m,
 Aprop/F_ray_n <=3(c/4)^m,
 Aprop/Cfull <12(c/4)^m,
 B_n Aprop <(15/2)(3/4)^m ->0.                      (25)

The first and fourth estimates use M<c^m and (6); the last uses (3).
For m>=8, c< (9/8)(5/2)=45/16<4, and c tends to lambda<4, so
all four reference ratios vanish. The scope is the untouched-coordinate
certificate only. The coupled chain itself is not excluded: a large
physical step could create an actual error much greater than (23).
No computational or qualitative claim about that actual error is used
to discard the weak certificate route and move to the third geometry.

## 5. Candidate three: an explicit weighted tree simplex

Keep the original enclosing m and c of Section1. To avoid collision with
the accepted denominator-ray symbols Z_b, define

 r_tree=2/c in(0,1),
 Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)r_tree^j=(1-r_tree^m)/(1-r_tree),
 w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom, 0<=j<m.                        (26)

There is one orthonormal coordinate e_v for EVERY binary word v of
length j<m, including the empty root. The dimension is N-1. For a leaf
s=(s_1,...,s_m) in {0,1}^m define the explicit feature

 a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) sqrt(w_j)*(-1)^(s_(j+1))*e_(s_1...s_j),
 Phi_T(x)=log[N^(-1)sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)].             (27)

The library has N features and all weights are 1/N. It may know m and
the entire original word. No online or dimension restriction is imposed
on competing adversaries. The tree is a choice of geometry, not a
lossless representation theorem for all smooth convex functions.

The features sum to zero and each has squared norm1. Their coordinate
rows are orthogonal: a row at depth j has N/2^j nonzero entries of
magnitude sqrt(w_j); a proper descendant row cancels separately inside
its support, and disjoint rows have zero product. Thus the feature
matrix has full row rank N-1. The centered Jensen argument from Section2
gives the unique attained minimum

                x_star=0, Phi_T,star=0.             (28)

The SAME argument also proves affine independence of the N vertices.
The only linear dependence among their columns is the all-ones vector;
a dependence with coefficients summing to zero must consequently vanish.
Equivalently, the map x -> (<a_s,x>)_s is onto the zero-mean logit space.

If two leaves first diverge at depth j, their squared feature distance is

             4w_j+2sum_(ell>j)w_ell.               (29)

Their common prefix cancels, the divergent coordinate contributes4w_j,
and their later paths use orthogonal coordinates. The quarter-distances
w_j+(1/2)sum_(ell>j)w_ell strictly decrease with j, since the difference
between consecutive ones is w_j-w_(j+1)/2>0. Therefore (8) gives the
globally valid constant

 Ltree=(1+w_0)/2.                                  (30)

It is SHARP. Choose a pair diverging at the root and prescribe equal
logits for that pair and logits smaller by T for all other leaves,
then subtract the common mean. The onto map just proved supplies an
actual point for every finite T. As T tends to infinity, its Gibbs
distribution tends to half mass on each chosen vertex. Covariance in
the difference direction tends to their squared distance divided by4,
which equals (30). This is a proof of the global supremum, not a
numeric eigenvalue calculation or an implicit feature-fitting step.

The full, actual, persistent vector and probability updates are

 x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Ltree)sum_s p_i,s*a_s,
 p_(i+1),s=p_i,s exp[-(h_i/Ltree)sum_t K_st*p_i,t]
          /sum_u p_i,u exp[-(h_i/Ltree)sum_t K_ut*p_i,t],
 K_st=<a_s,a_t>.                                    (31)

All entries of K are fixed by the explicit coordinates (27); K is not
an unknown Gram matrix. If s,t diverge at j, then
K_st=sum_(ell<j)w_ell-w_j; K_ss=1. Formula (31) defines every physical
query, not just level checkpoints. Values are Phi_T(x_i), and (11)
proves all original rows and optimum rows without separately fitting them.

## 6. A specified initial state, exact radius, and actual nonlinear reduction

Let t0=log2. Specify the complete initial point by

 x_0,(0^j)=t0/sqrt(w_j) for 0<=j<m,
 x_0,v=0 for every other internal node v.           (32)

Here 0^j is the all-left prefix of length j, including the root at j=0.
The full unique minimum is still (28), hence the TRUE squared radius is

 R_T^2=t0^2 sum_(j=0)^(m-1)1/w_j
      =t0^2*r_tree^(1-m)*Zgeom^2.                  (33)

The second equality follows from the two finite geometric sums in (26).
There is no discarded orthogonal component or convenient surrogate minimum.

Leaves can be grouped by the depth k of their first right turn,
0<=k<m, and by the all-left leaf k=m. Their uniform reference masses are

 q_k=2^(-(k+1)) for k<m, q_m=2^(-m).

All coordinates outside the leftmost spine remain ZERO at every physical
query. Indeed, if they are zero, logits are constant on each first-exit
group. Every non-spine node lies inside one such group, and its two
halves have equal counts and logits, so its mean feature coordinate is
zero. The next update preserves zero, completing induction.

Define the ACTUAL spine fields y_i,j=sqrt(w_j)*x_i,(0^j), exit logits

 ell_i,k=sum_(j<k)y_i,j-y_i,k for k<m,
 ell_i,m=sum_(j<m)y_i,j,
 P_i,k=q_k exp(ell_i,k)/sum_(u=0)^m q_u exp(ell_i,u).

The exact reduction of (31) is

 y_(i+1),j=y_i,j-(h_i*w_j/Ltree)
                    [sum_(k>j)P_i,k-P_i,j],
 y_0,j=t0.                                         (34)

These m equations are NONLINEAR and COUPLED through the same probabilities.
They are not independent scalar quadratics or a prescribed label policy.
For example, at the initial state the Hessian covariance between the root
and any deeper spine coordinate j is

 Cov(a_root,a_(0^j))
   =2sqrt(w_0)*P_0,0*E[a_(0^j)]>0.                 (35)

The equality holds because the root feature is +sqrt(w_0) wherever
the deeper coordinate is nonzero. Positivity follows from the explicit
initial means computed next. The changing distribution in (34), rather
than a fixed Hessian, determines later interactions.

Initially ell_0,k=(k-1)t0 for k<m and ell_0,m=m*t0. Thus

 P_0,k=1/(m+4) for k<m, P_0,m=4/(m+4),
 Phi_T(x_0)=log[(m+4)/4],
 E[a_(0^j)]=sqrt(w_j)*(m+2-j)/(m+4).                (36)

The weights and the starting point are fully specified before the
trajectory. Formula (34) is an exact analytic recurrence, not evidence
that its final value has a desired size.

Since sum_k q_k ell_i,k=0, there is an exact objective identity

 D_i:=Phi_T(x_i)=sum_(k=0)^m q_k log(q_k/P_i,k),
 V_T=2D_n/(Ltree*R_T^2).                            (37)

Thus D_i is relative entropy with the stated orientation: the uniform
leaf reference is the FIRST argument, not the current Gibbs law.
All probabilities are positive. Its nonnegativity also follows directly
from Jensen. This entropy is the actual value, not an added potential.

## 7. The priced missing estimate and exact same-word comparison interface

Equations (26), (30), (33), (34), and (37) specify a globally legal family
on every original H_m. They do NOT yet provide a useful terminal payoff.
The diagnostic target B_n V_T>=1 is EXACTLY

 D_n>=eta_m,
 eta_m=Ltree*t0^2*r_tree^(1-m)*Zgeom^2/(2B_n).       (38)

It is a concrete inequality for the entirely specified recurrence (34),
not an unknown initial state, unknown feature library, or Gram oracle.
Let r_inf=2/lambda, Zgeom_inf=1/(1-r_inf), and
Ltree_inf=(2-r_inf)/2. From (1) and (7),

 N*eta_m -> eta_inf
   =exp(1)*Ltree_inf*t0^2*r_inf/
                     [2 Z_1 (1-r_inf)^2] in(0,infinity).  (39)

Indeed N*r_tree^(-m)=c^m. Thus the needed entropy has order1/N after
the exact radius has been paid. The assertion D_n>=eta_m is UNPROVED.
No claim of even a uniform constant fraction of (38) is made.

One rigorous terminal diagnostic follows by conditioning the relative
entropy (37) on the last two leaves. They have reference masses1/N each,
and their current log probability ratio is2y_n,m-1. Splitting the
relative-entropy sum into this pair and its complement gives a binary
group divergence plus two nonnegative conditional divergences. Therefore

 D_n >= (2/N) log cosh(y_n,m-1).                    (40)

For detail, the conditional pair law has probabilities proportional to
exp(y_n,m-1),exp(-y_n,m-1). Its divergence from the equal pair law is
log cosh(y_n,m-1). The pair's reference weight is2/N. Nonnegativity
of the other divergences is Jensen applied to log. This is a LOWER
bound and need not capture the whole final entropy.

A SUFFICIENT deep-coordinate condition for (38) is consequently

 log cosh(y_n,m-1) >= N*eta_m/2.                    (41)

The exact accumulated coordinate update is

 y_n,m-1=t0-(w_(m-1)/Ltree)
                  sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i*(P_i,m-P_i,m-1).   (42)

Neither (41) nor a useful bound on the signed sum in (42) has been proved.
The trivial bound by S gives a coordinate uncertainty of order N and
does not supply a terminal lower bound. It also permits exact cancellation.

For clarity, the complete SAME-word comparisons remain the following
explicit, unresolved scalar thresholds, with NO replacement of a full
reference by a selected path or a lens:

 V_T>=K_n(h)       iff D_n>=Ltree*R_T^2*K_n(h)/2,
 V_T>=F_ray_n(h)   iff D_n>=Ltree*R_T^2*F_ray_n(h)/2,
 V_T>=Cfull(h)     iff D_n>=Ltree*R_T^2*Cfull(h)/2,
 B_n V_T>=1       iff D_n>=eta_m.                   (43)

The equality (37) is a complete payoff formula, but (43) is NOT a
completed performance comparison. All four terminal comparisons for
this third candidate are BLOCKED. In particular, legality alone and a
large initial entropy do not prove a useful hard instance.

## 8. Two direct attempts, their exact failures, and counterexample checks

Attempt A: propagate the equal initial spine fields or compare all leaf
masses to a constant multiple of uniform. Both proposed shortcuts fail
already in the explicit initial/first-step data. Equation (34) gives

 y_1,j=t0-[h_0*w_j/Ltree]*(m+2-j)/(m+4).

Thus

 y_1,0-y_1,1
 =-h_0*[w_0*(m+2)-w_1*(m+1)]/[Ltree*(m+4)]<0.      (44)

The equal-field ansatz is not invariant. Also P_0,m=4/(m+4), so
P_0,m/q_m=4N/(m+4) is unbounded. A proposed absolute, all-time
near-uniform probability cap is false for this very initial condition.
These observations do not exclude a later mixing statement with a paid
transient. No such statement is assumed in (38).

Attempt B: use the repeated original word H_j as an independent child
evolution. Split the feature tree at the root and write

 Phi_T=log[(exp(y_root)*exp(Phi_left)
                  +exp(-y_root)*exp(Phi_right))/2].

Each child Phi is the log-partition function of its explicitly inherited
descendant features on its own Euclidean coordinates. If P_left is the
current subtree mass, then

 grad_left Phi_T=P_left*grad Phi_left,
 x_left' =x_left-(h/Ltree)*P_left*grad Phi_left.       (45)

Thus, with the child's own sharp constant

 L_left=w_1+(1/2)sum_(j>1)w_j < Ltree,

its actual normalized step is

               h_eff=h*P_left*L_left/Ltree.         (46)

This is a state-dependent number, not the original physical h. Initially
P_left=(m+3)/(m+4), and it strictly decreases after the FIRST actual step.
To prove the strict change, the right child starts at0 and has zero
gradient, so stays at0. The root field strictly decreases because
P_left>1/2. The left child makes a positive step of size
h_0 P_left/Ltree <2/L_left, since h_0<2 and L_left<Ltree.
The smooth descent inequality shows its value strictly decreases, as
its gradient is nonzero by (36). Its odds
P_left/(1-P_left)=exp(2y_root+Phi_left-Phi_right)
therefore strictly decrease. This proves that even the first two
effective clock factors in (46) differ.

Consequently a recursion that inserts fresh copies of the ORIGINAL
child schedule silently changes the physical steps. An adaptive clock
bound might still be useful, but it must account for the full history
of subtree masses, coupling to ancestors, all returns and the exact
radius (33). No such bound has been proved in this round.

An additional counterexample check rules out a global entropy-monotonicity
shortcut for (34)'s function. In this SAME Phi_T, put only the root field
equal to s and all descendants zero. The function is log cosh(s), and a
step h gives s'=s-h*(w_0/Ltree)*tanh(s). Whenever
h*w_0/Ltree>2, sufficiently small positive s has s'<-s and therefore
Phi_T(x')>Phi_T(x). Such a level occurs in H_m for all sufficiently
large m, since its maximum h grows without bound whereas w_0/Ltree
tends to a positive constant. This point is a test state of the same
function, not a claim that the trajectory (32) visits it. It refutes
global monotonicity at those steps, not a trajectory-specific inequality.
This scalar slice is ONLY a counterexample test; it is not substituted
for the actual coupled construction (32)--(37).

After these two genuine attempts and the counterexample checks, the
unresolved estimate is (38), or an alternative actual terminal bound
strong enough to decide (43). Its scope is the specified growing tree
Gibbs family and original enclosing-m word. Mark it BLOCKED, not a
routine missing lemma, and not a theorem-level impossibility claim.

## 9. Literature, scope guards, and remaining work

The standard background is Boyd and Vandenberghe, Convex Optimization,
the log-sum-exp Hessian discussion on printed page74 and the entropy
conjugacy discussion on printed page93:
https://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf .
The exact primary-source passages and the page74 Cauchy--Schwarz proof
were read through web extraction. All actual claims used here were
derived in this joint manuscript; no theorem from that text is imported
as a Danus fact.

Retrieval audit: Matlas was queried for the finite-Gibbs covariance and
attainment mechanism. Its three results (2107.09572 Lemma1,

claim and were not used. The original www.stanford.edu PDF request
timed out in the web reader and returned HTTP403 under curl. The author

Local extraction failed because pdftotext was unavailable (exit127);
checks found no installed Python PDF extractor or alternate PDF CLI.
No installation was attempted. No successful local text extraction is
claimed. Primary-source indexed text supplied the background reading.

The accepted finite fact e9a55f57461f253c remains an original H6,
dimension66 result only. It is not an all-m, tree, or paid-radius premise.
Old finite-character policies, quadratic channels, near-Markov rules,
and the rejected rank-one Haar transcript do not decide (38). Conversely,
the scoped failures in Sections3--4 do not decide their full families
or all entropic mechanisms.

All physical queries are retained throughout. No minimum is chosen at
infinity. All actual functions are finite and globally smooth with a
proved admissible constant. The tree constant is sharp, so its payoff
gap cannot be attributed here to an unstated covariance normalization.
The chain's constant4 is only a valid bound, but its support certificate
has the same vanishing ratios at every admissible smoothness constant,
by the explicit lower bound2 on that constant. Its ACTUAL error is still
not upper bounded by this certificate screen.

Notation correction to preliminary direction7ec66d4b933755c3: the local
geometric sum is Zgeom throughout this canonical joint draft, to keep it

The next mathematical obligation, if separately authorized, is a genuine

# Two chronological core-return triangles: exact compatibility and mechanism failure

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Max4 round41,
the first of the two new rounds authorized by TASK.md and
master_guidance a23831f2a36b3c4a after completed40. Completed ceiling42,
attempted ceiling43 including the historical interruption. No formal
gate is allocated. This is neither a new fact nor a threshold seed.

## 1. Result and its limits

This draft analyzes a specific attempted escape into the remaining
second-step domain w>4/3. The actual chronological word is

    h=(u,w,z,v)=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d),
    a,b,c,d>0, alpha=ab<1, gamma=cd<1,
    C=1+u+w+z+v.

No reordering or reversal is used. These are explicit assumptions of
this witness family, not a reduction of the original certificate cone.

The proposed mechanism joins two saturated projection triangles,
one at the original first two steps and one at the original last two
steps, with a core query at original indices0,2,4. Their complete
transverse covariance is free. We prove the following statements jointly.

1. There is an exact closed interval test for whether these two
   prescribed triangles admit ANY joint Euclidean realization obeying
   every original interpolation row. The test is necessary and sufficient,
   and a static function is supplied whenever it passes.
2. If c(a+w)<1+v, no covariance gives a legal joint trajectory. Both
   marginal triangles remain individually legal. Thus local legality
   does not justify chronological concatenation.
3. On this family's entire phase w>=sqrt(2), v>=sqrt(2), every legal
   member, with EVERY legal assignment of its finite values, has
   primitive energy at least the OLD unit-quadratic energy. Arbitrary
   finite orthogonal sums do not change this conclusion. Thus this
   family cannot exclude any survivor of the old unit test on that phase.
4. Choosing transverse covariance, optimizing values, or increasing
   the ambient dimension cannot repair either of these failures while
   preserving the two prescribed marginal triangles.

The full original threshold cone is not decided. There is no new exact
survivor of its complete necessary conjunction and no new global or
regional full-cone exclusion asserted here. The proposed two-triangle
route is BLOCKED as a solution route; the exact missing extension is
stated in Section10. This is a precise mechanism failure under the
bounded-allocation guidance, not a claim that the requested seed was
found or proved impossible.

Only accepted facts04219554e41d9750 (Section10, the full primitive
identity) and1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation orientation and realization)
are correctness inputs. Every new ingredient is proved within this
single draft. No unverified earlier triangle or scalar result is used
as a premise. In particular defective43a616da7da451c7 and its unchecked
global reductions are neither used nor silently repaired.

## 2. Original certificate and primitive energy

Use all original indices0,1,2,3,4. Let Lambda_ij>=0 on all twenty
distinct finite pairs and Lambda_ii=0. Let e_all be the all-ones column,
T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i and0 for t>i, and set

    L_flow=diag(Lambda e_all)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2u,-2w,-2z,-2v,2(C-1))^T,
    Dprim=diag(u(2-u),w(2-w),z(2-z),v(2-v),-(C-1)(C+1)),
    M=Dprim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.

The sought certificate requires L_flow^T e_all=omega and M positive
semidefinite on ALL of R5. No formal coordinate or coefficient is
removed. For actual smoothness-one GD data define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)
         +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-(C-1)C||g_4||^2.

The accepted identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_4)>.

Every actual convex1-smooth trajectory has Q_ij<=0. Thus a negative
actual V_h excludes the entire original certificate, regardless of
rank, support, or coefficients. This is the only inference about the
original cone used below.

For the saturated values

    f_i^0=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

direct expansion gives

    Q_ij^0=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>,
    V_h(f^0)=||x_0||^2-||x_4+(C-1)g_4||^2.           (1)

For the second identity, the sum of
2h_i<x_i,g_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2 telescopes to
||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2. The remaining terms are
-2(C-1)<x_4,g_4>-(C-1)^2||g_4||^2.

## 3. The two prescribed marginal triangles

Define positive constants

    Kp=a^2 b+(1-alpha)(u+w),
    Kt=c^2 d+(1-gamma)(z+v),
    R=a^2 b/Kp, tau=c^2 d/Kt, Tnorm=R*tau.           (2)

Then0<R<1 and0<tau<1. The following alternative expressions will
also be needed:

    Kp=w+1-a(w^2-2),
    Kt=v+1-c(v^2-2),
    Kt-c=(v+1)(1-gamma).                            (3)

For example, Kp=a^2 b+a+w+1-ab(a+w+1)
=a+w+1-ab(w+1)=w+1-a((b+1)^2-2).
The same substitution with c,d gives the second identity; subtracting
c gives the third.

Let E,P,Z be vectors with the prescribed marginal inner products

    ||E||^2=1,
    <E,P>=||P||^2=R,
    <P,Z>=||Z||^2=Tnorm=R*tau.                     (4)

Only the cross inner product <E,Z> is not prescribed. Write

    <E,Z>=R*beta.

Define the two intermediate gradients

    Agrad=-(aE+P)/w, Bgrad=-(cP+Z)/v,

and the actual chronological transcript

    x=(E,-aE,P,-cP,Z),
    g=(E,Agrad,P,Bgrad,Z).                          (5)

All four GD updates hold exactly: E-uE=-aE,
-aE-wAgrad=P, P-zP=-cP, and-cP-vBgrad=Z.
The only nonzero target normals are

    n=x_1-g_1=(P-alpha E)/w,
    m=x_3-g_3=(Z-gamma P)/v.                        (6)

The constants in(2) were chosen to give the two local equalities

    <Agrad,n>=<P,n>=(1-alpha)R/w,
    <Bgrad,m>=<Z,m>=(1-gamma)Tnorm/v.               (7)

Here is the first equality explicitly. Expansion of Agrad and n gives

    w^2<Agrad,n>=a*alpha+(alpha-a)R-R.

Subtract w(1-alpha)R. The result is
a^2 b-[w+1-a(w^2-2)]R=0 by(2),(3).
The same expansion with initial squared norm R, parameters c,d,
and terminal squared norm R*tau proves the second equality.

Individually, the first triangle has vertices E,Agrad,P. Its only
nonzero query normal is n. Equation(7) gives equality at P, while

    <E-Agrad,n>=-alpha(1-R)/w<0.

Also aE+wAgrad+P=0 with positive coefficients. It therefore contains
zero and gives a legal static projection trajectory for the first
two steps. The second triangle has vertices P,Bgrad,Z, contains zero
because cP+vBgrad+Z=0, and its only nonzero query normal is m. Its
remaining comparison is

    <P-Bgrad,m>=-gamma(R-Tnorm)/v<0.

Thus both marginal trajectories are legal for every parameter in the
stated phase. Their joint legality is a separate question.

## 4. Every original directed row and every optimum row

Assign saturated values f_i^0 as in Section2. Targets0,2,4 have zero
normal, so all twelve off-diagonal rows into those targets are zero.
The eight remaining original finite rows, with source first, are

    Q_01^0=-alpha(1-R)/w,
    Q_21^0=0,
    Q_31^0=R[alpha*beta-tau-(c+v)(1-alpha)]/(v*w),
    Q_41^0=R[tau-alpha*beta-(1-alpha)]/w,

    Q_03^0=R[beta-gamma-(1-gamma)tau]/v,
    Q_13^0=R[-a*beta+gamma(1+a)
                        -(1+w(1-gamma))tau]/(w*v),
    Q_23^0=-gamma R(1-tau)/v,
    Q_43^0=0.                                      (8)

For example,
<Bgrad,n>=[-c(1-alpha)R-(Tnorm-alpha R beta)]/(vw).
Subtracting <Agrad,n> from(7) gives Q_31^0. Also
<Agrad,m>=[-a(R beta-gamma R)-(Tnorm-gamma R)]/(wv);
subtracting <Bgrad,m> gives Q_13^0. The other displayed rows follow
directly from(6),(7). This checks all twenty original rows.

Include x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Every Q_(i,star)^0 is zero.
The only nonzero Q_(star,i)^0 are

    Q_(star,1)^0=-(1-alpha)R/w,
    Q_(star,3)^0=-(1-gamma)R*tau/v,

and these are negative. Thus the optimum constraints add no omitted
restriction. The entire analysis is on the actual chronological word.

## 5. Exact covariance interval, attained in dimension at most three

Put

    S=sqrt((1-R)*tau*(1-tau)/R),
    Lp=(tau-1+alpha)/alpha,
    Up=[tau+(c+v)(1-alpha)]/alpha,
    Lt=[gamma(1+a)-(1+w(1-gamma))*tau]/a,
    Ut=gamma+(1-gamma)*tau.                         (9)

The COMPLETE necessary and sufficient condition for this family to
give a legal joint transcript is

    max(Lp,Lt,tau-S) <= min(Up,Ut,tau+S).           (10)

First, the Gram matrix of E,P,Z is

    [ 1       R       R beta ]
    [ R       R       R tau  ]
    [ R beta  R tau   R tau  ].

After eliminating the middle coordinate, its remaining quadratic is
the two-by-two matrix with diagonal1-R and R*tau*(1-tau), and
off-diagonal R(beta-tau). Both diagonals are positive. Hence full
Gram positivity is exactly |beta-tau|<=S. Equation(8) gives precisely
Lp<=beta<=Up and Lt<=beta<=Ut. This proves necessity of(10).

For sufficiency select any beta in(10). In an orthonormal coordinate
system E1,E2,E3 set

    P=sqrt(R)*E1,
    E=sqrt(R)*E1+sqrt(1-R)*E2,
    Z=sqrt(R)*tau*E1+R(beta-tau)/sqrt(1-R)*E2
      +sqrt(R*tau*(1-tau)-R^2(beta-tau)^2/(1-R))*E3. (11)

The radicand is nonnegative by(10). Formula(11) has exactly(4) and
<E,Z>=R beta, including either equality endpoint of the Gram interval.
Equation(8) proves every row. Let K be the convex hull of
E,Agrad,P,Bgrad,Z. It contains zero by the first relation in Section3.
Define ONE static function

    F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.           (12)

Completing the square identifies its unique maximizing g with the
Euclidean projection onto K. The normal inequalities(8) imply that
each g_i is that projection at x_i. In particular F_K(x_i)=f_i^0.
Adding two projection inequalities gives
<x-y,P_K(x)-P_K(y)> >= ||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2, so P_K is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing inequalities give
0<=F_K(y)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),y-x>
 <=<P_K(y)-P_K(x),y-x><=||y-x||^2.
Thus F_K is differentiable with gradient P_K, which is1-Lipschitz.
It is convex as a maximum of affine functions and finite because K
is compact. Since0 is in K, F_K>=0=F_K(0). It has a genuine minimizer.
This proves sufficiency and static attainment without an external
unverified interpolation result. No normalized objective error or
claim of a common function for all words is made.

One useful equivalent form of(10) follows from

    Lp<tau<Up,       tau<Ut.

If Lt<=tau, beta=tau is legal. If Lt>tau, the full condition is

    Lt<=Up, Lt<=Ut, Lt<=tau+S.                      (13)

The other lower endpoints are then inactive. These conclusions follow
because tau-Lp=(1-alpha)(1-tau)/alpha>0 and
Up-tau=(1-alpha)(tau+c+v)/alpha>0. No interval branch is suppressed.

## 6. Cross-stage obstruction and the failure of orthogonal gluing

Even before testing the Gram matrix, the two original rows into target3
force Lt<=Ut. Substitution from(9) and then(3) gives

    Lt<=Ut
 iff gamma <= [1+(w+a)(1-gamma)]*tau
 iff Kt <= c[1+(w+a)(1-gamma)]
 iff 1+v <= c(w+a).                               (14)

In the middle equivalence gamma=cd>0 and tau=c*gamma/Kt.
For the last one subtract c and use Kt-c=(v+1)(1-gamma)>0.
Consequently c(w+a)<1+v makes this entire joint family EMPTY, with
every transverse covariance and every ambient dimension allowed.
It does not make the original primitive cone empty.

The direct orthogonal concatenation of the transverse directions
corresponds to beta=tau. From the exact formula

    Lt-tau=(1-gamma)*tau*[u(1+v)-c*w]/(a*c),        (15)

it is legal exactly when c*w>=u(1+v). If this fails, allowing covariance
can help only when every inequality(13) holds. This is a concrete
restriction beyond the availability of the two marginal triangles.

For completeness, when Lt>tau the Gram part of(13) is equivalent to

    b*d*(1-gamma)*[u(1+v)-c*w]^2
          <=(1-alpha)(u+w)(z+v).                   (16)

To check it, square(15), and insert
(1-R)/R=(1-alpha)(u+w)/(a^2 b) and
(1-tau)/tau=(1-gamma)(z+v)/(c^2 d) into S^2.
All canceled quantities are strictly positive. The unsquared version
is retained in(10), so(16) is not applied in the Lt<=tau branch.

Here is an exact threshold diagnostic disproving automatic gluing:

    h=(5/4,9/4,3/2,7/4),    sum h=27/4>168/25,
    alpha=5/16, gamma=3/8, R=5/159, tau=6/71.

Both marginal triangles are individually legal by Section3. However

    Lt=603/568 > 243/568=Ut,

so the actual rows Q_13<=0 and Q_03<=0 are incompatible for every beta.
This word passes the old unit test, since C*alpha*gamma=465/512<1.
It is NOT a survivor of the complete older conjunction: its second
cut already fails, as C*(w-1)=155/16>(1+u)(1+z+v)=153/16.
The example refutes only automatic local-triangle gluing; it is not
an independent new test against the complete prior system.

## 7. All finite values are forced up to one common constant

This mechanism cannot be rescued by optimizing the sample values.
Fix ANY Gram realization of(4), in ANY dimension, whether or not its
saturated cross rows pass(10), and suppose arbitrary real finite
values f_i satisfy all original Q_ij<=0. Write
f_i=f_i^0+delta_i. Because all saturated residuals into each of the
core targets0,2,4 are zero, the corresponding inequalities imply

    delta_i<=delta_0,delta_2,delta_4 for every i,
    delta_0=delta_2=delta_4.

The zero residual Q_21^0 gives delta_2<=delta_1, while a core-target
row gives delta_1<=delta_0. Hence delta_1 equals the same constant.
Likewise Q_43^0=0 gives delta_4<=delta_3 and a core-target row gives
delta_3<=delta_0. Thus ALL five deltas are the same constant.

The primitive energy uses only the differences f_i-f_4. Every legal
finite value assignment therefore has exactly the same energy. This
argument uses only the full finite rows; imposing any further optimum
rows cannot create an additional value assignment or lower the energy.
Conversely(12) attains the displayed values with a genuine minimizer.

## 8. Whole-phase domination by the older unit quadratic

Since x_0=g_0=E and x_4=g_4=Z, equation(1) and Section7 give, for
EVERY legal value assignment to EVERY legal member,

    V_h=1-C^2 R*tau.                               (17)

Now rewrite(3) as

    b*Kp=1+(w^2-2)(1-alpha),
    d*Kt=1+(v^2-2)(1-gamma).                       (18)

For the first identity b(w+1)=w^2-1, and subtracting
ab(w^2-2) gives the result. The second is the same expansion with
c,d,v. Combining(2) and(18) gives the exact gain formula

    R*tau=alpha^2*gamma^2 /
      {[1+(w^2-2)(1-alpha)]
       [1+(v^2-2)(1-gamma)]}.                      (19)

On the ENTIRE stated phase w>=sqrt(2),v>=sqrt(2), both denominator
factors are at least1. Therefore

    V_h >= 1-C^2*alpha^2*gamma^2.                  (20)

The right side is exactly the primitive energy of the old unit
quadratic F(x)=x^2/2, started at1: its gradients are
1,-a,ab,-abc,abcd, its final point equals its final gradient, and(1)
gives1-C^2 a^2 b^2 c^2 d^2. Any original full certificate necessarily
makes this old energy nonnegative.

Thus the two-triangle mechanism cannot reject ANY old-unit-test
survivor on this phase. This is a complete comparison, with no numerical
sample, assumed certificate sparsity, or unsolved covariance optimization.
Equality in(19) occurs on this positive, alpha/gamma<1 phase exactly
when w=v=sqrt(2); the closed endpoints are included.

The comparison also holds for an arbitrary finite orthogonal sum of
scaled legal members on the same word. Their energies add with the
nonnegative squared scales. All their core-target rows, Q_21 and Q_43
remain zero, so Section7 also excludes a new joint value-price gain.
This does not assert a result about arbitrary correlated deformations
of the prescribed marginal inner products.

## 9. Explicit attacks on the proposed bridge

Direct attempt one fixed the two individually legal triangles and
placed their transverse directions orthogonally. Equation(15) gives
the exact failed hypothesis c*w>=u(1+v). Local legal interpolation
does not supply it.

Direct attempt two released the ENTIRE remaining covariance beta,
including both rank-two endpoints and all rank-three realizations.
Equations(10)--(16) solve that completion problem completely. The
diagnostic in Section6 defeats every covariance through two actual
original rows, without a PSD relaxation. Optimizing all finite values
then fails for the separate zero-row rigidity reason in Section7.

Finally, even whenever those repairs do succeed, the complete phase
w>=sqrt(2),v>=sqrt(2) is dominated by an old test by(20). Retuning beta,
combining members orthogonally, or changing the finite values cannot
produce the required advancement there. These are failures of the
specified new construction, not an impossibility proof for a seed.

No extra literature layer is needed to reject this exact mechanism:
it has reduced to the accepted finite interpolation orientation and
the original primitive identity, and every remaining calculation is
given here. Matlas returned unrelated proximal-gradient and bandit
lemmas after an earlier search timed out; web fallback found existing
composition references. No external theorem was imported or cited
as a mathematical premise.

## 10. Precise open bridge and bounded handoff

One possible continuation is to release at least one spherical
marginal constraint in(4), namely <E,P>=||P||^2 or
<P,Z>=||Z||^2, or to retain a nonzero normal at the middle query.
Those changes alter the individual triangle gains, cross-stage rows,
or value-rigidity graph. They are NOT covered by the closure above.

For example, for general vectors E,P,Z with ||E||=1, the same
chronological formulas(5),(6) remain valid, but their Gram variables

    A=<E,P>, B=<E,Z>, J=<P,Z>, R=||P||^2, T=||Z||^2

are free subject to the full three-by-three Gram being PSD. The four
nontrivial comparisons into EACH of targets1 and3 must then be tested
using(6), without imposing(7) or importing the energy(19). Even on
that broader core-return class, obtaining T>C^(-2) on every relevant
old survivor is still an unproved feasibility assertion. This draft
does not label it routine, solved, lossless, or equivalent to the
whole original cone. It is the remaining mechanism bridge, not a
new theorem that advances the original target by itself.

The actual original target remains an h with C>=193/25 and all20
nonnegative original multipliers, exact flow, and full-R5 PSD, or a
scope-complete decision of its remaining cone. No such object or
decision is supplied here. The route above is BLOCKED as an answer
to that target. One analytical round remains in the current wave
after this handoff; it is not a renewal beyond completed ceiling42.

Resources used: zero numerical evaluations, solvers, machine-algebra,
replays, formal-verifier calls, installations, consult calls, or native
subagents. Only symbolic reasoning, text retrieval and persistence.
No historical object, truth store, runtime counter or configuration
was edited. A proof handoff is not evidence that the outer loop has
already returned or stopped.
