# C100: remote reserve transport prices strong dense projections

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. Actual runtime round2 of the
allocation launched under guidance4226374d0649bf55; completed baseline29,
completed ceiling31, attempted ceiling32 including the historical
interruption. No numerical work or formal verification is authorized.
The original arbitrary-word exact-profile Goal, with W=2R, remains OPEN.

Accepted inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 for the original directed rows and
the static conjugate interpolant, and b98bb807d514e80d for the original
benchmark and scalar witnesses. Accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a,
dfde2fa326c908bf and e9a55f57461f253c are used only for the stated
same-word comparisons. C98 and C99 are unverified context, not premises.
All new mathematical arguments used here are proved together below.

## 1. Actual functions, the enlarged dependence class, and the result

Let F be an everywhere finite convex differentiable function on a finite
Euclidean space, with1-Lipschitz gradient and a nonempty minimizer set.
For a supplied word h of length n consider its actual trajectory

    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i,  g_i=grad F(X_i),
    f_i=F(X_i)-min F,  q_i=||g_i||^2,
    R_init=dist(X_0,argmin F)>0,  V=2f_n/R_init^2.

All directed interpolation inequalities concern this ONE function.
The actual terminal gradient is unrestricted. If any queried gradient
vanishes then all later iterates minimize F and V=0. In the remaining
case define, at the queried indices,

    delta_i=f_i-q_i/2,  Jval_i=f_i+q_i/2,
    kval_i=Jval_i/q_i=1+delta_i/q_i.

For an included minimizer chosen as origin define separately

    eta_i=<X_i,g_i>/q_i,
    z_i=<X_i,g_i>-f_i-q_i/2.

The optimum rows give delta_i>=0 and z_i>=0, and

    kval_i=eta_i-z_i/q_i.                         (1)

Thus a restriction on kval is a restriction on actual values, not on
support about an arbitrarily named minimizer. No restriction on eta_i,
z_i, anchor density, signs of initial coordinates, or dimension is made.

Use the ORIGINAL enclosing-m words, with parameters fixed throughout
every recursive child:

    m>=4, N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2),
    beta=1-1/m, c=sigma(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

Here h=H_m, 0<a<sqrt(2)-1, and the shortest step is lshort=1+a.
The following value range is the substantive restriction of this draft:

    1+a/2 <= kval_i <= 1+a  for every queried i.  (2)

Assume all adjacent QUERIED gradients have nonpositive inner product.
Set, for0<=i<n-1,

    d_i=h_i-1,
    t_i=-<g_i,g_(i+1)>/q_i >=0.

There is NO cap t_i<=1. For each odd i=1,3,...,n-4 define the signed
departure from the distance-two Markov identity

    defect2_i=<g_i,g_(i+2)>/q_i-t_i t_(i+1).       (3)

The middle physical step in each such pair is h_(i+1)=lshort.
Define for k in[1+a/2,1+a], writing e=k-1,

    Ushort_a(k)=[a-(1-a)e]/[1+(1-a)e],
    Aenv_a(k)=e[k+lshort Ushort_a(k)]/k^2,
    Cenv_a(k)=Ushort_a(k)(lshort-k)/k^2,
    Epair_a(k,d)=Aenv_a(k)+Cenv_a(k)d^2.            (4)

These are explicit real scalar functions; Epair is strictly positive.
For the indicated odd indices put

    eleft_i=|kval_i-kval_(i+1)|,
    eright_i=|kval_(i+2)-kval_(i+1)|,
    D_dep=sum_i d_i max(0,-defect2_i)
                    /[kval_(i+2) Epair_a(kval_(i+1),d_i)],
    E_mix=sum_i [(2/a)eleft_i+(1+4/a^2)eright_i]+D_dep,
    TV_val=sum_(i=0)^(n-2)|kval_(i+1)-kval_i|.

All sums over odd indices above stop at n-4. These quantities are paid
defects of an ACTUAL trajectory; they are neither anchor counts nor a
claim of interpolation sufficiency. For m>=256,

    E_mix <=26 TV_val+D_dep.                       (5)

The principal bound proved below is, for m>=256,

    V <= [64(1+2a)/(45a^3 beta^2)]
          beta^(2n)c^(-2m)(243/256)^(N/8)exp(E_mix)
      < 50 beta^(2n)c^(-2m)(243/256)^(N/8)exp(E_mix). (6)

In particular, the entire class (2) with E_mix=o(n) has B_n V->0.
This includes every constant-kval, arbitrarily dense Markov anchor,
with arbitrary nonstationary nonpositive correlations and arbitrarily
strong permitted long projections. It also includes variable-kval
Markov families with TV_val=o(n). More generally exact Markov dependence
is unnecessary: E_mix=o(n) is sufficient for the same obstruction.

No upper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m) is asserted. Leaving the value
range(2), permitting positive adjacent correlations, paying linear
value variation, or paying the indicated negative distance-two defects
are genuinely outside this conclusion. No necessary budget is described
as a sufficient construction.

## 2. Original rows and exact remote slack transport

The accepted original residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. (7)

For a general step h_i=1+d_i>1, adding both adjacent rows gives

    q_(i+1)/q_i <=d_i+(d_i-1)t_i.

Cauchy--Schwarz gives t_i^2<=q_(i+1)/q_i, so

    (t_i+1)(t_i-d_i)<=0,  0<=t_i<=d_i.             (8)

The nonnegative lower bound uses the assumed adjacent sign. In
particular at a shortest step q_(i+1)<=a q_i.

The reverse adjacent slack, a different quantity from z_i, is

    ell_rev_i=-Q_(i+1,i)
       =delta_i-delta_(i+1)+d_i t_i q_i-q_(i+1)>=0. (9)

Suppose for this paragraph the queried unit directions g_i/sqrt(q_i)
have the FULL Markov product law: their inner product at indices r<s
is the product of the adjacent normalized inner products from r to s.
Set p_(r,s)=product_(k=r)^(s-1)(-t_k), with p_(s,s)=1.
The amplitude ratios telescope to give <g_r,g_s>=q_r p_(r,s).
For i+1<j<n, expanding(7) and the GD recursion
gives the exact transport identity

    Q_(j,i)-Q_(j,i+1)
      =(delta_(i+1)-delta_i)(1-p_(i+1,j))
                         -ell_rev_i p_(i+1,j).      (10)

Indeed the difference before substituting(9) is

    delta_(i+1)-delta_i
         +p_(i+1,j)[q_(i+1)-d_i t_i q_i],

which equals(10). In particular the first remote reverse row is

    Q_(i+2,i)=-ell_rev_(i+1)
       +(1+t_(i+1))(delta_(i+1)-delta_i)
       +t_(i+1)ell_rev_i.                          (11)

Thus adjacent reverse equalities at i and i+1 force
delta_(i+1)<=delta_i. With a constant kval>1, they force
q_(i+1)<=q_i; hence t_i>1 is then impossible by Cauchy--Schwarz.
Attempting to saturate adjacent rows while using a strong projection
omits exactly the reserve increase charged by(11).

Equation(11) alone is not assumed sufficient for legality. The
whole-family bound below concerns actual functions satisfying ALL rows.
For the larger non-Markov class we use(3) explicitly instead of
transferring(10) outside its hypothesis.

## 3. A short-step cap that retains the actual value ratios

Consider a shortest step from a queried point of ratio k to another
queried point of ratio k', both in(2), and let its projection be u>=0.
Write r=q_next/q_current. The forward adjacent row is

    (k'-1)r >= (k-1)-a+u.                          (12)

Its sum with the reverse row yields r<=a-(1-a)u by(8).
Combining these inequalities, without replacing either value, gives

    u <= Ushort_a(k')+(k'-k)/[1+(1-a)(k'-1)].       (13)

The numerator of Ushort is positive on the whole range(2). Its derivative
in k equals -(1-a)(1+a)/[1+(1-a)(k-1)]^2, of absolute value at most1.
Consequently

    u<=Ushort_a(k)+2|k'-k|.                        (14)

Also Ushort_a(k)>=a^2/(1+a-a^2)>=a^2/2. The first inequality uses its
monotonic decrease in k, and the second uses1+a-a^2<2.

## 4. Exact two-step envelope, including a changed dependence law

Take an odd index i in the range used in Section1. Abbreviate its three
ratios by k0,k1,k2; put s=q_(i+1)/q_i, r=q_(i+2)/q_i,
t=t_i, u=t_(i+1), d=d_i, and v=defect2_i.
The reverse adjacent row at i gives

    k1 s <= k0-1+d t.                              (15)

The ORIGINAL remote reverse row from i+2 to i, expanded directly, is

    Q_(i+2,i)/q_i=k2 r-(k0-1)+d(tu+v)-lshort u s.

Thus, writing vminus=max(0,-v),

    k2 r <=(k0-1)(1+lshort u/k1)
              +u(lshort-k1)d t/k1+d vminus.        (16)

The coefficient lshort-k1 is nonnegative by(2). Since0<=t<=d,
replace dt by d^2 on the right. In the constant-ratio, v=0 case,
(13) allows u<=Ushort_a(k1);(16) then proves exactly

    r<=Epair_a(k1,d).                              (17)

No limitation on the size of t below its universal bound d was used.

For variable ratios put eleft=|k0-k1| and eright=|k2-k1|.
All coefficients in the right side of(16) after replacing dt are
nonnegative. Therefore its vminus-free part is bounded by Epair times

    (k1/k2) max(1,(k0-1)/(k1-1))
                          max(1,u/Ushort_a(k1)).

Because k1-1>=a/2, (14) and the lower bound following it give

    k1/k2 <=exp(eright),
    max(1,(k0-1)/(k1-1))<=exp(2eleft/a),
    max(1,u/Ushort_a(k1))<=exp(4eright/a^2).

If E0=(2/a)eleft+(1+4/a^2)eright, we have

    r<=Epair exp(E0)+d vminus/k2
      <=Epair exp(E0+d vminus/(k2 Epair)).          (18)

For the second inequality factor Epair exp(E0), use E0>=0 and
1+x<=exp(x) for x>=0. This proves the asserted extension to non-Markov
laws with an explicit signed price. Positive v only strengthens the
remote row and needs no penalty. The terminal gradient is not used.

## 5. Uniform real envelope coefficients

For0<a<=sqrt(2)-1 and k in(2),

    0<Aenv_a(k)<=1/3,   0<=Cenv_a(k)<=a^2/4.        (19)

Here is a direct proof. Set e=k-1 in[a/2,a], D=1+(1-a)e and
U=Ushort_a(k). It is positive and decreasing in e. Hence

    U <= a(1+a)/(2+a-a^2) <=a(1+a)/2.

Also a-e<=a/2 and k>=1+a/2, so

    Cenv=U(a-e)/k^2
       <=a^2(1+a)/[4(1+a/2)^2]<=a^2/4.

For the bound on Aenv, consider A(e)=e[k+(1+a)U]/k^2 on0<=e<=a.
Direct differentiation gives

    A'(e)={k+(1+a)(1-e)U
                -e k(1-a)(1+a)^2/D^2}/k^3.

The middle term is nonnegative. Moreover
e(1-a)(1+a)^2/D^2<=a(1+a)<1, since a<=sqrt(2)-1.
Thus A is increasing. At e=a, using U=a^2/(1+a-a^2),

    A(a)=a/(1+a-a^2)<=1/3,

the last inequality being equivalent to a^2+2a<=1. This proves(19).
In particular

    Epair_a(k,d)<=1/3+(a^2/4)d^2.                  (20)

This envelope holds at every macro separately, even when its k changes.

## 6. The complete norm and the actual closest distance

For the full product-correlated subclass, write A_i=sqrt(q_i) and
u_i=g_i/A_i, for0<=i<n. Its correlations are real numbers rho_i in[-1,0]
with <u_i,u_j>=product_(k=i)^(j-1)rho_k. Actual real coordinates are

    u_0=e_0,
    u_(i+1)=rho_i u_i+sqrt(1-rho_i^2)e_(i+1),       (21)

where each nondegenerate innovation is orthogonal to the past. The
literal finite sign law is

    P(s_0,...,s_(n-1))=2^(-n)
                     product_(i<n-1)(1+rho_i s_i s_(i+1)),
    s_i in{-1,1}.                                 (22)

Summing the last sign successively proves normalization. Equivalently,
start with a fair sign and independent bond signs of means rho_i; this
gives the stated products. Interior rho give positive probability to
every sign word. This law is not a substitute for interpolation.

Conversely the product identities imply that
(u_(i+1)-rho_i u_i)/sqrt(1-rho_i^2) is a unit vector orthogonal to all
earlier u_j, by expanding its inner products. Thus(21) also describes
the canonical coordinates of any such product law.

Let0 be any included minimizer and let X_perp be the component of X_0
perpendicular to every queried gradient. Since previous gradients are
orthogonal to each new innovation, its initial coefficient
w_(i+1)=<X_0,e_(i+1)> satisfies

    eta_(i+1)A_(i+1)=rho_i(eta_i-h_i)A_i
                            +sqrt(1-rho_i^2)w_(i+1).

The initial e_0 coefficient is eta_0 A_0. Orthogonal decomposition
therefore gives the COMPLETE identity

    ||X_0||^2=eta_0^2 q_0+||X_perp||^2
      +sum_(i:|rho_i|<1)
         [eta_(i+1)A_(i+1)-rho_i(eta_i-h_i)A_i]^2
                                             /(1-rho_i^2). (23)

At rho_i=-1 the numerator must vanish and there is no new summand.
Every dense initial coefficient and any perpendicular reserve is paid.
This is a norm about the included minimizer, not automatically the true
closest-minimizer distance. For the whole-class proof we instead use

    q_0<=R_init^2,                                 (24)

which follows from gradient Lipschitzness between X_0 and EVERY minimizer,
followed by taking the infimum. Hence the proof neither drops any initial
cost nor incorrectly declares a conveniently chosen minimizer closest.
The explicit finite interpolants in Section9 have unique minimizer0 and
their true distances are computed from all their coordinates.

## 7. Original enclosing-m product and the unrestricted final two steps

At level j, write dlev_j=beta c^(j-1) for the actual offset; that level occurs
N/2^(j+1) times. Level0 is a. Multiplying the recursive words gives

    D_word=product_(i<n)(h_i-1)
          =a^(N-1)c^(N-m-1)=beta^n c^(-m).         (25)

For example its a and c exponents satisfy the recursions obtained by
squaring the child product and multiplying by a c^j; induction gives
2^j-1 and2^j-j-1 for H_j, proving(25).

Use the first physical shortest step only through q_1<=a q_0 from(8).
Apply(18) to each odd index1,3,...,n-4. These pairs include every bridge
of level at least1 except the FINAL level1 bridge. Consequently

    q_(n-2)/q_0 <= a exp(E_mix)
       product_(j=1)^(m-1) Eupper(dlev_j)^(b_j),    (26)
    Eupper(d)=1/3+(a^2/4)d^2,
    b_1=N/4-1,  b_j=N/2^(j+1) for j>=2.

The last two physical steps are exactly1+beta and1+a, both below2.
Integration of the1-Lipschitz gradient along a step yields

    f_(i+1)<=f_i-h_i(2-h_i)q_i/2.

Therefore f_n<=f_(n-2), with no condition on g_n, no condition on its
support or value ratio, and no Markov extension to it. From(2),(24),

    V <=(1+2a)q_(n-2)/R_init^2
      <=(1+2a)a exp(E_mix) product_j Eupper(dlev_j)^b_j. (27)

This finite formula is valid already for every m>=4.

For m>=256 we now bound its product explicitly. The elementary estimates

    a^2 beta^2>1/6,  beta^4>49/50,  c^2>23/4       (28)

follow as follows. The ratio beta^2/(1+1/m) is increasing with m, and at
m=256 it exceeds79/80, since80*65025>79*65792. Also
sigma<29/12, so a beta>237/580 and6*237^2>580^2. This proves the first
inequality. Bernoulli's inequality gives beta^4>=1-4/m>=63/64>49/50.
Finally sigma^2=3+2sqrt(2)>23/4 proves the last inequality.

Normalize each Eupper by a^2 dlev_j^2. At level1, (28) gives

    Eupper(dlev_1)/(a^2dlev_1^2)<9/4.

At level2, a^2dlev_2^2=beta^4, so the ratio is at most
50/147+1/4<3/5. At every level j>=3, a^2dlev_j^2>=beta^4 c^2, so the ratio
is at most200/3381+1/4<5/16.
There are N/4-1, N/8, and N/8-1 such factors respectively. Thus

    product_j [Eupper(dlev_j)/(a^2dlev_j^2)]^b_j
      <(64/45)(243/256)^(N/8).                     (29)

Indeed (9/4)^2(3/5)(5/16)=243/256, and the two missing factors contribute
(4/9)(16/5)=64/45. The unnormalized baseline product is

    product_j(a^2dlev_j^2)^b_j=D_word^2/(a^4 beta^2). (30)

It consists of every step offset squared except the first shortest
step and the last pair of offsets(beta,a). Multiplying(29),(30) into
(27), then using(25), proves the first line of(6).

For m>=256, (28) gives a>2/5, while beta^2>49/50 and1+2a<2. Thus
64(1+2a)/(45a^3 beta^2)<50, proving the simpler second line. The same
a bound gives2/a<=5 and1+4/a^2<=26. The edges counted in the eleft and
eright sums are disjoint adjacent edges, proving(5).

## 8. Original-B and every applicable same-word comparison

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d uses the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark B_n,
not a chosen recursion tree, and gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)<=1+4*3^m.
Consequently (6) implies

    B_n V <50(1+4*3^m) beta^(2n)c^(-2m)
                        (243/256)^(N/8)exp(E_mix). (31)

For E_mix=o(n) the right side tends to zero. More quantitatively, any
family satisfying the stated assumptions with liminf B_n V>0 must obey

    liminf E_mix/N >=(1/8)log(256/243).             (32)

To check(32), choose a positive eventual lower bound v0 for B_n V and
take logarithms in(6). The exact necessary inequality is

    E_mix >=(N/8)log(256/243)+2n log(1/beta)+2m log c
          -log B_n-log[64(1+2a)/(45a^3 beta^2)]+log v0.

After division by N the extra terms tend to zero. In particular a
Markov family in(2) needs linear total variation of kval unless it leaves
another hypothesis: from(5), liminf TV_val/N is at least
log(256/243)/208 when D_dep=0 and the normalized payoff stays positive.
This is a necessary condition only.

There is a direct SAME-WORD comparison. If S_m=sum H_m and M_m=1+2S_m,
the recurrence S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a c^j gives

    S_m=N-1+a(c^m-N)/(c-2),   M_m<2c^m.            (33)

For the inequality, c>sigma>2, c^m>N, and
a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]<1 since sigma(sigma-2)=1. Thus S_m<c^m-1,
which proves the displayed strict bound on M_m.

The global Huber witness1/M_m is contained in K_n(H_m) by accepted
b98bb807d514e80d, in F_ray_n(H_m) by accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a, and in
V_lens(H_m) by accepted dfde2fa326c908bf. Therefore

    V/(1/M_m)<100 beta^(2n)c^(-m)(243/256)^(N/8)exp(E_mix),
    V/K_n(H_m), V/F_ray_n(H_m), V/V_lens(H_m)
                                         <=V/(1/M_m). (34)

All these ratios vanish for E_mix=o(n). No claim about a particular lens
parameter or a Chebyshev word is transferred to H_m.

For the ACTUAL m=6 word, a<3/10 and c^2<8 follow from
7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7. Since beta=5/6, (20) implies

    Eupper(dlev_j)<1/3+8^(j-1)/64.

For j=1,2,3,4,5 these are respectively bounded above by
3/8,1/2,4/3,9,65. In the constant-kval, zero-defect case E_mix=0,
(27) and its exact counts15,8,4,2,1 give

    V <(1/2)(3/8)^15(1/2)^8(4/3)^4 9^2 65 <1/256.

The second inequality uses3/8<1/2, (4/3)^4<4, 9^2<128 and65<128.
Hence this entire class on the ACTUAL H6 is below
1/256<1/50<56/2711, the accepted C59 witness e9a55f57461f253c.
No C59 assertion is transferred to later enclosing-m subwords.

The accepted orthogonal-moment obstruction is consistent with the
orthogonal subfamily here; it is not used as an unproved extension to
nonorthogonal gradients. The other accepted restricted obstructions and
conditional seed theorem supply no additional pointwise lower witness
for the present actual words, so no further comparison is inferred.

## 9. Real dense data: strong projection, all rows, exact radius

The excluded class is not an implicit t<=1 class. Here is a complete
three-dimensional family, useful also for testing the remote row.
Take h=(4,7/5), a=2/5, kval=6/5, eta=5/4, and a real parameter r>0.
In the standard orthonormal coordinates e_0,e_1,e_2 put

    g_0=e_0, g_1=-2e_0+e_1, g_2=sqrt(r)e_2,
    X_0=(5/4)e_0+(3/4)e_1+(5/4)sqrt(r)e_2,
    X_1=X_0-4g_0, X_2=X_1-(7/5)g_1,
    q_0=1, q_1=5, q_2=r, f_i=(7/10)q_i.

The support eta is5/4 at all three points; delta_i=q_i/5 and
z_i=q_i/20 are strictly positive. The queried projection t_0=2 exceeds1.
The full normalized directions have rho_0=-2/sqrt(5), rho_1=0;
their distance-two product is exact. The sign law is the positive law

    P(s_0,s_1,s_2)=(1/8)[1-(2/sqrt(5))s_0s_1].

The initial coefficients are all nonzero. Every original non-optimum
directed residual is as follows:

    Q_01=-9/5,             Q_10=-1/5,
    Q_12=-1-r/5,           Q_21=(6r-5)/5,
    Q_02=-r/5,             Q_20=(6r-1)/5.           (35)

The two optimum orientations are Q_(star,i)=-q_i/5 and
Q_(i,star)=-q_i/20. Hence the full data are legal exactly when0<r<=1/6;
they pass every adjacent and optimum row for0<r<=5/6. At r=1/2 only
the remote reverse Q_20=2/5 is violated. This is an explicit counterexample
to replacing full legality by these local rows, even with a positive
joint law, dense initial coordinates and positive reserves and slacks.

For0<r<=1/6 define the actual function from the accepted construction:

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+
          max{0, max_(i=0,1,2)(<X_i-g_i,v>-delta_i)},
    F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)).                         (36)

All rows in(35) and all optimum rows ensure interpolation by accepted
1252b8823bb5a290. Thus (36) is ONE convex1-smooth function realizing both
GD queries and the displayed actual terminal. As every delta_i>0, the
zero affine branch uniquely dominates in a neighborhood of v=0; hence
H equals||v||^2/2 there and its subgradient at0 is the singleton{0}.
The conjugate maximizer condition then implies argmin F={0}. Its true
initial radius and payoff, including every coordinate, are exactly

    R_init^2=(34+25r)/16,
    V=112r/(170+125r).                             (37)

At r=1/10 this is112/1825. Every residual in(35) is strictly negative.
For this same word the accepted scalar functional is K_2=45/19:
its global term is5/59, the first cut is45/19 and the second cut is4/625.
In particular112/1825<5/59, so K, ray and the augmented lens all dominate
this displayed witness. It is a legal strong-projection example, not a
benchmark improvement. C59 concerns a different word and is inapplicable.

For completeness the constant-ratio family on every actual H_m is also
nonempty with all initial innovations nonzero. Put k=1+a/2,
e=k-1=a/2, eta=1+3a/4, r0=e/(2k), and in R^(n+1) set

    q_i=r0^i, g_i=sqrt(q_i)e_i,
    X_0=eta sum_(i=0)^n sqrt(q_i)e_i,
    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
    f_i=(k-1/2)q_i.

Here all rho are zero, eta_i=eta, delta_i=e q_i, z_i=(eta-k)q_i>0.
For i<j, orthogonality gives

    Q_ij=(e-d_i)q_i-e q_j<0,
    Q_ji=k q_j-e q_i<=-(e/2)q_i<0.

Every optimum row also holds strictly. Formula(36), with all indices,
gives a static function with unique minimizer0, exact radius
eta^2 sum_(i=0)^n r0^i, and payoff
(2k-1)r0^n/[eta^2 sum_(i=0)^n r0^i]. These small coefficients demonstrate
nonemptiness only. Their density is not claimed to supply useful gain;
indeed the proved obstruction and the accepted orthogonal result exclude it.

## 10. Proof-state, literature, and the next genuine interface

The query-memory, obtain-immediate-conclusions, decomposition/direct-proving,
search-math-results and construct-counterexamples procedures were applied.
The search program was active-program repair of strong paid dense anchors.
Matlas searches returned short-step gradient-flow/self-contracted results,
proximal-gradient bounds and convex-hull projection estimates; their
assumptions do not supply the needed long-step Markov construction. No
returned theorem is a premise. Web search gave general long-step background.
The exact local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper arXiv1502.05666 was reread
at Section2.5, Theorem4, proof and conjugate construction. Its mu=0,L=1
specialization is already contained in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. No new
external theorem, black-box Markov fact or unverified C99 lemma is used.

The direct adjacent-saturation plan fails concretely by(11), and its
local-only relaxation is refuted by r=1/2 in(35). The repaired argument
retains the remote row and closes the WHOLE stated dense positive-reserve
class, including strong projections and slowly varying ratios. The
extension(18) explicitly rederives what changes for non-Markov data.

The next genuinely different interface must specify an actual rule which
either leaves range(2), changes the adjacent sign pattern, or supplies
linear E_mix through value variation or the signed distance-two defects.
A constant-ratio Markov law in this range is now excluded even if its
long t_i are large and its initial anchor is fully dense. A positive law,
an arbitrary Gram optimization, or meeting(32) alone is not a solution.
The full initial norm, true distance, all values, every remote row and
actual original-B payoff remain indispensable.

C100 and C99 have different, crossing scopes. C99 allowed arbitrary long-step
value ratios but capped the useful long projections. C100 permits strong
projections while requiring the positive value range(2) at every queried
point. Neither scope is silently substituted for the other. In particular
the low-reserve range1<=kval<1+a/2 remains open to strong-projection rules;
the present argument does not exclude it.

No fact_submit, accepted fact, numerical evaluation, solver, machine algebra,
replay, formal verifier, consult, installation or subagent was used. All
previous computational ledgers remain CLOSED. This final allocated round
completes31 only when it returns; runtime termination must be checked by
the main agent. No renewal, finalization, or resolution of the original
arbitrary-h matching Goal is inferred.

# An off-center unit step with two independently varying middle steps

Status: COMPLETE JOINT **UNVERIFIED** analytic draft, max2 round41.
Guidance d611bba957eb910a and6b4bef299b2b6563; plan440b961de58eac34.
This is the second and final allocated round after completed39.
No fact_submit was authorized or used. The original Goal remains OPEN.

The new family below has its unit step at index1, independent endpoints,
and two independent middle steps in a band of positive width. It is not
the previously considered reflected central-unit family. No reversal
closure, lossless reduction, multiplier symmetry, zero remainder, rank
restriction, or interpolation-edge restriction is used.

The only mathematical inputs from the fact graph are accepted
04219554e41d9750, Section10 (the original primitive identity), and
1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph1 (necessary interpolation inequalities).
Accepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e supplies the capacity motivation, not seed
existence. Every other argument used below is proved in this document;
no earlier unverified finding is a predecessor.

## 1. Original quantities and what a negative energy disproves

For any nonnegative length-n word h, normalize smoothness to1 and write

 s=sum_(i<n)h_i, A=1+s,
 x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_n||^2.

Every actual smooth convex trajectory satisfies Q_ij<=0, including all
remote, terminal, and optimum comparisons, by1252b8823bb5a290. We take
the optimum jet to be x_star=g_star=f_star=0.

For a full original multiplier array Lambda, let e be the all-ones
column, L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and

 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)),
 M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.

The hypotheses on a certificate are Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij>=0 for all
i!=j, and L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T. Accepted
04219554e41d9750 gives the exact identity

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
     =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.                         (1)

Here the right side is the matrix trace inner product. If M_prim is
positive semidefinite on the whole original R^(n+1), its pairing with
an actual gradient Gram matrix is nonnegative. Therefore an actual
trajectory with V_h<0 excludes EVERY certificate of this form.
For n=5, all30 ordered multipliers remain free and all six formal
gradients remain independent. No dual-attainment claim is needed.

## 2. Static projection functions and the complete normal check

For a compact convex set K containing0, define

 F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                    (2)

The unique maximizer is the Euclidean projection P_K(x), by completing
the square. It is characterized by

 <x-P_K(x),v-P_K(x)><=0 for every v in K.

Adding this inequality at two points yields
||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, so P_K is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing inequalities imply

 0<=F_K(x+z)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),z>
      <=<P_K(x+z)-P_K(x),z><=||z||^2.

Thus F_K is convex, differentiable, and1-smooth, with gradient P_K.
If0 is interior to K, it is the unique minimizer: F_K(0)=0, and for
x!=0 substituting a sufficiently small positive multiple of x in(2)
gives F_K(x)>0.

At a proposed query whose gradient is g_i=P_K(x_i), put

 y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

Direct substitution in the definition of Q gives

 Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                    (3)

Hence checking the entire normal cone at EACH original query proves
ALL ordered inequalities at once. Comparisons to the optimum are also
included since0 belongs to K; in particular Q_(i,star)=0.

The actual GD norm telescope is

 ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2
      =2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.

Using the actual terminal value in V_h therefore gives

 V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+s g_n||^2.                         (4)

This identity also applies to the scalar capped function below, since
its values have the same expression x*g-g^2/2 at every query.

## 3. The original global cuts come first

For any index j with h_j>1 define

 P=1+sum_(i<j)h_i, D=1+sum_(i>j)h_i, eta=(h_j-1)/D.

Take K=[-eta,1] and x_0=P. Every query through j has gradient1,
query j equals1, and the step h_j lands at -D*eta. Every remaining
gradient is -eta: the nonnegative later steps move monotonically on
the negative plateau, ending at x_n=-eta. These are actual projection
queries for the single static function(2), whose unique minimum is0.
The true initial distance is P>0. Formula(4) gives

 V_h=P^2-A^2(h_j-1)^2/D^2.

Consequently every original certificate must satisfy, for EVERY j,

 A(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+sum_(i<j)h_i)(1+sum_(i>j)h_i).         (5)

A failed cut already supplies an actual negative-energy witness. When
we analyze a word below, all cuts are first imposed; we need no further
test on a word already excluded by one of them.

For the first and last physical steps a,d of a length5 word, (5) gives

 a,d <= 2A/(A+1) <2,                                  (6)

provided A>1. If an endpoint is<=1, (6) is automatic; if it is>1,
its cut rearranges to (A+1)a<=2A or (A+1)d<=2A.

## 4. An adjacent-pair witness with every exterior query retained

Fix any two adjacent steps

 h_j=1+B, h_(j+1)=1+C, B,C>0,
 P=1+sum_(i<j)h_i, D=1+sum_(i>j+1)h_i,
 U=B+C+D+1=A-P.

All other physical steps are arbitrary and nonnegative. Define the
normalized defect and its unnormalized version by

 H=BC[1+B(P+A)/P^2]-D,
 Delta=P^2 H=BC[P^2+B(P+A)]-DP^2.                       (7)

We prove that D<=BC always excludes the word, and that H>0 excludes it
when D>BC. This is a necessary test, not a completeness assertion.

### 4.1 The capped scalar case D<=BC

Use the static function

 F(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1, and F(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1.

Its derivative min(x,1) is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz;
the function is convex, differentiable,1-smooth, with unique minimum0.
Start at x_0=P. The prefix has gradient1 and ends at query j equal
to1. The next two positions and gradients are

 x_(j+1)=-B, g_(j+1)=-B, x_(j+2)=BC.

All suffix queries are>=1, because their total movement is D-1 and
BC-(D-1)>=1. Their gradients are1 and x_n=BC-D+1. Every original
update and query is therefore a query of this same static function.
Its actual values satisfy f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, so(4) gives

 V_h=P^2-[P+(1+B)(1+C)]^2<0.                           (8)

Here s=P+B+C+D was used. In fact V_h<-3 because P>=1 and
(1+B)(1+C)>1. All original interpolation inequalities hold by the
actual smooth-convex function, including the optimum and terminal.

### 4.2 The planar triangle case D>BC

Define

 kappa=B^2 C+U(D-BC), t=B^2 C/kappa,
 u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))),
 v=-(B u+D w)/(1+C), K=conv{u,v,w}.                    (9)

We have kappa>B^2 C>0 and0<t<1. The independent vectors u,w and the
positive relation B u+(1+C)v+D w=0 show that K is a nondegenerate
triangle containing0 in its interior.

Start at x_0=P u. Prescribe gradient u at every original query through
j, gradient v at query j+1, and gradient w at every query from j+2
through the terminal n. The positions are

 x_i=(1+sum_(r=i)^(j-1)h_r)u for i<=j,
 x_(j+1)=-B u,
 x_i=(1+sum_(r=i)^(n-1)h_r)w for i>=j+2.               (10)

The sum over an empty range is zero. Formula(9) makes the middle update
x_(j+2)=D w, so every GD equality in(10) holds.

We check all normal cones, including every exterior query. The basic
inner products are

 ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,
 <u,v>=-(B+D t)/(1+C), <v,w>=-(B+D)t/(1+C).

Every prefix normal is a nonnegative multiple of u. Its comparisons
with w-u and v-u are respectively multiples of t-1<0 and
-(B+D t)/(1+C)-1<0.

At query j+1 the normal is y=-B u-v. Its comparisons are

 <w-v,y>=(-B^2 C+kappa*t)/(1+C)^2=0,
 <u-v,y>=-BC(1-t)/(1+C)<0.                             (11)

For an explicit algebra check, write
y=[-BC u+D w]/(1+C). The coefficient of t in the first numerator is
D(B+1+C+D)-BC(1+C+D), which equals
UD-BC(U-B)=B^2 C+U(D-BC)=kappa.
Subtracting -BC(1-t)/(1+C) from the second expression leaves exactly
(-B^2 C+kappa*t)/(1+C)^2, hence zero.

Every suffix normal is a nonnegative multiple of w. Its comparisons
with u-w and v-w are respectively zero and multiples of
-t[1+(B+D)/(1+C)]<0. This proves every cone in(10). In particular
the prescribed gradients are actual projections onto ONE static K;
(3) proves every original and optimum ordered interpolation row.

The true initial distance is P, and x_n=g_n=w. Equations(4),(9) give

 V_h=P^2-A^2 t=-U*Delta/kappa.                         (12)

Indeed
A^2 B^2 C-P^2 kappa
 =U{BC[P^2+B(P+A)]-DP^2}=U*Delta.
Thus H>0 gives an actual negative-energy witness. We will also use

 0<kappa=UD-BC(C+D+1)<UD,
 V_h<-Delta/D whenever Delta>0.                       (13)

Nothing in this construction identifies formal certificate gradients;
some ACTUAL gradients happen to coincide in the chosen counterexample.

## 5. Screening a new band of off-center-unit words

Now let the actual five-step word be

 h=(a,1,b,c,d), a,b,c,d>=0,
 |b-c|<=1/50, A=a+b+c+d+2>=49/5.                      (14)

This is a four-parameter family with a unit at index1. The endpoints
are independent, and the middle steps are independent within a band.
We impose all five original global cuts(5). Any failure was already
handled by Section3. Consequently(6) applies.

The two middle excesses differ by at most1/50, so each is at least

 (A-a-d-4)/2-1/100.                                   (15)

Since a,d<2 and A>=49/5, both middle excesses are>89/100. In particular
b,c>1, so the tests we use have strictly positive excesses.

### 5.1 Last step d<=8/5: use the actual pair (b,c)

For this pair take

 B=b-1, C=c-1, P=a+2, D=d+1.

We have 2<=P<4 and1<=D<=13/5. The sharper endpoint bound in(6) and
(15) imply

 B,C >= A/2-19/5+1/(A+1)-1/100.

The displayed function of A has derivative1/2-1/(A+1)^2>0 on the
entire region. At A=49/5 its value is

 161/135-1/100=3193/2700>7/6.                          (16)

Since (P+A)/P^2 decreases with P>0 and increases with A, it follows
that

 (P+A)/P^2 >= (4+49/5)/16=69/80.

Therefore the defect H from(7) satisfies

 H >= (49/36)[1+(7/6)(69/80)]-13/5
    =251/1920>1/8.                                   (17)

If D<=BC, Section4.1 already excludes the word. Otherwise Section4.2
provides the full static triangle, and (13),(17) give

 V_h<-P^2/(8D)<=-5/26.                                (18)

The prefix consists of the actual steps a,1 and the suffix consists
of the actual step d; every one of their queries is included in(10).

### 5.2 Last step d>=8/5: use the actual final pair (c,d)

For this pair take

 B=c-1, C=d-1, P=a+b+2, D=1.

We have 3/5<=C<1, B>0 and P>3. We will prove

 BC[1+B(P+A)/P^2]>1+1/125.                            (19)

First suppose 49/5<=A<=10. By(6),

 a,d<=20/11<11/6.

Equation(15) yields B>317/300. Also C<5/6 and

 B >= 221/150-C/2,
 BC >= C(221/150-C/2) >= 88/125.                       (20)

For the last inequality, the derivative of the middle expression is
221/150-C>0 for3/5<=C<=5/6; its value at C=3/5 is88/125.
The prefix satisfies

 P=(A+a-d+2)/2+(b-c)/2
    <367/60+1/100=919/150<25/4.

Thus

 (P+A)/P^2>1284/3125,
 B(P+A)/P^2>33919/78125.

Using(20), the left side of(19) is strictly greater than

 (88/125)(1+33919/78125)
     =9859872/9765625
     =1+94247/9765625>1+1/125.                         (21)

Now suppose A>=10. The inequalities a,d<2 and |b-c|<=1/50 give

 B>A/2-401/100,
 P<A/2+121/100,
 BC>=C(A/2-351/100-C/2)>=357/500.                      (22)

For the last bound, the derivative in C is
A/2-351/100-C>=49/100>0 for3/5<=C<=1. Its minimum is at C=3/5;
the resulting (3/5)(A/2-381/100) is>=357/500.

Put z=A/2+121/100>=621/100. Then

 B(P+A)/P^2 >= R(A),
 R(A)=(z-261/50)(3z-121/50)/z^2
     =3-452/(25z)+31581/(2500z^2).

Its derivative with respect to z is

 (45200z-63162)/(2500z^3)>0,

because z>=621/100>6. Consequently

 R(A)>=R(10)=160479/385641=17831/42849.

Together with(22), this gives

 BC[1+B(P+A)/P^2]
   >=(357/500)(1+17831/42849)
    =361046/357075=1+3971/357075>1+1/125.              (23)

This proves(19) throughout the second region. If BC>=1, Section4.1
supplies the capped scalar witness. If BC<1, Section4.2 gives a full
static triangle, and(13),(19) imply

 V_h<-P^2/125<-9/125.                                 (24)

Here the actual prefix is a,1,b, and the suffix is empty. The formula
checks all six original queries, including the unit step and terminal.

## 6. Whole-family conclusion and its exact scope

The preceding direct proof establishes the following candidate theorem.

For every a,b,c,d>=0 with |b-c|<=1/50 and
A=a+b+c+d+2>=49/5, the actual word h=(a,1,b,c,d) has an actual
finite-dimensional convex differentiable1-smooth counterexample with
unique minimizer0 and nonzero starting point for which V_h<0.
If any original global cut fails, Section3 gives a scalar example.
Otherwise Sections5.1--5.2 and4 give a scalar or planar example.
In the cut-surviving cases the chosen example even satisfies V_h<-1/15,
using(8),(18),(24). Thus the word has NO full original30-multiplier
certificate with ANY PSD remainder on all six original formal gradients.

This is a complete JOINT DRAFT, not a verifier-accepted theorem.

The threshold49/5 lies strictly below the accepted sufficient threshold:

 Gamma5_c107-49/5=78807/806000>0.

So no retuning within this entire new band can supply a seed for the
accepted length5 lift, even if the capacity requirement is weakened
from99/10 to just above Gamma5_c107. The result does not exclude
|b-c|>1/50, other unit positions, all length5 words, length6 words,
or the unrestricted original minimax problem. It asserts no reflection
theorem and gives no lossless reduction to this family.

## 7. Exact asymmetric word passing all global cuts strictly

Choose the new word

 h_test=(7/5,1,491/200,489/200,8/5), A=99/10.           (25)

Both endpoints and the two middle steps differ. Its middle difference
is1/100, strictly inside the band. The five original global-cut slacks,

 (227/50,18,5497/2000,367/400,59/25),                  (26)

all strictly positive. Its unit-curvature product is zero. We show

Use the actual pair at indices2,3, and set

 P=17/5, D=13/5, B=291/200, C=289/200, U=13/2,
 BC=84099/40000, D-BC=19901/40000,
 B^2 C=24472809/8000000,
 kappa=50344109/8000000, t=24472809/50344109.

The exact vectors and static function are

 u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))),
 v=-[(291/200)u+(13/5)w]/(489/200),
 K=conv{u,v,w}, F=F_K from(2).

All six actual queries and gradients are

 x=( (17/5)u, 2u, u, -(291/200)u, (13/5)w, w ),
 g=( u,u,u,v,w,w ).                                   (27)

The first two prefix normals are (12/5)u and u, the third is zero,
the fourth is -B u-v, the fifth is (8/5)w, and the sixth is zero.
Equations(9)--(11) check their complete normal cones. Define EVERY
actual value by f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Equation(3) then proves
all30 ordered finite inequalities and every optimum comparison.
The positive relation B u+(1+C)v+D w=0 puts0 inside K, so the unique
minimizer is0 and the true squared initial distance is289/25.

Its exact negative primitive energy is

 V_h=289/25-(9801/100)(24472809/50344109)
    =-36332042201/1006882180<0.                        (28)

For the integer arithmetic in(28), the unreduced numerator is

 1156*50344109-9801*24472809
   =58197790004-239858001009=-181660211005,

over denominator5034410900. Dividing both by5 yields(28).
This is an exact static-function witness, not a numerical Gram or a
feasible prospective seed. It is outside the earlier central-unit
families and must not be recycled as a seed by changing the remainder.

## 8. Literature, resources, and remaining interface

The query-memory procedure recalled round40 and relevant new shared

## 10. A missing partial-tail clipped-return test rejects this entire family

The previous sections prove survival of the PRE-EXISTING scalar system.
They do not imply a seed, and the following new actual function now
proves that every displayed word is INFEASIBLE in the full original cone.
The original global decision remains open.

Here is a new necessary inequality for every four-step full seed with
h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), all a,b,c,d>0:

                 C b d <= (2+a)(2+c).                 (9)

No ordering, fixed capacity or cut saturation is assumed. Define
alpha=b/(2+c)>0 and the scalar function

 f(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1,
 f(x)=x^2/2 for -alpha<=x<=1,
 f(x)=-alpha*x-alpha^2/2 for x<=-alpha.

Its derivative clamp(x,[-alpha,1]) is continuous, nondecreasing and
1-Lipschitz, so f is one globally convex differentiable1-smooth
function with unique minimizer0. Start at x0=P=2+a. The first four
queries and gradients are exactly

 x0=P,      g0=1,
 x1=1,      g1=1,
 x2=-b,     g2=-alpha,
 x3=-alpha, g3=-alpha.

Indeed x2=-(2+c)alpha and the next step has length1+c.
The last point is x4=d*alpha>0, with g4=min(d*alpha,1).
Every sample of this function satisfies f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2.
Telescoping the actual GD recursion therefore gives the exact primitive
energy V=x0^2-(x4+s*g4)^2, s=C-1.

If d*alpha<=1, then x4=g4 and V=P^2-C^2 d^2 alpha^2.
If d*alpha>1, then g4=1 and x4+s>C>P, giving V<0 directly.
Thus a full original nonnegative flow/PSD certificate necessarily
forces (9). Conversely, if (9) is violated, this exact function has
negative V in either case. Accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10,
then contradicts the full certificate identity, retaining every formal
coordinate and every original multiplier. This argument requires no
unproved prefix or tail heredity.

For h(t) from Section1, d*alpha<15/32<1. The difference between the
two sides of (9) is

 C b d-P(2+c)=3339/2000-(117/80)t>0
                                         for0<=t<=1/100.

Hence the entire family passing the old scalar tests, including C82
and C83 on0<=t<=10^(-12), fails the ORIGINAL full certificate cone.
At t=0 all data are rational:

 alpha=125/232,
 x=(12/5,1,-5/4,-125/232,375/928),
 g=(1,1,-125/232,-125/232,375/928),
 V=(12/5)^2-(2895/928)^2
   =-(3339*25611)/(25*928^2)<0.

Every original interpolation pair is legal because these are the
actual samples of the single explicit smooth convex function above.
This is a concrete missing constraint, not a violation inferred from
independently chosen jets or a numerical Gram.

The old system's global-coverage implication is still refuted by
Sections1--8. The enlarged system including (9) is a new question:
its whole-domain emptiness is NOT proved, and no surviving full seed
has been constructed. In particular this witness must not be turned
into an unsupported statement that every four-step seed is impossible.

# Actual binary-character pyramid: complete projection dynamics and a scoped full-word obstruction

Status: **one complete joint analytical candidate, UNVERIFIED**. Worker max2,
allocated invocation65 after ended baseline64, under TASK.md and shared guidance
90760fb68a7680b7. ONE policy is selected; its parameters and initialization are
not additional policies. No mathematical program, numerical evaluation, replay,
optimizer, recurrence execution, subordinate agent, or verifier call is used.
The original exact-profile target remains OPEN.

The policy is an actual fixed convex dictionary, not a prescribed character
gradient history. The proof gives its real projection, every physical query,
all original directed rows, a fixed maximal prefix completion retaining every
old jet, complete minima and exact closest distances for both explicit
functions. Its growing-rank screen is uniform in the rank and all widths.

Accepted inputs: fact_id1252b8823bb5a290 for the complete finite-prefix
interpolation/conjugate construction and ordered interpolation necessity;
fact_id5fd396eba75651e7 ONLY for its already accepted scalar SAME-word
Huber/original-benchmark comparison. No spiral-class exclusion is applied to
this new body. Fact_idc3bfc54321ddec65 supplies the accepted example of retaining
all old conjugate anchors, including first-query anchors on a revisited face;
none of its special-word performance formulas is used here. All new cone,
threshold, hierarchy and family-optimum arguments below are supplied jointly,
not imported from unverified global findings.

## 1. Exact policy, real character realization, and target normalization

For a finite nonnegative physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), let W_n(h) be the
original supremum of 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite real
dimensions, all convex differentiable L-smooth functions with an attained
minimum, every minimizer x_star and x_0 different from it, with the actual
updates x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R in the project's rate
notation. In this document a closest-distance normalized attained payoff is
denoted V; no distance is denoted by the rate symbol R.

Choose any integer q>=1, any positive reals b_1,...,b_q, any kappa>=1, and any
real vector z_0=(z_(0,1),...,z_(0,q)) satisfying |z_(0,j)|<=b_j. All choices may
depend on the complete known word and horizon, and are made before defining
the function or running GD. The ambient metric is the identity matrix on
R^(q+1). Coordinate0 is the carrier; the other q coordinates are transverse.

The finite group is G=(Z/2Z)^q. For s=(s_1,...,s_q) in G its real coordinate
characters are chi_j(s)=(-1)^(s_j). Give every group element the predetermined
weight 2^(-q). These weights describe the full orbit; the support below is a
maximum over the whole orbit, not an expected gradient or expected Gram.
Every real slope and offset is specified by

    v_s=(1,b_1 chi_1(s),...,b_q chi_q(s)),  offset_s=0,
    D=conv({0} union {v_s:s in G}),
    J(y)=max(0,max_(s in G)<v_s,y>)
        =max(0,y_0+sum_(j=1)^q b_j |y_j|),
    F(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2},
    x_0=(kappa,z_0).

All old support planes have height/offset zero. There is no unspecified
field-valued Gram: the displayed q+1 real coordinates are the realization.
The full dictionary has 2^q+1 listed points. Its real vertex Gram is

    <v_s,v_t>=1+sum_j b_j^2 chi_j(s)chi_j(t).

This is merely a geometric identity; interpolation is proved separately.
Its convex hull and generated cone have exact descriptions

    D={(t,w):0<=t<=1, |w_j|<=b_j t for all j},
    C={(t,w):t>=0, |w_j|<=b_j t for all j}.

For the hull description, at t>0 any w/t in the rectangular base is the
convex combination with coefficients
2^(-q) product_j[1+chi_j(s)w_j/(t b_j)]. These coefficients are nonnegative
and sum to1 by separate summation in each sign; their mean coordinates give
w/t. Add the origin coefficient1-t for D. The case t=0 gives only zero.
This proves the descriptions from the full real orbit, without an abstract
incidence or covariance assumption.

The cone is closed by its finite displayed inequalities, and its polar is

    C^polar={(c,z): c+sum_j b_j |z_j|<=0}.

Indeed maximizing a linear form on the base at carrier1 gives precisely
c+sum b_j|z_j|; positive scaling tests the cone. In particular every point
of C^polar has a nonpositive carrier coordinate. The chosen initial point
belongs to C, even when it lies far above the cap of D.

## 2. Exhaustive projection and original word-to-face map

Write an arbitrary real query as x=(c,z). For a proposed carrier t in[0,1],
the closest feasible transverse coordinate is

    w_j(t)=clip(z_j,[-b_j t,b_j t]).

After this coordinate minimization the squared distance to D is

    (t-c)^2+sum_j(|z_j|-b_j t)_+^2.

Define its half-derivative

    Psi_x(t)=t-c-sum_j b_j(|z_j|-b_j t)_+.

It is continuous and strictly increasing: increasing t by d>0 increases
the first term by d and cannot increase any subtracted summand. Hence the
unique projection carrier T_D(x) is given by the following complete rules:

* T_D=0 if c+sum b_j|z_j|<=0;
* T_D=1 if 1-c-sum b_j(|z_j|-b_j)_+<=0;
* otherwise T_D is the unique interior root of Psi_x(t)=0.

The interior root is an explicit finite piecewise formula, not a feasibility
oracle. Order the predetermined query ratios r_j=|z_j|/b_j decreasingly,
breaking equal ratios by the coordinate index. Set r_(0)=infinity and
r_(q+1)=0. For each k=0,...,q form the mathematical expression

    t_k=[c+sum_(j in first k ratios)b_j |z_j|]
                       /[1+sum_(j in first k ratios)b_j^2].

The root is t_k for an index with r_(k+1)<=t_k<=r_(k) and 0<t_k<1.
Existence follows by locating the unique root among these breakpoint cells.
At a tied breakpoint several indices may qualify and give the same number;
the strictly increasing Psi proves uniqueness of the number. This is an
exact formula for any finite rank; no enumeration, sorting, root solver or
trajectory was executed in this round.

The complete real projected vector and active face are

    P_D(x)=(T_D(x),w_1(T_D(x)),...,w_q(T_D(x))).

If T_D=0 the projected face is the origin. If T_D=1 the cap is active.
Each coordinate with |z_j|>b_j T_D has its sign-specified lateral constraint
active; coordinates at equality also lie on that constraint. Unsaturated
coordinates lie within the lateral interval. These equalities, and t=0 or
t=1 when applicable, specify the minimal face. All signs and tie cases are
included. This accounts for every vertex, every lateral plane and the cap,
including planes that become active much later in the run.

For C the identical formula uses t>=0 without the upper endpoint1. Denote
its unique nonnegative threshold by T_C and its projected vector by P_C.
When T_C<=1 it is also the D projection, since the minimizer over C then
belongs to its subset D.

For every physical index of any supplied word define, mathematically,

    g_i=P_D(x_i), r_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=<r_i,g_i>,
    f_i=F(x_i)=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i,       0<=i<n.

The same projection formula defines the actual old terminal g_n and f_n.
These are finite exact piecewise expressions fixed by h and the parameters,
not a computed transcript or freely selected gradients. Zero steps repeat
the identical jet and all original indices remain. The formula supplies
the full word-to-face map in every dimension, at every depth; the bounds
below control its full depth uniformly rather than extrapolating from a
finite sample.

## 3. Fixed global function, all directed rows, and old true distance

The strictly concave maximum defining F has a unique maximizer P_D(x).
Its normal condition is

    <x-P_D(x),v-P_D(x)><=0 for every v in D.

Adding two such conditions with the two projected vectors proves
<x-y,P_D(x)-P_D(y)> >= ||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2. Thus projection is 1-Lipschitz.
Evaluation at the two maximizing vectors bounds
F(y)-F(x)-<P_D(x),y-x> between0 and
<P_D(y)-P_D(x),y-x>. This proves differentiability with gradient P_D,
convexity, finiteness and global smoothness at most1. D has interior by its
description, where P_D(x)=x and F(x)=||x||^2/2, so its least smoothness is1.
Consequently the displayed recurrence is the actual GD on this one function.
Equivalently F is the unit Moreau envelope of the fully specified support J:
if r=x-g is a projection normal, g maximizes <r,v> on D, so g is a support
subgradient at r and minimizes J(r)+||x-r||^2/2. Its value is delta+||g||^2/2.

The exact residual of any ordered pair of actual samples is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.

The normal condition applies to every g_i in D, including remote samples,
the unqueried terminal and any zero gradient. This is an exhaustive proof
of both directions of every original interpolation row, not merely of their
adjacent or cyclic sums. Any full gradient/initial Gram is PSD because all
vectors have the displayed real Euclidean coordinates.

The complete old minimum set and value are

    min F=0,       argmin F=C^polar.

To see necessity, if <x,v_s> is positive for a vertex, a sufficiently small
positive multiple of v_s belongs to D and gives positive objective. If all
these inner products are nonpositive, every objective term is nonpositive
and zero attains the maximum. This proves the full set. Since x_0 belongs
to C, every minimum y has <x_0,y><=0. Therefore

    dist(x_0,argmin F)^2=||x_0||^2=kappa^2+Z,
    Z=sum_j z_(0,j)^2,

and zero is the unique closest minimum. No inverse-Gram norm or a selected
nonclosest minimum replaces this distance.

## 4. Complete fixed prefix completion, with every old anchor retained

Retain every physical old prefix jet at 0<=i<n and the optimum jet at zero.
For n>=1 define

    H_pre(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,max_(0<=i<n){<r_i,v>-delta_i}),
    F_pre(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H_pre(v)}.

Each individual jet lower quadratic from accepted1252 reduces exactly to
||v||^2/2+<r_i,v>-delta_i, since f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.
No first-query, returned-face, remote or zero-step anchor is dropped. All
slopes r_i, offsets delta_i and weights1 in the maximum are given by the
preceding explicit projection formula. Thus this is a function determined
from the full known word BEFORE the run. Mathematical recursion defining
those finite coefficients is not an executed oracle.

Fact_id1252b8823bb5a290 applies because all original prefix and optimum rows
were just proved. It gives one everywhere finite convex differentiable
1-smooth F_pre, its full prefix interpolation and its pointwise maximality
among legal functions interpolating these jets and the optimum. Induction
on the original physical indices proves that actual GD on the fixed F_pre
has every promised old prefix point, value and gradient. Its unqueried
terminal is the actual F_pre(x_n) with gradient determined by this conjugate,
not the old projection gradient unless separately shown. Both orientations
of all terminal and optimum rows hold by the accepted interpolation
necessity applied to this actual function.

Since delta_i>=0 by the old normal comparison with zero, H_pre(0)=0 and
F_pre>=0. Its complete minimum set is

    argmin F_pre=conv({0} union {r_i:0<=i<n, delta_i=0}).     (A)

Indeed F_pre(x)=0 precisely when zero maximizes its defining conjugate,
that is, x belongs to the subgradient of H_pre at zero. Exactly the displayed
zero-offset affine terms are active there. This proves the full set and
attainment, including all old zero-height anchors.

For n=0 use H_pre(v)=||v||^2/2 and F_pre=||x||^2/2. Its minimum is zero
and the empty word is kept. The diagnostic below has n>0.

## 5. SAME enclosing-m diagnostic and all-depth prefix products

Fix m>=1536 and tau=1/4. All subwords retain these SAME enclosing parameters:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), e=1/(4m), beta=1-e,
    c=lambda(1+e), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i.

Every physical step is retained. Its excess u_i=h_i-1 is a c^(nu_2(i+1));
nu_2 is the exponent of2 dividing a positive integer. Let

    A_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, pi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t), p_i=|pi_i|,
    pi_0=p_0=1,
    P=product_(i<n)u_i=beta^n c^(-m), Qunit=P^2.

No product or recurrence was executed. Here is the complete symbolic count.
For the fixed enclosing parameters the full product on H_j, denoted P_j,
obeys P_0=1, P_(j+1)=P_j^2 a c^j. Induction gives
P_j=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j), including j=0. In particular P_m=P.

Every nonempty prefix of H_j has product at most a, and every prefix
including the empty one has product at most1. Prove this simultaneously
by induction. H_1 has its nonempty product a. In H_(j+1), j>=1, prefixes
within the left child have the claimed bound. The product through its
middle bridge is

    P_j a c^j=beta^(2^j)/c<=a.

Multiplying this by any prefix product in the right child, bounded by1,
proves the assertion on all remaining prefixes. This accounts for every
inherited physical state, without resetting m or child parameters. Thus

    p_i<=a for every1<=i<=n,       u_i>=a>0.              (B)

For transparent constants, e<=1/64 and lambda<29/12 imply
2/5<a<1/2, 2<c<5/2. For the lower bound,
a>(12/29)(63/65)=756/1885>2/5; the upper follows from beta<1 and c>2.
The level1 bridge is k_bridge=1+beta<2; leaves are l=1+a<3/2.

Writing v=a/(c-2), the exact full physical mass is

    S=v c^m+(1-v)2^m-1,       4/5<v<1.                 (C)

The equality follows by summing the counts2^(m-j-1) at each levelj.
The lower bound on v follows from a>2/5 and c-2<1/2. For v<1 use
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta. Since c>12/5 and m>=5,
c^m>51, and (C) gives

    c^m/2<S<c^m,       S>5,       Qunit<=c^(-2m).       (D)

The comparison (12/5)^5>51 is the integer inequality248832>159375.

## 6. Two cone inequalities proved from normals

For x=g+r with g=P_C(x), the normal comparisons with zero and2g show

    r in C^polar, <r,g>=0,       F_C(x)=||g||^2/2,

where F_C is the same quadratic conjugate restricted to the unbounded cone.
The normal proof of projection nonexpansiveness applies also to C, and
P_C(r)=0. For a physical step h>1, put u=h-1. Then

    ||P_C(x-hg)||=||P_C(r-u g)-P_C(r)||<=u||g||.        (E)

There is a second, coordinate-specific inequality needed to pay the CAP,
which a norm bound alone does not establish in growing dimension. Write
g=(t,w), with |w_j|<=b_j t. At t'=u t the threshold function for x'=r-u g
satisfies

    Psi_(x')(u t)
      =2u t-r_0-sum_j b_j(|r_j-u w_j|-b_j u t)_+
      >=2u t-r_0-sum_j b_j|r_j|>=0.

The first inequality uses |w_j|<=b_j t, and the second is the full polar
condition r_0+sum b_j|r_j|<=0. Strict monotonicity of Psi gives

    T_C(x-hg)<=u t.                                    (F)

Both inequalities retain the entire normal vector r. No old transverse
state is cancelled or discarded. For 0<=h<=1, the normal pair instead
gives P_C(x-hg)=(1-h)g; the original diagnostic uses only h>1.

## 7. Arbitrary exterior initialization: every cap exit and later guard

Define the first carrier exit index, if it exists, by

    k=min{i in{1,...,n}: kappa-A_i<1}.

If it does not exist, k is left undefined. At every preceding physical
query i<k the actual carrier is c_i=kappa-A_i>=1 and the exact data are

    x_i=(c_i,pi_i z_0), g_i=(1,pi_i z_0),
    r_i=(c_i-1,0), delta_i=c_i-1,
    f_i=c_i-1/2+p_i^2 Z/2.                            (G)

This is proved by induction. By (B), |pi_i z_(0,j)|<=b_j. The projection
threshold at1 is Psi(1)=1-c_i<=0, and each transverse coordinate is
unchanged by projection. The actual GD subtraction gives the next carrier
and transverse product. All lateral and future orbit planes have already
been checked by the exhaustive projection formula.

If k exists, at x_k=(c_k,pi_k z_0) one has
1-h_(k-1)<=c_k<1. Since each transverse coordinate is bounded by b_j,
Psi_(x_k)(1)=1-c_k>0. Therefore T_C(x_k)<1, and its cone projection is
also its actual bounded-body projection. Its residual lies in C^polar,
and its height delta_k is zero.

Assume k<n and put u=u_k, d=max(1,1/u). In the NEXT ORIGINAL step, write
x_k=g_k+r_k with the cone normal pair. Then

    x_(k+1)=r_k-u g_k,      (x_(k+1))_0<=0,
    |(x_(k+1))_j|<=max(1,u)|(x_k)_j|
                         <=max(1,u)p_k |z_(0,j)|.       (H)

For the coordinate bound, cone projection clips the transverse coordinate
with its own sign and magnitude between0 and |(x_k)_j|. Subtracting h_k
times that coordinate gives the indicated maximum of1 and h_k-1.
The carrier sign follows from the nonpositive carrier of r_k and g_k>=0
in that coordinate. This pays the next leaf or bridge, whichever it is.

Let T=max(1,u)p_k=d p_(k+1). The transverse bounds in (H) imply
Psi_(x_(k+1))(T)=T-(x_(k+1))_0>=0. Hence its cone carrier is at mostT.
If u>=1, T=p_(k+1)<=a. If u<1, T=p_k<=a. Its cone projection is thus in D.
Also P_C(((x_(k+1))_0,0))=0 because this carrier is nonpositive; projection
nonexpansiveness gives

    ||g_(k+1)||<=||((x_(k+1))_j)_(j>=1)||
                    <=d p_(k+1) sqrt(Z).                 (I)

Apply (E) and (F) at every remaining ORIGINAL query. Induction yields,
for every i>=k+1 through the terminal,

    T_C(x_i)<=d p_i<=1,
    ||g_i||<=d p_i sqrt(Z),   delta_i=0,   f_i=||g_i||^2/2. (J)

Here d<=1/a by u>=a, and (B) proves d p_i<=1 in EVERY dimension. Thus
the cone projections remain in D and are actually the old bounded-body
projections. The cap cannot reactivate. This is the missing global guard;
no continuity, symmetry or presumed fixed-face argument substitutes for it.
Lateral faces and signs may continue to change according to the exact map.

This proof allows any kappa>=1, any predetermined rank, any widths and any
base-bounded initial transverse vector. In particular it includes a cap
lasting through a selected late bridge, and has no small-initial-carrier
or small-transverse-norm hypothesis. No rank-dependent loss is hidden.

## 8. Full new minimum set and true initial distance

In (A), every cap anchor with zero offset is zero, since (G) gives
r_i=(delta_i,0). Every zero-height cone anchor from k onward is its FULL
r_i in C^polar. Therefore the complete minimum set in (A) is a finite
convex hull contained in C^polar and contains zero. Since x_0 belongs to C,

    min F_pre=0,
    dist(x_0,argmin F_pre)^2=kappa^2+Z.                   (K)

Zero is uniquely closest by the same squared-distance expansion used for F.
No remote anchor is removed to obtain (K): every zero-height one participates
in the convex hull. Positive-height cap anchors remain in the fixed function
even though they are inactive at zero.

There is also a useful bound for ANY other static legal function Kfun that
interpolates the complete prefix and optimum zero. Its trajectory has the
same prefix and terminal position. Pointwise maximality gives
Kfun(x_n)<=F_pre(x_n), but its closest distance must be paid separately.
The initial gradient is g_0=(1,z_0). The initial old optimum row is equality,
so for every minimum y of Kfun the original interpolation row forces
<g_0,y><=0. Thus

    dist(x_0,argmin Kfun)>=(kappa+Z)/sqrt(1+Z)>=sqrt(1+Z). (L)

If k>=2, or if there is no exit, the original query1 is still capped and
g_1=(1,-a z_0). The identity

    x_0=[(1+a kappa)/(1+a)]g_0+[(kappa-1)/(1+a)]g_1

has nonnegative coefficients. Applying the optimum row at both original
queries gives <x_0,y><=0 for every minimum y. Since zero is a minimum,
the TRUE closest distance for Kfun is then exactly sqrt(kappa^2+Z).
For k=1 only (L) is asserted for arbitrary Kfun; the two explicitly defined
functions F and F_pre still have the exact distance (K). This distinction
prevents maximal terminal value from being silently combined with an
unpaid radius from another completion.

## 9. Actual unqueried terminal: all near-end exceptions paid

From the last ORIGINAL query, any realizing completion obeys the outgoing
terminal row of accepted1252. Completing its terminal-gradient square gives

    f_terminal<=delta_(n-1)+||x_n-r_(n-1)||^2/2
               =delta_(n-1)+(h_(n-1)-1)^2||g_(n-1)||^2/2.

The final physical step is the leaf l=1+a. This inequality bounds the
ACTUAL value of F_pre(x_n) while leaving its gradient unrestricted. It does
not identify it with the old cone gradient. The four exhaustive cases are:

### 9.1 Exit k<=n-2

The last query is in (J), with zero height and norm at most
d p_(n-1)sqrt(Z). Therefore

    2F_pre(x_n)<=d^2 P^2 Z<=a^(-2)Qunit Z,
    V(F_pre,h)<=a^(-2)Qunit.

The same true-distance bound holds for ANY legal prefix completion: use (L)
to divide by at least1+Z. This includes the k=1 case where its complete
minimum set need not coincide with (A). F is pointwise below F_pre and has
the explicit paid distance, so its own attained score has the same ceiling.

### 9.2 Exit k=n-1

The crossing step is the ACTUAL level1 bridge at index n-2, of size1+beta.
The entry carrier lies in[-beta,1), so its squared magnitude is at most1.
The cone projection at the last query has norm at most the query norm,
giving

    ||g_(n-1)||^2<=1+p_(n-1)^2 Z,
    2F_pre(x_n)<=a^2+Qunit Z.

All prior queries are capped and
kappa>=A_(n-2)+1=S-(1+beta)-(1+a)+1>S-5/2>S/2.
Every legal prefix completion has the exact radius here by Section8.
Consequently V<=4a^2/S^2+Qunit<=4/S^2+Qunit.

### 9.3 Exit k=n

Every prefix query is capped, and
kappa>=A_(n-1)+1=S-l+1>S-1/2>S/2.
All old conjugate anchors are (delta_i,0) with0<=delta_i<=kappa-1, including
the largest one from query0. Their exact maximum, WITH the optimum, is

    H_pre(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,(kappa-1)(v_0-1)).          (M)

For v_0<=1 the maximum is zero; for v_0>=1 the largest delta_i is active.
This proves the equality to the full old-prefix maximum, not a deletion
of an unproved remote constraint. At x_n its carrier w=kappa-S lies in
[1-l,1)=[-a,1), so its magnitude is at most1. Conjugating (M) in this region
gives the ACTUAL completed terminal gradient (w,pi_n z_0) and value

    2F_pre(x_n)=w^2+Qunit Z,
    V<=4/S^2+Qunit.

This pays a terminal that may lie on the other carrier ray. Setting the old
terminal to zero instead would not have been a valid completion argument.

### 9.4 No exit through the terminal

Again (M) is the exact completed conjugate. Now w=kappa-S>=1 and w<=kappa.
The actual terminal gradient is (1,pi_n z_0), and

    2F_pre(x_n)=2kappa-2S-1+Qunit Z,
    V=[2kappa-2S-1+Qunit Z]/[kappa^2+Z]
         <=max{1/(1+2S),Qunit}.                         (N)

For the scalar term expand
kappa^2/(1+2S)-(2kappa-2S-1)
 =(kappa-(1+2S))^2/(1+2S)>=0.
The full ratio is a convex combination of that scalar ratio and Qunit.
The same old terminal is obtained for F because its transverse coordinates
remain within their base intervals. All other legal prefix completions have
the same exact radius and a no larger terminal value.

## 10. Exact family optimum and ORIGINAL B_n comparison

Combining the four full-word cases gives, uniformly over all q, b_j,
kappa and z_0 in the declared policy, for both explicit functions and indeed
every static legal complete-prefix completion with optimum zero,

    V<=max{H_base,a^(-2)Qunit,4/S^2+Qunit},
    H_base=1/(1+2S).

By (D) and a>2/5, the second term is at most(25/4)c^(-2m), and the third
is less than17c^(-2m). Also S<c^m implies H_base>1/(3c^m). Since c^m>51,

    a^(-2)Qunit<H_base,       4/S^2+Qunit<H_base.

Thus V<=H_base for EVERY member of the full declared family. Attainment is
explicit: choose z_0=0 and kappa=1+2S, with any rank and any positive widths.
All queries and the terminal stay on the capped carrier axis and (N) is
equality. The old and completed functions have the exact radius kappa,
and both attain H_base. Therefore the supremum over this entire specified
static character-pyramid policy, including all its legal prefix completions,
is exactly

    sup V=1/(1+2S),      on every SAME-m tau1/4 H_m,m>=1536.

This is a JOINT UNVERIFIED family-optimum statement, not an accepted fact.

The original benchmark keeps ALL integer splits:

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    Gbench(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)Gbench(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

These are definitions, not an executed dynamic program. At the actual
n=2^m-1, accepted fact_id5fd396eba75651e7, proof Section10 equation(77), gives
precisely B_n H_base(H_(m,1/4))<237/250 for every m>=1536. Applying ONLY this
scalar denominator comparison to the jointly derived family optimum yields

    B_n sup V<237/250.

No asserted upper bound for unrestricted W_n is obtained. The failure of
this specified family on the actual dyadic words screens out its proposed
uniform lower channel; it does not resolve the original minimax question.

## 11. Initial geometric face changes really occur

The obstruction is not obtained by assuming all gradients are on one ray.
For a symbolic illustration inside the SAME policy take q=2, b_1=2,b_2=1,
z_0=(2,1/2), kappa=l=1+a. At query0 the actual cap gradient is(1,2,1/2).
After its actual first leaf, x_1=(0,-2a,-a/2). The threshold formula has only
coordinate1 saturated and gives

    T_C(x_1)=4a/5,
    g_1=(4a/5,-8a/5,-a/2).

After the ACTUAL level1 bridge k_bridge=1+beta, x_2 is
(-4a k_bridge/5, a(8k_bridge-10)/5, a(k_bridge-1)/2).
Both transverse coordinates are now positively saturated, with

    T_C(x_2)=a(29k_bridge-45)/60,
    g_2=(T_C(x_2),2T_C(x_2),T_C(x_2)).

For the chosen enclosing m, k_bridge>7/4. The threshold is positive;
coordinate1 saturation follows from k_bridge>15/19, and coordinate2 from
30(k_bridge-1)>29k_bridge-45. All thresholds are below1. These exact textual
formulas are a check of the projection cells, not an executed trajectory
or finite-sample payoff claim. The complete H_m trajectory and terminal
are governed by Sections2 and7--10. Noncollinear gradients, a sign change,
and a change in the active lateral set are compatible with the failure.

## 12. Scope, prior failures, literature and the remaining target bridge

The FULL old findings ae1fe666152d2dcf,27ff70f6035b8eb1,d6d76de419f81e83,
d13ea2e7d6a1794f were read. They constrain their prescribed product or
common-prefix character measures and positive gradient amplitudes, on their
specified words. They are UNVERIFIED and are not premises here. Their fixed
correlation patterns and local angular thresholds do not describe the actual
face map derived above.

The earlier projective-incidence regular-simplex radius/initial-cycle
failure da60e624fbed0a15, remote cancellation failure87225e1b68c94b70, and
carrier-tree pilot aa3c5e1d91baf405 were recalled at their relevant scopes.
The last covers its particular tree body, carrier and leaf start. Here the
base is a complete binary sign orbit, its widths and rank are arbitrary,
its initial carrier is arbitrary above the cap, and all maximal-completion
anchors are retained. Cap capture is proved by the coordinate threshold,
not by reweighting that old tree pilot or adopting its draft as a theorem.

Matlas search identified Moondra, Mortagy and Gupta, *Reusing Combinatorial
Structure: Faster Iterative Projections over Submodular Base Polytopes*,
arXiv:2106.11943v3, Theorem4. Its face-persistence test separates displacement
parallel to the face from displacement in its normal space; both must remain
in the corresponding cells. The theorem and proof were read as a method
comparison. Its projection algorithms and probability statements give no GD
terminal profile here. No external theorem is imported. The PDF was downloaded
to this worker's downloads directory; local pdftotext and three PDF-reader
modules were absent, so the exact PDF proof was read via web extraction.
An HTML text-extraction fallback was also requested, with its outcome logged.

The precise excluded scope is: Euclidean D with the full independent binary
sign base, zero support offsets, carrier cap1, kappa>=1 and initial
|z_(0,j)|<=b_j; the actual projection function and all static interpolants of
its complete old prefix and optimum zero, on SAME-m tau1/4 H_m. Rank, widths,
initial carrier, initial transverse norm and legal terminal gradients may
all vary with the full word. No bounded rank or matched child amplitudes are
assumed.

Initial transverse coordinates outside their base intervals are NOT covered.
Nor are general group orbits, dependent character sets, non-Euclidean metrics,
nonzero offsets, tilted caps or arbitrary finite-field incidence bodies.
A separate counterexample in this directory shows that deleting the initial
base condition actually permits cap reactivation on this SAME full H_m.
Thus cap capture is not a general finite-character impossibility theorem.

The original all-word/profile cover and quantitative useful witness remain
theorem-level interfaces. This full family optimum supplies a scoped dead
end and explicit initialization boundary, not a new lower channel and not a
resolution of those interfaces. There is no fact submission or original-Goal
completion in this allocation.

## 13. Local glossary and resource accounting

G,chi_j,v_s: the real binary group, its coordinate characters and full orbit
slopes. D,C,C^polar: capped pyramid, its full cone and full polar. J,F,F_pre,
H_pre: support, old projection objective, fixed maximal complete-prefix
objective and its conjugate. q,b_j,kappa,z_0,Z: rank, widths, initial carrier,
initial transverse vector and its squared norm. T_D,T_C,Psi_x,w_j,clip: exact
projection thresholds, their increasing scalar equation, clipped coordinates
and scalar interval clipping. x_i,g_i,r_i,delta_i,f_i: every actual old physical
jet and its full projection residual/height/value. Q_ij: the original ordered
residual defined in accepted1252. k: first carrier exit query. u_i,p_i,pi_i,A_i,
P,Qunit,d: physical excess, absolute/signed full-prefix products, full-prefix
mass, terminal absolute product, unit-quadratic payoff and capture factor.
lambda,e,beta,c,a,H_j,m,n,S,v,l,k_bridge: fixed enclosing hierarchy definitions
in Section5; the local c is distinct from a query carrier c_i. H_base: SAME-word
Huber payoff. C_n,B_n,Omega,Gbench,U_n^ZJ: original all-split benchmark
definitions in Section10. Kfun: an arbitrary static legal complete-prefix
interpolant with optimum zero. V: its attained W=2R closest-distance score.

New mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0; arithmetic replays0;
executed trajectories/recurrences0; optimizers/solvers/DP0; installations0;
consults0; subagents0; formal gates/fact_submit0. File I/O, text retrieval,
PDF/HTML download, memory publication and integrity readback only. One policy;
no fourth policy, further round, publication/finalization/revocation/shutdown
or original-target completion is inferred.
